Survey Corps issue · 16 September 2026

Wings of Freedom

Phase 1 playtest packet

The Walls have stood for a hundred years. In 845 one of them fell. You are soldiers of the Survey Corps, the branch that rides out beyond the Walls on purpose, and the years from 845 to 850 are yours to survive. Every Titan you meet is taller than a house and heals from any cut but one. You carry Blade Sets that break, gas that runs out, and a harness that jams when you need it most. What keeps you alive is the soldier beside you.

What Phase 1 covers. Making a soldier, the core dice, harm and fear, gear, and the Titan Engagement: a fight against the standard Small, Medium, and Large Titans and one Abnormal. The playtest rules add the minimum you need to ride out and come back: Expeditions, Downtime, Requisition, and Skirmishes against people.

What Phase 1 does not cover. Shifters, Chases, XP and advancement, and the campaign rules for Commendations, Factions, headquarters, and the Canon Clock. Where a rule would need one of these, this packet tells you what to use in the meantime.

What you need to play

  • Six-sided dice in four colours, a handful of each: one colour for base dice, one for Gear Dice, one for Stress Dice, and one for the Titan Dice the GM rolls for Titans.
  • Twenty cards numbered 1 to 20 for each round's deal, or a D20 (re-roll any number already dealt that round).
  • The printable field in a sheet protector, a dry-wipe pen, and a token for each soldier, horse, and Titan.
  • Tokens for Openings, for the marks a Titan remembers, and for the Heave count on a fallen Titan.
  • Pencils and copies of the sheets: a character sheet for each player, one Squad sheet, and one tracker for the GM.
  • A copy of the Quick Reference for each player.

Part One: The Soldier's Rules

Everyone at the table reads this part.

Core Rules

"Count your sixes. Then count your comrades. Both numbers keep you alive."Training Corps instructor, first morning drill

Every roll in Wings of Freedom works the same way. You build a dice pool, roll it, and count sixes. When that is not enough, you can Push, trading your nerve for a second chance. When a Titan or a Foe attacks you, your roll cancels its roll. This chapter gives you those rules.

Playing a Phase 1 session Playtest rule

What a session holds depends on where the Squad stands. The Playtest rules run Expeditions, Downtime, Requisition, and Skirmishes.

  • On an Expedition, the Squad rides Leg by Leg and makes a Night Camp after each day's Legs. A day passes at each Night Camp. Before the Expedition begins, and on arriving at a Depot, every soldier receives a full Standard Issue and Squad Supply is restocked (see Expeditions and the Night Camp).
  • In Downtime, from the gate that ends an Expedition until the next Expedition begins, seven days pass at the infirmary, and the Squad takes its Downtime Actions and its Squad Action (see Downtime).
  • While no Expedition and no Downtime is under way, such as before your Squad's first Expedition, start each session with the two steps below.
  1. The interim day. One day passes (see Healing time). Its care window includes every soldier in the Squad, and Field Repair can be rolled in it. Stress and Grief do not change on this day.
  2. The interim issue. Every soldier in the Squad receives the interim issue of Standard Issue together, and Squad Supply is restocked (see Standard Issue).

If a session begins partway through a procedure, such as a Titan Engagement or a care window, the interim day and the interim issue wait until that procedure has ended. They never happen while an Expedition or Downtime is under way.

Titan Engagements and Skirmishes begin when a rule or a table row names one, such as a Leg Hazard, or when the GM frames a scene that sets the Squad against Titans or people, including a Waypoint scene or the stakes of a failed called roll. How each one starts, runs, and ends is set by the rules (see Starting a Titan Engagement and Skirmishes).

When to roll, and what stands

Every die in a dice pool or on a table is a six-sided die. D6 means roll one die. 2D6 means roll two dice and add them; you only add dice for table results, never for a dice pool. D66 means roll two dice and read the first as tens and the second as ones, for a result from 11 to 66.

The GM rules before the dice, never after them.

  • Roll when it matters. You roll when you take an action the Action Catalog marks as rolled, when a rule or a table result calls for a roll, or when the GM calls one. The GM calls a roll, a called roll, when you attempt something no rule covers, its outcome is in doubt, and failure would cost something. If failure would change nothing, or you have the time, the tools, and the training to do it unpressed, it happens without a roll. If it cannot be done, the GM says so before anyone rolls. An act whose one outcome succeeds or fails for several of you at once, such as forcing a gate or searching one house, is one called roll, made by one soldier you choose, with the others Helping. A stake of Stress, damage, or a fall falls on the soldier who rolled; a lost item is one of the roller's, a lost unit is the Squad's, and lost time or a worse position falls on the situation; an act each of you makes for yourselves is each soldier's own roll. In a fight, the GM can call a roll in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, but only for an act that no entry marked as an action you could take in that fight could model, and that would change nothing the fight tracks: no Position, Engaged or Apart, Attention, Opening, card, clock, or Foe value, and no change an Action Catalog entry makes. The GM looks for a model entry first (see When the steps do not resolve an action). Such a roll spends your action and is made on your turn, never in place of a Reaction: a soldier facing an attack dodges or Blocks. Its Help is that fight's own Help, and you can try it again on a later turn.
  • Name the roll and the stakes first. Before you build the pool for a called roll, the GM names the Catalog entry closest to the act (or an attribute alone if none fits), its Circumstances, and its stakes: what failure costs, from a fixed menu (it does not happen; 1 Stress; a fall, low or high and never extreme; 1 to 3 damage; a lost item or unit of Squad Supply; lost time or a worse position), and, where it is not obvious, what success gives. When the stakes name damage or a fall, the GM says your current Health with them, so the table knows before the dice whether a failure can put you Down. What success gives is the act itself, information, a better position that the next roll reckons with, an object that is not a gear item, which counts only toward Circumstances, or Squad Supply found as Squad Supply caps it. It never gives a gear item, Stress relief, a Health box or healing, a Commendation, a Research Point, or a Faction Standing change: each comes only from its own rule or table. In a fight, a cost of time or position is a change in the situation outside what the fight tracks, never a Position, a card, a turn, a move, an action beyond the one the act spent, Attention, an Opening, or a clock; and success never gives a Position, which only a move changes, nor anything else the fight tracks, nor found Squad Supply, since a called search is made once the fight has ended (see Squad Supply). A fall staked on a called roll in a fight is a fall in full: it lands as Falls gives, and that change of Position is the fall rule's, not the ruling's. You may change your approach or back out before you roll. A roll a rule calls for takes only its Circumstances from the GM: its entry, needs, Help, and failure are its rule's.
  • Results stand. Once the dice are rolled, the GM never overrides, re-rolls, or ignores a die or a table result, and never changes the roll's entry, its Circumstances, or its stakes. A roll changes only through a Push or through a rule that names the change. Titan Dice, a Foe's Attack Dice and Guard, and every roll on a table are made in the open. The one hidden roll in a fight is a Titan's Next Behavior, rolled once.
  • Trying again. In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish you can attempt an action again on a later turn, a called roll included, spending that turn's action and nothing else. Outside one, a rule that says a roll is never tried again is final. Otherwise you try the same thing again only when something has changed: a new approach, a better tool, another soldier, or time spent, at a cost of time or position the GM names before the new roll. That cost falls when you try again, whatever the new roll's result, and it is not that roll's stakes. Each soldier gets one attempt at a given obstacle per changed situation.
  • Secret rolls. The only roll of yours the GM makes out of sight is a passive roll: a Spot or a Size Up for you against something you do not know is there, outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish. A Titan's Next Behavior is the Titan's own one hidden roll. A passive roll uses your pool as it stands, with the Circumstances the GM names to themselves, and no other Bonus Dice. It cannot be Helped, Covered, or Pushed, and a Stress Die showing 1 on it does nothing. You learn the result only through what you notice. A passive roll is not a called roll: it has no stakes, and failing it means only that you do not notice. If you say you are looking, you roll Spot in the open as a called roll.
  • Where the GM does not rule. The GM applies the rest of the rules as written, from the Push and the Bonus Dice cap to every table and every Titan's numbers (see What stands).

A called roll needs 1 success. It can be Pushed and Covered, and it can be Helped: outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish as the GM rules, and in a fight as that fight's own Help rule gives (see Help).

Circumstances

Every attribute roll has Circumstances: the one step of this ladder the GM names for the situation the roll is made in. If the GM names none, the roll is Standard.

Circumstances
CircumstancesEffect on the pool
Effortless+3 Bonus Dice
Routine+2 Bonus Dice
Easy+1 Bonus Die
Standardnothing
Harda 1-die penalty
Harsha 2-die penalty
Desperatea 3-die penalty
  • One step per roll. The GM weighs everything about the moment and names one step: rain, dark, and a snapped strap are one judgment, not three. A step named for a condition that lasts is the step of every roll that condition touches while it lasts. When a further condition touches a roll already under a lasting step, the GM names one step for that roll, weighing both: steps never add, and the lasting step applies again to every later roll the further condition does not touch. The GM names the step with the stakes, before Bonus Dice are declared, so you can answer a hard step with Help.
  • Plus steps are Bonus Dice. Effortless, Routine, and Easy are one Bonus Dice source and count toward the cap of 4 with Help, Openings, and every other source. A plus step the cap leaves out spends nothing.
  • Minus steps are penalties. Hard, Harsh, and Desperate apply after the Bonus Dice cap, remove base dice only, and add up with any penalty you carry. They never take your base dice below 1 and never remove Gear Dice or Stress Dice, so a 6 can always save you.
  • Needs never change. Circumstances change dice, never what a roll needs. A Nape strike against Nape Depth 4 at Hard still needs 4 successes, from one fewer die.
  • Too easy, or impossible. A task easier than Effortless is not rolled: it happens. A task the GM judges impossible is not rolled: it does not. Anything possible is at worst Desperate. Routine and Effortless mostly serve a soldier who carries penalties.
  • Fixed once named. A Talent, a Push, or a Cover never changes the step, and nothing changes it once the dice are rolled.
  • A Reaction's step is fixed before the attack. A Reaction takes the step in force when the attacker's card comes up: a Titan's card (see Dodging a Titan) or a Foe group's card (see Skirmishes). Until that card ends, no step is named, raised, or lowered for a Reaction against it, and a condition that arises during the card counts from the next card. A dodge whose successes cancel against a later card that round keeps the step it was rolled at.
  • Which rolls. Every attribute roll you or a Squadmate makes, a roll made for a group taking one step on its roller's pool. Never a roll that is not an attribute roll (a Gas Roll, the fall roll, the infirmary roll, any roll on a table), the Death Roll, the performance roll, the Graduation Exam's Trials, Titan Dice, or a Foe's Attack Dice and Guard.
  • In a Titan Engagement the roll is Standard unless the GM names a step for something no rule already prices, such as rain on the rooftops, smoke, full dark, or ice (see Circumstances in a fight).

Example: a called roll at Hard

Private Mila Brandt wants into a locked supply shed at night, and a Garrison sergeant holds the key. No rule covers talking him into it, his answer is in doubt, and a refusal would cost the Squad, so the GM calls a roll: Persuade, at Hard (he is half asleep and under orders). On a success he opens the shed. On a failure it stays shut and he sends a runner to the Squad's officer.

Mila has Empathy 3, no Talent that names Persuade, and Stress 1. Private Oskar Wendt stands beside her, so the GM rules him present and able, and he Helps, spending nothing. Pool: 3 + 1 for the Help, then −1 for Hard after the cap: 3 base dice and 1 Stress Die. The Help has cancelled the step.

She rolls 5, 2, 4 and a Stress Die of 3: no success. She Pushes, gaining 1 Stress and a new Stress Die, and comes up 6, 1, 3 with Stress Dice 2 and 5. One success: the sergeant unlocks the shed. Had the Push failed, the stakes would have fallen as named and nothing more.

Attribute rolls and your dice pool

A roll is an attribute roll when its rule names an attribute. Rolled actions, Reactions, Death Rolls, called rolls, and any attribute roll a rule calls for are attribute rolls. Only attribute rolls use a dice pool, count successes, take Circumstances, and can be Pushed.

Other rolls, such as a Gas Roll, a Stress Response roll, a Fear Roll, or a roll on a table, use exactly the dice their rule states. They add nothing to their dice, take no Circumstances, and can never be Pushed.

A dice pool holds three kinds of die. Use a different colour for each so you can tell them apart when you Push:

  • Base dice come from your attribute, your Talent, and Bonus Dice.
  • Gear Dice come from one piece of equipment.
  • Stress Dice come from your current Stress.

Building your dice pool

  1. Name the roll and its Circumstances. Every attribute roll is made for one Action Catalog entry. The entry names the attribute and the gear that can add dice. Here, before any Bonus Dice are declared, the GM names the roll's Circumstances, and for a called roll also its entry and its stakes. When a rule, or the GM for a called roll, calls for an attribute roll without naming an entry, roll that attribute alone: no Talent dice, and Gear Dice only from an item a rule names. A called roll on an attribute alone has no Gear Dice; a tool that helps counts toward its Circumstances.
  2. Attribute. Take base dice equal to the attribute's rating.
  3. Talent. Add base dice equal to the level of one Talent that names this entry, if the roll meets that Talent's condition for it. Horsemanship, for example, adds its dice only to a dodge made mounted with your horse. A roll never uses more than one Talent that adds dice. Talents that bend a rule instead of adding dice apply whenever their trigger happens, and they stack.
  4. Bonus Dice. Declare your Bonus Dice sources and add that many base dice, 4 at most. A plus step of Circumstances is one of these sources.
  5. Penalties. Remove the base dice any penalty names, a minus step of Circumstances included. Penalties apply after the Bonus Dice cap, never take your base dice below 1, and never remove Gear Dice or Stress Dice.
  6. Gear Dice. Pick one item the entry allows and add Gear Dice equal to its current rating. An item worn down to 0 adds nothing. If the entry allows no gear, you roll no Gear Dice.
  7. Stress Dice. Add Stress Dice equal to your current Stress. The Death Roll has no Stress Dice, and neither does the Training Year performance roll.

Counting successes

  • Every 6 is a success, on any kind of die in a dice pool.
  • Your roll is final once you stop Pushing, or cannot Push. Count every 6.
  • A roll needs 1 success unless its rule names another number, and every such number is a quantity in the fiction or a set value. A Nape strike, for example, needs the Titan's Nape Depth. A called roll always needs 1. The GM never raises or lowers what a roll needs, and Circumstances never change it.
  • A Reaction needs no number of its own. Each of its successes cancels one of the attack's successes (see Attack and Reaction).
  • A failed roll does only what its rule says a failure does, or, for a called roll, what the GM staked. If the rule says nothing, it has no effect.
  • Successes beyond what you need do only what the roll's rule lists. Some rules add successes up across several rolls instead, such as a Body Part's Toughness.
  • Each success is used once. A success that counts toward what you need cannot also pay for an extra effect. The one exception is a Reaction: its successes cancel against each attack from the same attacker in the same round, separately.

Pushing

When you Push, you re-roll every die that is not showing a 6, except a Gear Die showing 1, and you pay for it with Stress. You can Push a roll that failed or one that succeeded.

You cannot Push if any of these is true:

  • Any Stress Die showed a 1, on the roll or after an earlier Push of it.
  • The roll's rule forbids a Push, as the Death Roll does.
  • The roll is a passive roll, which you do not know was made.
  • The roll is not an attribute roll.
  • You have already Pushed it as many times as you may: once, unless a Talent allows more.
  • A state or result you hold forbids it.

The Push

  1. Roll the pool. If any Stress Die shows a 1, you suffer one Stress Response and cannot Push. Go to Finishing a roll.
  2. Decide. You may Push. Before any die is re-rolled, one comrade who is able may offer to Cover it.
  3. Gain Stress. Gain 1 Stress and add 1 new Stress Die to the pool. If the Push is Covered, the Covering soldier gains the Stress instead, and no new Stress Die is added.
  4. Re-roll every base die and every Stress Die not showing a 6, with any new Stress Die, and every Gear Die showing 2, 3, 4, or 5. Dice showing 6 stay. A Gear Die showing 1 is locked: it stays showing 1 and is never re-rolled.
  5. Check. If any Stress Die now shows a 1, you suffer one Stress Response.

One Stress Response per roll. However many Stress Dice show 1, and however many Pushes a Talent allows, a roll causes at most one Stress Response.

What a 1 means.

  • Base die: nothing.
  • Gear Die: if the roll was Pushed and any Gear Die shows 1 when the roll is final, the item that supplied it wears by 1. That is one point per Pushed roll, whether one Gear Die shows a 1 or all three. A roll that was not Pushed wears nothing, whatever its Gear Dice show. What wear does is in Gear.
  • Stress Die: a Stress Response.

Pushing and gas. If you Push a roll whose gear item is your ODM Gear, a dodge included, your Gas Roll at the end of that round is three dice instead of two. It is three dice however many such rolls you Pushed, unless your Gas Roll for the round has already been made. A Pushed dodge made with your horse wears the horse and does not make the Gas Roll three dice.

Finishing a roll

  1. Count successes.
  2. If the roll caused a Stress Response, resolve it on the Stress Response table, using your Stress after any Push, unless the roll's own rule names another way, as the Graduation Exam does. If the result changes the roll, apply that now. The changed count is the roll's count for every later use, including a Reaction cancelling against later attacks.
  3. Apply the roll's effect, or, for a failed called roll, the cost that was staked.
  4. If the roll was Pushed, apply wear from Gear Dice showing 1.

Covering

When a comrade Pushes, you can Cover the Push and take the Stress yourself. You can Cover if all of these are true:

  • You are not the soldier Pushing, and you are not Down.
  • You are a player character. Squadmates never Cover.
  • In a Titan Engagement, you are in the Pushing soldier's zone or an adjacent zone.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement, you could Help that roll: under the rule that called for it; in a Skirmish, under the Skirmish's Help rule (see Skirmishes); or, on a called roll outside a Skirmish, as the GM rules. If a rule that calls for a roll allows no Help, no one can Cover the roll.
  • No state or result you hold forbids it.

Offer to Cover after the Push is declared and before any die is re-rolled. Only one soldier Covers a Push. The Pushing soldier may refuse, and chooses if several comrades offer. A Push that would re-roll no die cannot be Covered.

The Covering soldier gains 1 Stress. Covering is not an action and spends nothing. The Pushing soldier gains no Stress and adds no Stress Die, but everything else about the Push is unchanged: their Stress Dice are still re-rolled, a 1 on one still gives them a Stress Response, and their Gear Dice still wear their gear. The Covering soldier rolls nothing, so Covering never gives them a Stress Response.

Stress

Stress is your mounting fear and adrenaline. Attribute rolls get Stress Dice equal to your current Stress. A Stress Die showing 6 is a success like any other, and a Stress Die showing 1 sets off a Stress Response. As Stress rises, you grow more capable and more likely to break.

  • Stress has no maximum. It carries over between Titan Engagements, Expeditions, and sessions.
  • Minimum Stress equals your number of Scars, so it is 0 with none. Stress never drops below it. If a new Scar raises your minimum above your Stress, your Stress rises to it at once.
  • Stress changes only through the triggers below. The GM never raises or lowers it without one: a ruling changes Stress only as the stakes of a called roll.
What changes Stress
TriggerWhoChange
You Push and are not Coveredthe Pushing soldier+1, and 1 new Stress Die on that roll
You Cover a comrade's Pushthe Covering soldier+1
You fail a called roll whose stakes were Stressthe soldier who rolled+1, once for that roll however often it was Pushed; never on a success
A table result, a Talent, or a playtest rule names a gain, such as hunger on an Expedition, or a Fear Roll result that gives Stress to comrades in the soldier's zone or an adjacent zone (Stress only, never a Fear Roll)as it statesas it states
A Titan Engagement ends, for any reason, including a retreatevery soldier who held a Position at any point during it−1
A Nape strike kills a Titanevery soldier who holds a Position in that Titan Engagement when the kill happens−1
A table result or Talent names a reductionas it statesas it states
Camp Relief: at a Night Camp, the camp roll succeeds and the camp does not meet hunger Playtest ruleevery soldier on the Expedition−1
A Skirmish ends, for any reason Playtest ruleevery soldier who took part in it−1
In Downtime, you take Recover or Visit Haven, or a Squadmate takes the Squadmate relief Playtest rulethat soldier−2
The Graduation Exam endsevery Cadet who took itStress returns to 0

"Every soldier" in this table includes Squadmates. A Nape kill that also ends the Titan Engagement sets off both reductions in full. Rally clears a Stress Response but never lowers Stress.

A Stress Response comes only from a Stress Die showing 1 (see Stress Responses). A Fear Roll never comes from the dice, only from a horrifying event on the closed list of triggers. One trigger is the GM's: outside a Titan Engagement, the GM may call a Fear Roll for an event at least as horrifying as a listed one. Inside a Titan Engagement only the listed triggers call one (see Fear Rolls).

Bonus Dice

Bonus Dice are extra base dice. Only the sources in this table add them.

Bonus Dice sources
SourceDiceApplies toConditionSpends
Help1 per helper, up to 3 helpersAny attribute roll except the Death Roll, the performance roll, or a roll whose own rule forbids HelpSee HelpIn a Titan Engagement, the helper's action; outside one, what the calling rule states, or, on a called roll, nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when the Help is declared
CircumstancesEasy 1, Routine 2, Effortless 3Any attribute roll except the Death Roll, the performance roll, and the Graduation Exam's TrialsThe GM named the plus step with the stakes (see Circumstances)Nothing
Opening1 per Opening spentNape strikeThe struck Titan has an Opening that you did not createThe Opening
Titan grounded by a Broken leg2Nape strikeThe struck Titan has a Broken legNothing

Call It2DodgeA comrade's Read Called the behavior you dodge; the reader's own dodge gains nothingNothing
Medical supplies1 (1 unit at most per roll)A Treat Injury roll in a care window; never in a Titan Engagement, never an aftermath rollThe Squad Supply holds at least 1 medical unit1 medical unit of Squad Supply
Momentum1 per point spentBite: a strike or a Break Attention you take this turn against the Focus Titan your Flight named. Brace: your next dodge this roundYou spend Momentum on Bite or on Brace in a Titan Engagement1 Momentum per die
Terrain Trait1Break AttentionYou are mounted, in an Open zone (see Terrain Traits)Nothing
Ambush Playtest rule2A soldier's Fight or Shoot roll in a SkirmishYour Squad has the Ambush in that Skirmish, and the Foe you attack has not yet acted in itNothing
  • Declare before rolling. No source can be added after the dice are rolled, not even before a Push.
  • Cap. A roll gets at most 4 Bonus Dice from all sources together.
  • Choosing under the cap. If more are available, you pick which to use. Unused sources are not spent: Openings stay on the Titan, a comrade whose Help you do not use keeps their action, and a plus step of Circumstances spends nothing.
  • Bonus Dice are base dice. They succeed on a 6, a Push re-rolls them unless they show a 6, and a 1 on one does nothing.

Help

When you Help, you add 1 die to a comrade's roll. A comrade is any other soldier in the Squad, player character or Squadmate. Up to 3 comrades can Help one roll. You can Help if all of these are true:

  • You are not the soldier rolling, and you are not Down.
  • You declare the Help before the roll.
  • The roll is not a Death Roll, a performance roll, or a passive roll, and its own rule does not forbid Help.
  • In a Titan Engagement: your action this round is unspent, and you are in the roller's zone or an adjacent zone.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, on a roll a rule calls for: that rule allows Help and says who qualifies and what it spends. If it says nothing, the roll cannot be Helped or Covered.
  • In a Skirmish, on a roll a rule calls for: that rule allows Help, and you are taking part and not Down, whether Engaged or Apart, spending your action. If the rule says nothing about Help, or says the roll is never Helped, it cannot be Helped or Covered.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, on a called roll: the GM rules you present and able to help concretely. Help spends nothing unless the GM names, before the dice are rolled, a cost of time or position for it. That cost falls when you declare the Help, whatever the roll's result; it is not the roll's stakes, and it does not count against the one cost of a failed roll. A comrade who could Help may Cover.
  • In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, on a called roll: Help is that fight's own Help, and the GM names no cost for it. In a Titan Engagement, your action is unspent and you are in the roller's zone or an adjacent zone, spending that action. In a Skirmish, up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action, whether Engaged or Apart.
  • No state or result you hold forbids it.

Each helper adds 1 Bonus Die, which counts toward the cap of 4. The helper does not roll, cannot Push the roll, gains no Stress from it, and suffers no Stress Response from it.

In a Titan Engagement, Help spends your action. You can Help on a comrade's turn or during their Reaction as long as your action is unspent. If your own card comes later that round, your turn has only its move.

Turns, actions, and Reactions

A Titan Engagement runs in rounds. The order of play within a round is in Fighting Titans.

  • Your turn is one move and one action. A move takes you one step, into an adjacent zone or to another attachment in your zone, or farther when you ride or spend Momentum on Carry. In a Titan Engagement every ODM move is a Flight and is rolled. An action is Help, one Action Catalog entry marked as an action, taken as that entry is written, an improvised act modelled on one such entry, or a called roll for an act no such entry could model that changes nothing the fight tracks (see Actions outside the Catalog). While you are Overloaded, every ODM move spends your action as well (see Carrying).
  • You can spend each once per round, and you cannot save them for a later round. Both refresh at the start of each round, unless they were spent in advance.
  • Declining. You may decline your move, your action, or both on your turn. Whatever you leave unspent stays available until the round ends, but only for Help (your action) and Reactions (which need both). A move held alone does nothing after your card has passed.
  • Spending a turn. When a rule spends a turn of yours, such as a Reaction or a Fear Roll result, it spends your earliest turn, starting with this round's, whose move and action are both unspent.
  • Spent in advance. A turn spent in advance still happens and still counts as a turn, but it begins its round with its move and action spent. You cannot Help that round or make a Reaction with that turn. If a result spends only your next action, that turn keeps its move.
  • States that forbid. A state or result forbids you to Push, Help, Cover, or make a Reaction only if it names that. Down forbids all four. Grabbed forbids Help, Covering, and Reactions.
  • Not actions: Reactions, Pushing, Covering, declaring Bonus Dice, and spending Openings on the roll they join.
  • When a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish ends, every turn or action spent in advance that has not come up yet is cancelled. You owe nothing.

Reactions

A Reaction is a dodge or a block you make when a Titan or a Foe attacks you, outside your own turn. Against a Titan, that moment is when one of its cards resolves a behavior against you and its dice have been rolled.

  • The roll. A Reaction is an attribute roll with a full pool, made for its own Action Catalog entry. It can be Helped and Pushed. A dodge is Agility, with Gear Dice from your ODM Gear, or from your horse while you are mounted. A block is Strength, with Gear Dice from a Blade Set if you have one, and otherwise with no Gear Dice.
  • One per attacker per round. You make at most one Reaction against each Titan, and against each Foe, each round.
  • It spends a whole turn: your earliest turn, starting with this round's, whose move and action are both unspent. If any part of this round's turn is spent, the Reaction spends your next turn in full, and what is left of this round's turn can still be used on your card if it has not come up. If that next turn is also partly spent, it spends the one after. A Titan's Grab that lands on you can give a turn back (see The Grab).

Attack and Reaction

Every attack a Reaction can answer, from a Titan or from a person, is resolved by these steps.

  1. The attack roll. The attacking side rolls and finishes its roll first. A soldier attacking a person may Push; a Titan and a Foe never Push. The number of successes the attack scores is its Severity, announced to every target. From then on the attack roll never changes.
  2. The Reaction. Each target decides whether to make a Reaction, knowing the Severity, and declares Help and Bonus Dice before rolling as usual, at the Circumstances in force when the attacker's card came up. The reacting side Pushes last: your Reaction changes only through your own Push and through step 2 of Finishing a roll. If you have already made a Reaction against that attacker this round, you roll nothing new, and that Reaction's successes cancel against this attack.
  3. Cancel. Each success of your finished Reaction cancels one of the attack's successes.
  4. Land or whiff. The attack's successes left over are its Net Successes against you. On 1 or more, the attack lands on you, and its effect reads the Net Successes wherever its rule names them. On 0, it whiffs against you and does nothing to you.
  5. No Reaction. If you make no Reaction, or a state or result forbids you one, you cancel nothing, and every success of the attack is net against you. An attack that scores 0 successes whiffs against every target, including one who cannot make a Reaction. No Reaction is made against it, so you keep yours for a later attack.

Every target cancels against the same attack roll with their own Reaction, so one attack can land on one target and whiff against another.

Against a Titan, your only Reaction is a dodge, because Titans cannot be blocked. A Titan never builds a dice pool. Each harming Behavior Table entry prints its Attack Dice, the number of dice it rolls. The GM rolls them as Titan Dice, in the open: each die showing 5 or 6 is a success. Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, Stress Dice, or Circumstances. Roll them in a colour no soldier's pool uses.

  • Choosing to dodge. When a Titan's card resolves a behavior against you and its roll scores at least 1 success, choose whether to dodge. If you don't, every success is net against you, and you can still dodge a later card from that Titan this round.
  • One dodge covers the round. Once rolled, your dodge's successes, counted after any Stress Response result, cancel against the card it answered and, separately, against every later card that Titan resolves against you this round. Each card lands or whiffs on its own Net Successes. Cancelling against a later card does not use the successes up.
  • A landed card. A Critical Injury from a landed card adds 1 to its roll for each Net Success beyond the first (see Critical Injuries). A Grab, a knock loose, and a Stress gain add nothing for Net Successes. The full card procedure is in Dodging a Titan.

Against a person, in a Skirmish, your Reaction is a dodge or a block, as the attack allows. You can block only an attack whose rules allow it: a Foe's Fight attack. A Foe rolls its Attack Dice as base dice, succeeding only on 6, with no Circumstances. One Reaction covers every attack that Foe makes against you that round, cancelling against each separately. A landed attack deals its weapon's damage plus 1 for each Net Success beyond the first. The rest is in Skirmishes.

Example: a Pushed dodge

A Medium Titan's card resolves Swat against Private Hanne Roth. The GM rolls its 6 Attack Dice as Titan Dice in the open: 5, 2, 6, 1, 3, 4. The 5 and the 6 are successes, so the Severity is 2. Hanne dodges. Her card has not come up this round, so the dodge spends this round's turn. Hanne has Agility 3, ODM Gear rated 3, and Stress 1. Private Dieter Lang holds her Position with his action unspent, so he Helps.

Pool: 3 + 1 = 4 base dice, 3 Gear Dice, 1 Stress Die. She rolls base 6, 5, 2, 1; Gear 1, 4, 2; Stress 3. One success cancels one of Swat's two, so Swat would land with 1 Net Success. No Stress Die shows 1, so she Pushes. Dieter offers to Cover, and she accepts: he gains 1 Stress, and Hanne adds no Stress Die.

Re-roll: the three base dice not showing 6 come up 6, 3, 2, her two Gear Dice showing 4 and 2 come up 5 and 3, and the Stress Die comes up 4. Her Gear Die showing 1 was locked and stays. Two successes cancel both of Swat's: 0 Net Successes, so Swat whiffs against her. The roll was Pushed and a Gear Die shows 1, so her ODM Gear wears by 1 point, from 3 to 2, and her Gas Roll this round is three dice. Dieter spent his action Helping, so when his card comes up he has only his move.

Later that round, the same Titan's next card targets Hanne and scores 3 successes. She rolls nothing new: her two successes cancel two, and the card lands with 1 Net Success.

Making a Soldier

"Three years to make a soldier. One morning outside the Walls to find out whether we did."Survey Corps enlistment officer, Trost District

Every player character is a graduate of the Training Corps who has chosen the Survey Corps. There are three ways to make one:

  • The Lifepath rolls where your soldier grew up, why they enlisted, and three years of training, ending at Graduation. It is always allowed.
  • The Template Build takes a Specialty's template ratings and lets you choose the rest.
  • The Free Build lets you place a fixed set of ratings and choose the rest.

Every result comes from a roll, a table, or a choice the rules give you. The GM decides which methods the campaign allows and otherwise only reads tables with you.

Create your soldiers together, step by step. The optional Graduation Exam and the starting Squad involve everyone at once.

Shared choices. Some choices belong to all of you: whether to run the Exam, the Campaign Year, how many starting Squadmates to take when fewer than the rule gives are proposed, and the templates of starting Squadmates. When players disagree, one player who proposed each option rolls a D6 for it. The highest roll decides, and options tied for highest roll again. This is the roll-off, and every shared choice in this packet uses it. The GM's choice of creation methods is not a shared choice, and the order of Cadets in an Exam Trial is rolled, not chosen.

Creating your soldier

At session zero

  1. Creation methods. Before the campaign's first soldier is created, the GM records once which methods the campaign allows: the Lifepath alone, or the Lifepath with the Template Build, the Free Build, or both. The choice never changes. It covers every soldier created in the campaign, including a new character for a player whose soldier dies or retires.
  2. Campaign Year. If the campaign has none yet, record the year it begins, from 845 to 850.
  3. The Exam vote. Decide whether to run the Graduation Exam. Only Lifepath soldiers take it.

For each soldier

Pick any method the campaign allows. Players at one table can mix methods.

With the Lifepath:

  1. Start. Set every attribute to 2.
  2. Origin. Roll D66. Add 1 to each of the row's two attributes, take level 1 in one of its two Talents, record one of its two Havens, and record its Canon Tie if you want it.
  3. Why You Enlisted. Roll D66. Add 1 to the row's attribute and record a Drive.
  4. First and Second Year. For each, roll D66 on the year's events, then make the year's performance roll.
  5. Third Year. Roll D66 on the year's events. Then make the performance roll, or, if you voted to run it, take the Graduation Exam once every Cadet has rolled their Third Year event.
  6. Graduation. Find your Class Rank, choose your Specialty, apply the swap and floor, apply Top 10, and take your Specialty Talent level.

With the Template Build or the Free Build (full steps in Building without the Lifepath):

  1. Specialty. Choose one of the nine Specialties.
  2. Attributes. Template Build: record the Specialty's template ratings. Free Build: place one of the two arrays, with a 4 on the key attribute.
  3. Origin. Choose a row. Take its Haven, its Canon Tie if you want it, and level 1 in one of its Talents. Its attributes gain nothing.
  4. Drive. Record the Drive of any Why You Enlisted row.
  5. Training Years. You may name one event of each year for your story. It gives nothing.
  6. Talents. Take 1 level in a Talent on your Specialty's list, then 3 levels in any Talents.
  7. Merit and Class Rank. Record none for both.

Every method finishes the same way:

  1. Finish. Record Health as a row of boxes, Resolve, Stress 0, and minimum Stress 0. Record Rank Private, Health lost 0, not Down, no Scars, Grief 0, and no Critical Injuries. Receive Standard Issue. Name your soldier.

Once every soldier is finished

  1. Starting Squadmates. Create them as Squadmates describes.
  2. Named comrade. Each player whose Drive needs a named comrade names one now: a living player character or Squadmate in the Squad, other than their own soldier.

Attributes

AttributeCoversAction Catalog entries that name itKey attribute of
StrengthForce, cutting power, and physical endurance.Nape strike, Body Part strike, Break Free, Heave, Fight, Block, Endure, Death RollSlayer, Brawler
AgilityQuickness, balance, and control on ODM Gear or in the saddle.Dodge, Fly, Leap Clear, Ride, Shoot, SneakFlier, Rider

EmpathySteadying comrades and moving people to act.Rally, PersuadeLeader

The Covers column is a summary. Which attribute a roll uses is set only by its Action Catalog entry.

  • Attributes are rated 2 to 5. Only your Specialty's key attribute can be rated 6.
  • Attributes never rise after creation, whichever method made your soldier. You grow through Talents and Scars instead.

Attributes on the Lifepath

  • Every attribute starts at 2, and the Lifepath adds 6 points: 2 from your Origin, 1 from Why You Enlisted, and 1 from each Training Year's event.
  • Before Graduation, no attribute can go above 5. A point that would raise one above 5 goes to another attribute rated below 5, of your choice.
  • There are no free points. The Graduation swap and floor never change the total, so every Lifepath soldier has 18 attribute points, or 19 with a Top 10 Class Rank.

Attributes on a build

A Template Build or Free Build soldier has 18 points, every attribute rated 2 to 4, the key attribute at exactly 4, and at most two attributes at 4. There are no rolled points, no swap, no floor, and no Top 10 bonus. See Building without the Lifepath.

Derived values

  • Health is 2 plus half of Strength plus Agility, rounded up. Halve Strength plus Agility, round up, then add 2. Keep it as a row of that many boxes. It runs 4 to 6 on a built soldier and as high as 8 on the Lifepath.
  • Resolve is half of Instinct plus Empathy, rounded up, plus 1 per Scar, minus 1 per point of Grief (at most 3 Grief counts).
  • Stress starts at 0, the minimum Stress of a soldier with no Scars.
  • Carrying limit: Strength + 4 items before you are Overloaded.

Work out Health and Resolve from your final attributes: after the Graduation swap and floor, or from the ratings a built soldier records.

Origin

Roll D66. If the row names a Campaign Year the campaign has not reached, roll again. Add 1 to each of the row's two attributes, take level 1 in one of its two Talents, and record one of its Havens.

A Haven is what your soldier has to return to. In Downtime, the Visit Haven Downtime Action sends you there (see Downtime). A Canon Tie is an optional personal link to one canon character; you can have at most one, and only from your Origin. A Canon Tie has no mechanical effect in Phase 1.

Drives

Your Drive is what keeps you fighting. When a Fear Roll result is about to take effect on you, check your Drive's trigger. If it is met and you have not used your Drive this session, you may shrug off the result: none of its effects happen. A Drive shrugs off one Fear Roll result per session. Mark it when used, and clear the mark at the start of each session.

Read each trigger this way:

  • "When I hold" or "When I am airborne" triggers are met if that is true as the result is about to take effect. A Position counts only if your own move put you there: your most recent change of Position was a move you made. A starting Position, a fall, a Grab (even one that leaves you On Body), or any other rule does not count until your own move changes it. Flight counts only on your own ODM Gear; being carried never counts.
  • "On my most recent turn" triggers are met if you took the named act on your most recent turn in this Titan Engagement or Skirmish, earlier this round or in an earlier round.
  • "Since the start of my most recent turn" triggers are met if you took or used the act at any time from the start of your most recent turn in this Titan Engagement or Skirmish until now, on that turn or outside it. That reaches Help, Cover, a dodge, and a Push made on a comrade's turn.

Called rolls and improvised acts. A called roll counts as your own act of the entry the GM named for it, and an improvised act as your own act of its model entry. A called roll made with an attribute alone names no entry, so it is no act.

Turn triggers count turns in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish. They are never met before your first turn there, and never outside one; a Leg roll or a camp roll is not a turn. Position triggers need a Titan Engagement and are never met in a Skirmish. Freedom's flight trigger can be met anywhere.

Named comrade. Stay Beside Them counts only acts used on your named comrade: Help to their roll, Cover of their Push, or an entry taken on them.

Pay It Forward counts an act used on a comrade who was Grabbed or Down when you used it, or who is Grabbed or Down when the result is about to take effect. The act must already have been used. The acts that count are Help to that comrade's roll, Cover of their Push, Treat Injury or Lift Comrade taken on them, Break Free on a Grabbed comrade's behalf (Pry Loose), and a Body Part strike against the hand that holds a Grabbed comrade.

  • A named comrade is a living player character or Squadmate in the Squad, other than you. A Drive recorded after the Squad forms (a replacement Drive, a promoted Squadmate's Drive, or a new character's Drive) names its comrade when it is recorded. If no other living soldier is in the Squad, record the Drive of a row that does not need one.
  • If your named comrade dies, retires, or leaves the Squad, your Drive can never trigger again. Promotion does not make a soldier leave the Squad.
  • At the start of the next session after your Drive can no longer trigger, pick a new Drive from any row. You gain no attribute point.

Training Years

Resolve each Training Year the same way:

  1. Event. Roll D66 on the year's events. Add 1 to its attribute, gain 1 level in one of its two Talents, and add its Merit to your Merit total. Merit can go down.
  2. Performance roll. Make the year's performance roll and add the Merit it earns.

Talent limits. No Talent can be above level 2 at creation, and a rule Talent cannot go above level 1. If neither of an event's Talents can gain a level, take 1 level in any Talent on that year's curriculum that can. A new Lifepath soldier gains exactly 5 Talent levels: 1 from the Origin, 3 from the events, and 1 from the Specialty.

The performance roll

  • Roll the higher of the year's two performance attributes; if they are equal, pick one.
  • Roll attribute dice only: no Talent dice, Bonus Dice, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice. It cannot be Pushed or Helped, and each year has exactly one.
  • Find your successes on the table below. If you run the Graduation Exam, it replaces the Third Year's performance roll, but the Third Year's event still happens.
Performance roll successesMerit
00
11
22
3 or more3

Graduation

  1. Class Rank. Add up your Merit and find your Class Rank below. Several graduates can share one.
  2. Specialty. Choose one of the nine Specialties. It is not rolled and has no requirement.
  3. Swap. If another attribute is rated higher than your key attribute, swap the key attribute's rating with the highest of the other five (pick one if several tie).
  4. Floor. If the key attribute is still below 4, raise it to 4, and for each point added lower another attribute rated 3 or more by 1.
  5. Top 10. If your Class Rank row says Top 10, you are offered a place in the Military Police and decline it; record "Declined the Military Police". Your key attribute gains 1, to a maximum of 6.
  6. Specialty Talent. Gain 1 level in one Talent on your Specialty's list, to a maximum of level 2.

A Template Build or Free Build soldier, and a promoted Squadmate, has no Merit total and uses no row: record Merit none and Class Rank none. They are never Top 10.

Example: rolling the Lifepath

The players record Campaign Year 846 and vote not to run the Exam. Mira starts with every attribute at 2.

  • Origin 25: Wall Rose Farm. Strength and Perception go to 3. She takes Horsemanship 1 and the family farm as her Haven.
  • Why You Enlisted 42: Strength goes to 4. She keeps the row's Drive, Shield the Walls.
  • First Year 44: Fire in the stables. Perception 4, Keen Eyes 1, Merit 1. She rolls Strength (4 dice) for performance and scores 1 success: Merit 2.
  • Second Year 13: The wooden nape. Strength 5, Clean Cut 1, Merit 4. She rolls Perception (4 dice) and scores 1 success: Merit 5.
  • Third Year 34: Arguing with an instructor. Empathy 3, Silver Tongue 1, Merit 4. She rolls Empathy (3 dice) and scores 1 success: Merit 5.
  • Graduation. Merit 5 is Class Rank 25, not Top 10. She chooses Slayer. Strength is already her highest rating, so there is no swap. Her Specialty level raises Clean Cut to 2.

Mira finishes with Strength 5, Agility 2, Wits 2, Perception 4, Instinct 2, and Empathy 3 (18 points); Clean Cut 2, Horsemanship 1, Keen Eyes 1, and Silver Tongue 1 (5 levels); Health 6 and Resolve 3. Had she chosen Tactician, her Instinct 2 would have swapped with her Strength 5.

The Graduation Exam (optional)

The Exam is a short played prologue of three Trials. Its Merit replaces the Third Year's performance roll.

  • Who and when. If you vote to run it, every Cadet created together with the Lifepath takes it, after every Cadet has rolled their Third Year event and before Graduation. A replacement character created later decides before their own Lifepath and takes it alone.
  • Built soldiers take no Exam. A Template Build or Free Build soldier takes no Trial and earns no Exam Merit, whatever the vote. Only Cadets rolling the Lifepath count in each Trial's order and in the field exercise's Help.
  • It is not a Titan Engagement. There are no rounds, turns, Positions, Attention, or Titans. Requirements that need any of those, a Grabbed or Down soldier, a patient, a comrade with a Stress Response, or a worn or Jammed item are ignored.
  • Exam issue. Each Cadet uses training ODM Gear, a training Blade Set, a training medical kit, and a training tool kit, each rated 1. There are no Gas Rolls and no Blade Set counting. Wear lasts only for the Exam, and the issue is handed back when it ends.
  • Pools. Every Trial roll uses the full dice pool. Bonus Dice come only from the third Trial's Help. Rule Talents apply. Only the third Trial can be Pushed.
  • Stress. Every Cadet starts at 0. Stress carries into later Trials, and returns to 0 when the Exam ends.
  • Stress Responses still happen, and the roll still cannot be Pushed after one. Instead of the Stress Response table, each costs the Cadet 1 Merit on that Trial, which can make a Trial's Merit negative.
  • Order. Every Cadet completes a Trial before the next begins. Before each Trial, every Cadet rolls a D6 and they go highest first; ties roll again.
OrderTrialAction Catalog entryGear itemCan be PushedHelpMerit
1The ODM balance testFlytraining ODM Gearnonone0–1 successes: 0 Merit; 2 or more successes: 1 Merit
2The Titan dummy courseNape striketraining Blade Setnonone0–1 successes: 0 Merit; 2 or more successes: 1 Merit
3The squad field exerciseone of the entry choices belowlisted with each choiceyesfixed by the Trial's roll order (see the list below)0–2 successes: 0 Merit; 3 or more successes: 1 Merit
The squad field exercise: entry choices (each needs 3 successes)
EntryGear item
Readnone
Break Attentiontraining ODM Gear
Body Part striketraining Blade Set
Treat Injurytraining medical kit
Field Repairtraining tool kit
Rallynone
  • Trials 1 and 2 cannot be Pushed, Helped, or Covered.
  • Trial 3: Help is fixed. The Cadet who rolls next in the order Helps the current roller, and the first roller Helps the last. Each Cadet Helps exactly one roll, declared before it, and that Help spends nothing else. A Cadet taking the Exam alone has no Help.
  • Trial 3: Covering. Any Cadet who could Help a roll can Cover its Push, unless they have already made their own roll in this Trial. Covering spends nothing.
  • Each Cadet's Merit comes only from their own rolls.

Building without the Lifepath

When the campaign allows them, you can make your soldier with the Template Build or the Free Build instead of rolling. The two builds share every step except the attributes.

  1. Campaign Year. If the campaign has none yet, record it. A built soldier takes no Graduation Exam, whatever the vote.
  2. Specialty. Choose one of the nine Specialties. It is not rolled, has no requirement, and never changes.
  3. Attributes.
    • Template Build: record your Specialty's template ratings from the Squadmate templates, unchanged. Take only the ratings, not the template's Talent.
    • Free Build: choose one of the two arrays below and place its six ratings on your six attributes, one each, with a 4 on your key attribute.
  4. Origin. Choose an Origin row whose condition the Campaign Year meets. Record one of its two Havens and, if you want it, its Canon Tie. Gain level 1 in one of its two Talents. The row's attributes gain nothing.
  5. Drive. Record the Drive of any Why You Enlisted row. No attribute gains a point.
  6. Training Years, for the story. You may name one event row of each Training Year. A named row gives no attribute point, Talent level, or Merit, and you make no performance roll.
  7. Talents. Gain 1 level in one Talent on your Specialty's list, then 3 levels in any Talents, within the limits below.
  8. Merit and Class Rank. Record Merit none and Class Rank none. You are not Top 10: there is no Military Police offer and no key attribute bonus.
  9. Finish and join the Squad as a Lifepath soldier does (see Creating your soldier).

Attribute ratings

Every built soldier has 18 attribute points, every attribute rated 2 to 4, the key attribute at exactly 4, and at most two attributes at 4. Health and Resolve come from the ratings you record. Only two Free Build arrays meet those limits:

Free Build arrays
ArrayRatings to place
One 44, 3, 3, 3, 3, 2
Two 4s4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2

Talent limits

  • 5 levels in total: 1 from the Origin row, 1 from your Specialty's list, and 3 from any Talents.
  • No Talent above level 2, and at most one Talent at level 2.
  • No rule Talent above level 1.
  • A level gained in a Talent you already hold raises it by 1.

What only the Lifepath reaches

A built soldier always has 18 attribute points, a key attribute of 4, Health of 4 to 6, and Resolve of 2 to 4. A Free Build soldier who places both 2s on Strength and Agility has Health 4 and is fragile: they hold the fewest boxes in the Squad, and four untreated Critical Injuries put them Down.

Only the Lifepath can give a key attribute of 5 or 6, a Top 10 Class Rank, or a place in the Graduation Exam. If you want that chance, roll.

Example: a Free Build

The campaign allows the Free Build. Tomas's player chooses Medic, whose key attribute is Wits, and the two-4s array: Wits 4, Empathy 4, Perception 3, Instinct 3, Strength 2, Agility 2. That gives Health 4, so Tomas is fragile, and Resolve 4.

He chooses Doctor's Household as his Origin, takes his parent's clinic as his Haven and Careful Nursing 1, and records the Drive Duty. His Specialty level goes into Field Medicine. His 3 free levels raise Field Medicine to 2 and add Steady Voice 1 and Iron Nerve 1. That is 5 levels, with one Talent at level 2. He records Merit none and Class Rank none, then finishes like any other soldier.

  • Choosing. You choose your Specialty at Graduation, or at a build's Specialty step. It is not rolled, has no requirement, and never changes.
  • What it grants: its key attribute, the only attribute that can be rated 6; on the Lifepath, the Graduation swap and floor; and 1 Talent level from its list, to a maximum of level 2.
  • The Talent list limits only that one Specialty level. The General list is not a Specialty: any soldier can hold its Talents, but the Specialty level never goes into one.
  • A Specialty never gives permission. Every player character can attempt every Action Catalog entry and hold any Talent, including one from another Specialty's list. A Squadmate can attempt every entry except Cover and the Downtime Action. It never Pushes, and its only Reaction is the dodge. Without the Talent you still roll your full attribute.
  • Through Standard Issue, a Medic also receives a medical kit and an Engineer a tool kit, each rated 1. A Squadmate uses its template's Specialty.

Talents

A Talent is a narrow trained ability that adds dice or bends a rule, but only for the Action Catalog entries it names. There are 84 Talents: 33 dice Talents and 51 rule Talents, listed below by Specialty list with the General list last. A Talent's description only summarizes; its other columns are the rule.

Levels

  • Talents run from level 0 to 3. No Talent can be above level 2 at creation.
  • Every rule Talent has a maximum level of 1. Holding it at level 1 means having it.

Dice Talents

  • A dice Talent adds base dice equal to its level to a roll for an entry it names.
  • If the Talent gives a condition for that entry, it adds its dice only to a roll that meets the condition. Horsemanship adds its dice to a dodge only when you make it mounted, with your horse as its gear item. Ground Work adds its dice only against a grounded Titan.
  • A condition can name a moment of a playtest procedure, such as Route Finder's "on the Leg roll, as Lead". A roll made anywhere else never meets it.
  • A roll uses at most one dice Talent. If several qualify, you pick. A Talent that does not name the roll's entry adds no dice, however close the action seems. On a called roll, that is the entry the GM named.
  • No Talent changes, ignores, or lowers a roll's Circumstances. A Talent helps against bad conditions only by adding its dice.

Rule Talents

  • A rule Talent applies its effect every time its trigger happens, within its limit. Rule Talents stack with each other and with your dice Talent.
  • A rule Talent changes only what its effect says. Unless the effect says so, it adds no Bonus Dice, changes no Stress, and calls for no roll.
  • "You declare" triggers happen as you declare the entry, before its requirements, targets, pool, and cost are checked, so the Talent changes those checks. If the use still does not qualify, it is not taken and nothing is spent. Every other trigger happens when the event it describes happens.
  • A rule Talent whose effect changes something the whole Squad shares, such as a Leg's hazard roll, a Night Camp's ration, or Camp Relief, applies once for that use, however many soldiers hold it.

Limits

  • Once per Titan Engagement means once between the start and end of each Titan Engagement. The same Talent can also be used once per Leg, once per Night Camp, and once per Skirmish where its trigger can happen. It is never usable outside those.
  • A fight inside a Leg or a Night Camp has its own use. A Titan Engagement or a Skirmish that begins inside a Leg or a Night Camp is its own procedure: a use in the fight does not spend the Leg's or the Night Camp's, and a use in the Leg or the Night Camp does not spend the fight's.
  • Once per Leg, once per Night Camp, once per Skirmish, and once per Downtime mean once during each such procedure, and never outside one.
  • Mark each limited use beside its Talent on your sheet.

Gaining Talents

  • At creation you gain 5 levels. On the Lifepath: 1 from your Origin, 3 from your Training Year events, and 1 from your Specialty. On a build: 1 from your Origin row, 1 from your Specialty's list, and 3 in any Talents, with at most one Talent at level 2.
  • The Lifepath's rows offer only some Talents. A Talent no row offers is reached by a build, or after creation.
  • After creation, Talents come from XP and the Train Downtime Action, which Phase 1 does not include.

Talent reference

Talents are grouped by Specialty list, with the general list last; a rule Talent that states no limit works every time its trigger occurs.

Squadmates

A Squadmate is a named NPC soldier who rides with the player characters. Any player can direct one, Titans can target one like anyone else, it never Pushes or Covers, and it can be promoted to a player character. The Squadmates currently serving are the Squad Pool.

The starting Squad

  • The Squad aims for 6 soldiers. It starts with as many Squadmates as 6 minus the number of player characters, or none with 6 or more player characters. The default Squad is 4 player characters and 2 Squadmates.
  • Fewer is allowed. At session zero the players may start with fewer Squadmates, down to none. When they disagree on how many, it is a shared choice. It costs two things: fights run about three times as deadly for the player characters, and a player character who dies or retires while no living Squadmate is in the Squad Pool has no one to promote.
  • For each starting Squadmate, the players choose a template, name it, and give it Standard Issue. It starts with Health lost 0, not Down, Stress 0, and no Scars, Grief, or Critical Injuries.
  • In Downtime, the Recruit Squad Action brings the Squad Pool back up to 6 minus the number of player characters, except while the playtest configuration holds (see Downtime).

What Squadmates are for. Two more soldiers mean two more turns a round and more Help on every strike, so Titans die sooner and the Squad faces fewer of their cards. Squadmates reach a Grabbed comrade in time to cut the holding hand or help them break free. They turn a Titan's head with a decoy, or screen the soldier who holds its Attention. When a player character dies or retires, one of them is promoted to take that player's seat. And they are comrades a Drive can name and a Squad can grieve. The easiest way to run them is on Wings: a Squadmate on a Wing acts right after its player character, and that player has the last word on what it does.

Playtest configuration Playtest rule

The first playtest campaign runs 4 player characters and no Squadmates, from session zero on. While it holds:

  • the starting Squad is the 4 player characters alone;
  • Recruit is not offered and brings no Squadmate, so the Squad Action is Honoring the Fallen;
  • no Squadmate serves, so no one is promoted: a player character who dies or retires is replaced by a new character;
  • every Straggler victim is a player character, and Formation Drill has no effect.

Squadmates stay the rule for every other campaign.

Brawler432333Grip Breaker 163

A Squadmate's key attribute is 4, four others are 3, and one is 2, for 18 points. Its Health is worked out from its own Strength and Agility like any soldier's, so it is 5 or 6. It has one dice Talent at level 1. A Template Build player character takes these same ratings, without the Talent.

Which rules apply

Fear Rollsyes
Health, Critical Injuries, Down, and Death Rollsyes

Griefyes
Driveno
Gas RollsyesAlways two dice, because a Squadmate never Pushes.
Blade Sets, carried items, and Overloadedyes
Talent dice and rule TalentsyesOnly the template Talent.
XP and gaining TalentsnoA Squadmate gains no XP and no Talent levels.
Downtime ActionsnoA Squadmate takes no Downtime Action. In each Downtime it loses 2 Stress and 1 Grief instead.
  • A Squadmate can take every entry a player character can take as an action, including Help and options such as Call It, and makes any roll a rule calls for. It must meet each entry's requirements.
  • In a Skirmish it takes part like a player character, on its own card.
  • It records gear exactly as a player character does. Because it never Pushes, its gear never wears from a Push.
  • Its stat block records name, Specialty, attributes, Talent, Health and Health lost, Resolve, Stress, minimum Stress, Scars, Grief, Critical Injuries, Down, gear, its Wing, and every other body and mind line of a character sheet. It has no Origin, Haven, Drive, Canon Tie, Merit, Class Rank, or XP.

Directing Squadmates

  • Any player can propose what a Squadmate does.
  • If players disagree about a Squadmate on a Wing, the player whose soldier's Wing it is decides.
  • If players disagree about a Squadmate on no Wing, use the roll-off.
  • The GM never directs a Squadmate, though the GM may voice one.

Promotion

When a player character dies or retires (see Retirement), their player takes over a Squadmate.

  1. When. When the procedure in which the death or Retirement happened ends: the last end step of a Titan Engagement, the last step of a day passing, the end of a care window held for harm outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, with its Death Rolls, the last step of Downtime, or the end of any other procedure as its rule states. If no procedure is under way, it happens at once.
  2. Who chooses. First, every soldier due to retire retires, and a retiring Squadmate leaves the Squad Pool. Then every player whose soldier died or retired chooses a living Squadmate in the Squad Pool, all at the same time. If two or more choose the same one, each rolls a D6; the highest takes it, and ties roll again. The others choose again from those left, until everyone has one or none remain.
  3. Conversion. The Squadmate keeps everything it has, just as it stands: its attributes, Health, injuries, Stress, Scars, Grief, and gear. Its template Talent counts as its Specialty Talent level. It rolls an Origin for a Haven, an optional Canon Tie, and 1 level in one of the row's Talents; rolls Why You Enlisted for a Drive, naming a comrade now if needed; and rolls once on each Training Year's events for 1 Talent level each. A Talent it already holds rises by 1 level, within the creation limits. It gains no attribute points and no Merit, records Merit and Class Rank as none and Rank as Private, leaves any Wing, and follows every player character rule from then on. It stays in the Squad.
  4. No Squadmate left. Create a new soldier by any method the campaign allows; the Template Build, when allowed, is the quickest. The new soldier joins the Squad, with Standard Issue, at whichever comes first: the next Waypoint the Squad reaches while an Expedition is under way; the start of the next Titan Engagement while no Expedition is under way; or the next Downtime.

Your character sheet

Who you are

  • Name, Origin, Haven, and Canon Tie (optional).
  • Drive, its named comrade if it needs one, and whether you have used it this session (clear this at the start of each session).
  • Attributes, Specialty, and Talents, with each limited use marked beside its Talent.
  • Merit and Class Rank (none for a built soldier or a promoted Squadmate), "Declined the Military Police" for a Top 10 Class Rank, and Rank (Private).

Body

  • Health as a row of boxes, Health lost, and whether you are Down. Mark a box with a slash for damage and an X for an untreated Critical Injury. Treating erases an X; a day passing or a revive erases slashes.
  • Critical Injuries, one line each: its Injury Location and side (left or right for an arm or a leg, none for the torso or head), its Injury Type (Crush, Bite, Burn, Cut, or Pierce), whether it is treated, its time limit (turn, Titan Engagement, day, or none), healing days left, and whether Careful Nursing has halved it. For example: "Right arm, Bite: Bitten Off at the Shoulder, untreated, turn, 28 days".
  • Healed Critical Injuries with permanent effects, each with its side.
  • Pinned, while you are: the body that pins you (its Focus Titan's letter, and whether it is a Corpse), the pinned limb and its side or a body pin, and the pinning Body Part if any. For example: "Pinned under A (Corpse), right arm, by right arm".
  • Prosthetics fitted, each with its kind (arm or leg) and the side it is fitted to.

Mind

  • Resolve, Stress, minimum Stress, Scars, and Grief.
  • Which Critical Injuries have already given you a Scar.
  • Lasting Stress Responses and when each ends, who has Rallied you outside a Titan Engagement since your last lasting Stress Response, and any dice penalty waiting on your next roll.
  • Fear Roll results still waiting: a forced move and where it leads, or a forced strike and its Titan. Also how many Blade Sets a Fear Roll result has made you drop this Titan Engagement.
  • Has faced a Titan. Has killed a person. Retiring, from your fifth Scar or your choice of medical Retirement until Retirement happens.

Gear (see the gear sheet)

  • ODM Gear rating and current rating, Gas Rating, spare canisters, the Blade Set in your handles and each carried Blade Set's rating, your horse (rating, current rating, mounted or not, and in a Titan Engagement its zone while you are dismounted), medical kits, tool kits, and each firearm with its kind, rating, current rating, and whether it is loaded.
  • Which of your items are Kept Items.
  • Whether you are airborne, the comrade you carry, and the comrade carrying you.
  • In a Titan Engagement, your zone and your attachment; and after your death, the zone where your left items lie.
  • Shot, like all Squad Supply, is recorded once for the whole Squad on the Squad sheet.

Actions

"If it is not on the list, it is not a drill. Find the drill that does what you need."Survey Corps field manual, section on improvisation

The Action Catalog is the complete list of named actions and rolls a soldier can make. Talents name its entries, and every attribute roll is made for one of them. Every player character can attempt every entry, whatever their Specialty, as long as they meet its requirements.

A Squadmate can attempt every entry except Cover and the Downtime Action. It never Pushes, and its only Reaction is the dodge.

The Action Catalog

Type tells you how an entry is used:

  • An action spends your action on your turn in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish.
  • A Reaction is a dodge or a Block made when a Titan or a Foe attacks you, outside your own turn. It spends one whole turn: a move and an action.
  • A roll is made when a rule calls for it, or, for the entries a called roll is made for, when the GM calls one. It is not an action and spends no turn.
  • An option is part of something else. It spends nothing of its own unless its row says so.

Roll and Gear Dice names the attribute and the gear that can add Gear Dice. If more than one item qualifies, you pick one.

Leap Clear is not a dodge. It is made whatever you have spent, and it spends nothing.

Called rolls. Fly, Ride, Spot, Size Up, Survive, Persuade, Endure, Sneak, and Recall are also the entries a called roll is made for: the GM may call one for any act it fits. A called roll needs 1, Fly included outside a Titan Engagement, and its Help is as the GM rules outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, and that fight's own Help inside one. In a Skirmish, Fly is named only for a called roll, the one Fly roll a Skirmish has (see Skirmishes). The Stress Response roll, the Fear Roll, the Gas Roll, the Death Roll, the performance roll, and Leap Clear are made only when their rule calls for them.

Downtime options Playtest rule
EntryTypeWhoChoose one row
Downtime Actionoption of the Downtime Actions stepEach player character, once per Downtime. Squadmates take none.Recover, Visit Haven, Requisition, or Maintain Gear
Squad Actionoption of the Squad Action stepThe Squad, once per DowntimeHonoring the Fallen, or Recruit (not offered while the playtest configuration holds)

Entries that need gear

This applies every time an entry is taken, made, or called for: whether you name it or reach it through an action outside the Catalog, and whether the soldier is a player character or a Squadmate. Some entries need one of their gear items. If you have none of them, the entry tells you what happens:

  • Wits alone. Treat Injury and Field Repair roll Wits with no Talent dice and no Gear Dice. Everything else about the entry still applies. Only Make Do changes this, for Field Repair.
  • Cannot be used. The Nape strike, Body Part strike, Fly, and Ride cannot be made at all. A soldier whose last Blade Set is gone cannot make a Nape strike, however the player describes it.
  • No Gear Dice. Every other entry with gear items is rolled without Gear Dice when you have none: Break Attention, Break Free, the dodge, Block, Fight, and Leap Clear.
  • Firearms. Shoot is taken only with a loaded firearm you hold, or with a flare fired at a person. A firearm that is empty or at 0 counts as not had, so it cannot be fired (Firearms).
  • Shot and flares. Reload needs 1 Shot in Squad Supply, and a fired flare needs 1 flare. With none left, the entry cannot be used.

An item at 0 counts as not had. So does ODM Gear with Gas Rating 0, a Blade Set that is not in your handles, a firearm that is empty, and, for the dodge, Ride, and Leap Clear, a horse you are not mounted on. For Break Attention, a horse counts if you are mounted on it or it is in your zone.

Actions outside the Catalog

You can describe anything your soldier does. Outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, when your action is meant to make one of the changes in the table below, the steps are the default way to resolve it; in a fight the steps are not used (see In a fight, below). The table is an index of what soldiers change by acting, not a limit on what they can try: an action the steps do not resolve goes to the GM, who rules on it before anyone rolls.

Resolving an action outside the Catalog, outside a fight

  1. Name the change. Pick the one change from the table below that your action is meant to make. Your description never limits which change you name. If it is meant to make none of them, the GM rules on it (below).
  2. Moves. If the change is your zone or attachment, or becoming Engaged with or Apart from a Foe, the action is a move.
  3. Match. Take the entries that make that change. If you meet the requirements of at least one, pick one. Your action is that entry for every purpose: its type, roll, gear, successes needed, effect, what it spends, and every Talent that names it.
  4. Gear. Apply the entry's gear need, as for any use of it. An entry you cannot use without gear cannot be picked.
  5. No match. If no entry can be used and the change is to a clock, counter, or obstacle whose rule names an attribute roll, make that roll with the attribute alone. Otherwise the GM rules on the action (below).

When the steps do not resolve an action

Outside a fight. Outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, the GM says that the action happens with no roll, that it cannot be done, or that it is a called roll. For a called roll the GM names, before you build the pool:

  1. The entry: the closest of Spot, Size Up, Survive, Persuade, Endure, Sneak, Recall, Ride, and Fly, or an attribute alone if none fits.
  2. The Circumstances, Standard if the GM names none (see Circumstances).
  3. The stakes: what failure costs, from the menu (see When to roll), and, where it is not obvious, what success gives, which is only what When to roll lists and never a gear item.

A called roll needs 1 success and is made for the named entry for every purpose: its attribute, its gear, its gear need, and every Talent that names it. It can be Pushed and Covered, and Help on it is as the GM rules. Once the dice are rolled, the entry, the Circumstances, and the stakes do not change.

In a fight. In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish the steps above are not used. Your action there is Help, an Action Catalog entry you name and take as it is written, or an improvised act: an act you describe that is not an entry taken as written, even when a change in the table below would match an entry. Whether what you describe is an entry taken as written is part of the GM's ruling. If the act would change nothing, it is described with no roll; if the GM judges it cannot be done, the GM says so before anyone rolls. Otherwise, before you build the pool, the GM names its model entry: the one existing entry whose effect the act imitates. That ruling stands. A model entry is an entry marked as an action that you could take in that fight, never a Reaction (the dodge or the Block), a roll such as Leap Clear, a fixed roll, or an option such as Call It, Cover, or Reload. The improvised act:

  • spends your action;
  • is made as the model entry is made: rolled only if that entry is rolled, with its attribute, gear, Talents, and needs, and with Circumstances as any roll;
  • meets every requirement and restriction of the model entry: the soldier holding a Titan's Attention still cannot strike its Nape, a Feint keeps its Position rule, and a Foe's Guard still rolls;
  • on a success, produces only the model entry's effect, at that entry's size.

It never kills a Titan, never changes Nape Depth, Toughness, Attack Dice, or a card, never cancels or delays a card, never creates more Openings than the model entry could, and never gives an effect no entry has. Only a Nape strike you take as its entry is written kills a Titan; an act modelled on the Nape strike never does. Such an act is resolved as a Nape strike that falls short, whatever its successes: each success creates 1 Opening, created by you, Relentless adds 1 more where its trigger is met, and the act marks you as having struck the Titan's Nape, as any Nape strike does, whatever its result. Successes that reach the Titan's Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else, and the act lowers no Nape Depth.

A called roll in a fight. When no entry marked as an action you could take in that fight has an effect the act imitates, and the act would change nothing the fight tracks (no Position, Engaged or Apart, Attention, Opening, card, clock, or Foe value, and no change in the table below), it is a called roll, made as Outside a fight gives: hauling a granary's doors shut so a Titan cannot follow the civilians through. It spends your action and is made on your turn, never in place of a Reaction. Help on it is that fight's Help. A fall it stakes is low or high, never extreme, and no height step raises it; it is a fall in full, and it lands as Falls gives, a change of Position the fall rule makes and not the ruling, so a soldier who falls from On Body or Blind Spot holds In Reach once the fall is resolved. A cost of time or position changes only the situation outside what the fight tracks, and success gives the act, information, or an object that is not a gear item, never a Position, never found Squad Supply, and never an effect an entry has. You can try it again on a later turn, spending that turn's action.

Taking cover. A dive behind a chimney stack is a move if it changes your zone or attachment, and otherwise changes nothing and needs no roll. It is never modelled on the dodge and makes no later dodge easier: Positions already price where you stand.

Rolls called by attribute. A rule that calls for an attribute roll without naming a Catalog entry calls for that attribute alone: no Talent dice, and Gear Dice only from a gear item the rule names. Every other step of building a dice pool applies, and the rule states who can Help. A called roll made with an attribute alone is built the same way, with Help as the GM rules outside a fight and as that fight's Help in one. A ruling names no gear item, so it takes no Gear Dice: a gear item or other object that helps counts toward its Circumstances instead, and a Push cannot wear it. A called roll the GM names for Fly or Ride takes that entry's Gear Dice.
