Deal damage to a FoeFight, Shoot
Hold a Foe with a GrappleFight
Let go of a Foe you holdRelease
Load an empty firearmReload
Make a Foe group yieldPersuade
Learn a Foe group's Grit, how many more of it must fall before it breaks, and what each of its Foes does on its next cardSize Up
Give the Squad the Ambush against a Foe groupSneak
Lower a soldier's Stress in DowntimeDowntime Action
Lower a soldier's Grief in DowntimeDowntime Action, Squad Action
Bring a new Squadmate into the Squad PoolSquad Action
Change Position, or become Engaged with or Apart from a Foenone: this is a move
Change a clock, counter, or obstacle a rule creates and names an attribute roll fornone: roll that attribute alone

Examples

  • You bind a comrade's bleeding arm with a torn sleeve. The change is "treat or stabilize a Critical Injury", so the action is Treat Injury. You have no medical kit, so you roll Wits alone.
  • In a Titan Engagement, you shout so a comrade can avoid a Titan's hand. No entry is a shout, so it is an improvised act, and the GM names its model entry before the pool is built. Call It, which adds dice to a comrade's dodge from a Read, is an option and never a model entry, so the GM names Help: the shout spends your action, needs Help's Position, is not rolled, and adds 1 die to the comrade's dodge.
  • You jam a broken lance under a Corpse's wrist to lever it off a Pinned comrade. The change is "free a soldier it Pins". You hold In Reach and are not Down, so you pick Heave: roll Strength with no Gear Dice, add each success to the Corpse's Heave count, and take 1 Burn damage after the roll.
  • You steady your own nerves with a prayer in the middle of a fight. No entry lowers your own Stress outside Downtime, so the GM rules that the prayer happens, with no roll and no mechanical effect: Stress relief comes only from a written trigger.
  • During an evacuation, you brace a collapsing beam so civilians can get clear. No change in the table fits, the outcome is in doubt, and failure would cost something, so the GM calls a roll: Strength alone, at Hard because the beam is already sagging. The stakes: if you fail, the beam comes down and you take 2 Crush damage. You roll Strength with a 1-die penalty and your Stress Dice, needing 1 success. You may Push, and a comrade beside the beam may Help as the GM rules.
  • In a fight: a supply wagon cut loose to roll past a Titan is Break Attention, with its needs; a lantern swung from a rooftop is Draw Attention, never from Distant; a chimney dropped on a Titan's leg is a Body Part strike whose successes count toward that leg's Toughness; a rope thrown to a comrade is Help or Lift Comrade; and an object that is not a gear item, hurled at a person, is modelled on Shoot, at damage 1 and Crush, with no Gear Dice (see Skirmish weapons).

Harm and Mind

"Bind it tight, keep them talking, and do not look at the hand in the trees."Survey Corps field medic, to a new arrival

This chapter covers what the field does to a soldier's body and nerve. A rule or a called roll's stakes name every harm, and a table gives every result. The GM rules on harm only before the dice: the stakes of a called roll may name damage of 1 to 3 or a fall (see Harm a ruling names), and outside a Titan Engagement the GM may call a Fear Roll for a horror no trigger lists. The GM never deals a Critical Injury or death directly, and never picks an Injury Location, a row, or how long an effect lasts.

Health

Your Health is 2 plus half of Strength plus Agility, rounded up, kept as a row of that many boxes: 4 to 6 on a standard build, and as high as 8 at the ceiling of the Lifepath. It never changes. Each box is clean, marked by damage, or crossed off by a Critical Injury.

  • Crossed off. Each untreated Critical Injury you hold crosses off one box. The boxes crossed off are the smaller of your untreated Critical Injuries and your Health, so extra ones cross off nothing.
  • Health lost is damage you have taken. It is never more than Health minus the boxes crossed off.
  • Current Health is Health, minus the boxes crossed off, minus Health lost, never below 0.

The kinds of harm

Every harm is one of three kinds, and the rule that inflicts it names the kind and its Injury Type (see Injury Types).

  • Damage adds to Health lost. It comes from harm that is not a Titan attack. It names the Injury Type of the Critical Injury it gives at 0 current Health.
  • A Critical Injury crosses off a box while untreated and never adds to Health lost.
  • Death kills the soldier. It comes from an instant-death row, a failed Death Roll, the Grab's devour, or any other rule that names death. No Behavior Table entry names death.
Where harm comes from
SourceKindInjury TypeRule

A Falling Titan, on a soldier who fails to Leap ClearCritical Injury that cannot be lethal, and PinnedCrushThe Falling Titan

A failed called roll whose stakes named harmdamage of 1 to 3, or a fallthe type of its source: Crush, Cut, Pierce, or BurnHarm a ruling names

Harm a ruling names

When the GM calls a roll, the stakes named before the dice may say that failure harms the soldier who rolled. They name one of two harms, and no other:

  • Damage of 1 to 3, of the Injury Type of its source: Crush for a blow or a hard knock, Cut for an edge, Pierce for a point, Burn for fire. It is taken as any damage, so it reaches a Critical Injury only at 0 current Health, and that Critical Injury's Injury Location is rolled.
  • A fall, low or high as the GM names for its height, never extreme, or low if the GM names none (see Falls).

The GM says your current Health with the harm, so the table knows before the dice whether a failure can put you Down with a Critical Injury. The harm does not change once the dice are rolled. It is never 4 damage or more. No ruling deals a Critical Injury, death, a Death Roll, a Scar, or Grief directly, names an Injury Location, a row, or a time limit, or opens or closes a care window. Harm a ruling causes outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish holds a care window as any other harm there does.

Taking damage

  1. If the damage is 0, it does no harm, whatever your current Health: Health lost does not change, and you gain no Critical Injury.
  2. If your current Health is above 0, add the damage to Health lost, up to Health minus the boxes crossed off.
  3. If that brings current Health to 0, you are Down and gain one Critical Injury of the damage's Injury Type at once. Then check Down again.
  4. If your current Health was already 0, Health lost does not change, and you gain one Critical Injury of the damage's Injury Type at once.

Roll the Injury Location unless the damage's rule names one.

A Critical Injury with no clean box crosses off a box marked by damage instead, and Health lost falls by 1. If every box is already crossed off, it crosses off none.

A Critical Injury that crosses off your last box makes you Down and gives no further Critical Injury. Only damage that brings current Health to 0, or damage taken at 0, gives one.

Titan attacks bypass Health

A Titan attack is the harm a Behavior Table entry inflicts when a Titan's card resolves it against you. It is always a Critical Injury, never damage, whatever your Health. A Titan's Bite is Bite. Every other Titan attack is Crush.

When a Titan attacks you, its entry's Attack Dice are rolled in the open as Titan Dice. Each success of your dodge cancels one of its successes, and the attack lands on 1 or more Net Successes. If the Titan's roll scores 0 successes, it whiffs against everyone. A soldier who cannot make a Reaction, such as a Down soldier, cancels nothing, so the attack lands on them on 1 or more successes. A landed attack's Critical Injury adds 1 to its roll for each Net Success beyond the first (see Gaining a Critical Injury).

Harm another rule causes as a result, such as a fall when you become Down while airborne, is the kind that rule names.

The Grab's crush is a torso Crush Critical Injury that cannot be lethal. The Grab lands on 1 or more Net Successes, and its crush never adds for Net Successes. The Grab's lift and devour are steps of the Grab, not Titan attacks: they roll no Attack Dice, and no Reaction answers them.

Titan harm outside the Behavior Tables. Steam, the Falling Titan, and Corpse Heat are not Titan attacks. They roll no Attack Dice, no Reaction answers them, and their Critical Injuries never add for Net Successes. Steam is damage, so it reaches the Critical Injury tables only at 0 current Health.

A soldier with no Health lost can be Down, or dying, from Titan attacks alone: each Critical Injury crosses off a box, and a row can put you Down or be lethal.

Getting Health back

  • A revive (Treat Injury on a Down soldier at 0 current Health) erases 1 damage mark per success.
  • A day passing erases every damage mark. Crossed-off boxes stay.
  • Treating or healing a Critical Injury gives back its box, unless you still hold at least as many untreated Critical Injuries as your Health.

Nothing else restores Health in the field.

Critical Injuries

A Critical Injury is a lasting injury at one Injury Location (left arm, right arm, left leg, right leg, torso, or head) with one Injury Type (Crush, Bite, Burn, Cut, or Pierce). Its row says whether it puts you Down, whether it is lethal and how fast, its effects, and its healing time.

Gaining a Critical Injury

  1. Injury Location. Use the one the harm's rule names. If it names an arm or a leg without a side, roll D6 for the side: odd is left, even is right. Otherwise roll D6 on the Injury Location table.
  2. Roll 2D6 and add 2 for each Critical Injury that counts toward worsening at that Injury Location and side. Every Critical Injury you hold there counts, treated or not, and so does every healed one there whose row has permanent effects. One at your other arm or other leg never counts. The torso and the head have no side. Both arms use the arm table, and both legs the leg table.
  3. Net Successes. If a Titan attack inflicts it and landed with 2 or more Net Successes, add 1 to the total for each Net Success beyond the first. Against a soldier who made no Reaction, or could not make one, every success of the Titan's roll is net. Nothing else takes this bonus: not the Grab's crush, a fall from a knock loose, damage, or a Stress gain. Find the row whose results include the total.
  4. Gained before. If that row has permanent effects and you have gained it before at the same side, held or healed, use the row its Effects cell names instead. You lose each arm and each leg at most once, and an eye at most once.
  5. Cannot be lethal. If the harm's rule says its Critical Injury cannot be lethal and the row is now lethal or instant death, use the row the table's caption names instead, whatever the Net Successes added.
  6. Rider. If the table has a rider for this Injury Type on this row, apply it (see Injury Types and riders).
  7. Record it as untreated, with its side, its Injury Type, its time limit, and its healing time. It crosses off a Health box. Apply its effects, its permanent effects, and any lost-limb grade you now meet. Gain any Stress it names once, now. On an instant-death row, you die instead.
  8. Check Down. If it crossed off your last box, you are Down, and no further Critical Injury follows.

The worsening bonus makes a worse row more likely, not certain: the row comes from the new total alone. Every table's first and last rows are open-ended, so every total has a row.

Injury Location (D6)
D6Injury Location
1left arm
2right arm
3left leg
4right leg
5torso
6head

Side. When a rule names an arm or a leg without a side, roll D6: odd, left; even, right.

Example: a Bite lands

The Standard Medium Titan's Bite has 9 Attack Dice and names the torso. Its Titan Dice score Severity 4. Ilse dodges and scores 2 successes, which cancel 2. The Bite lands with 2 Net Successes.

Ilse already holds a treated Crush Critical Injury at her torso. She rolls 2D6 and adds 2 for worsening and 1 for the second Net Success. The dice show 5, for a total of 8: the torso's 8–9 row. Its Bite name is Ribs Staved In by Teeth: a 2-die penalty on Nape strike, Body Part strike, and Break Free, healing in 14 days. The torso table has no Bite rider. The injury crosses off one of her Health boxes.

Injury Types and riders

The rule that inflicts harm names its Injury Type. Damage names the type that the Critical Injury it gives at 0 current Health records. The GM never picks a type, except that damage a called roll's stakes name takes the type of its source. The type picks which of the row's names the Critical Injury shows, and applies any rider its table carries for that type. Nothing else about the row changes.

  • Crush has no rider.
  • Bite (arm and leg tables): the 12 and 13 or more rows have a Turn limit.
  • Burn (every table): healing days are doubled, and Treat Injury on it needs a medical kit or medical supplies (see Treat Injury). Every lethal row has a Day limit, so a Burn kills by the day.
  • Cut (every table): every lethal row with an Engagement limit has a Turn limit instead. A lethal Cut bleeds: its Death Roll comes at the end of each of your turns, in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish.
  • Pierce (every table): from the 7 row up, Treat Injury on it takes a 1-die penalty, and it does not heal while untreated (see Healing time).
  • Blast is a delivery, not a type. A rule that names a blast inflicts two Critical Injuries, one Burn and one Crush, each at its own rolled Injury Location.

A rider applies only to the Critical Injury it picks. Its time limit replaces the row's, its doubled healing days multiply the row's, its no-healing rule keeps the injury from healing while untreated, and its Treat Injury requirement or penalty applies to every Treat Injury roll on it. The riders line under each table names the exact rows.

The Injury Location tables

Reading the tables. The second cell gives the row's name for each Injury Type. Down until treated means you are Down while this Critical Injury is untreated. Lethal gives its time limit (see Death Rolls). The Death Roll penalty removes base dice from each Death Roll made for it. Effects apply while you hold it and end when it heals. Permanent effects apply from the moment you gain it and stay for life. The arm table serves both arms, and the leg table both legs.

Holding and treating

  • You hold a Critical Injury from when you gain it until it heals. An arm or leg Critical Injury is held at its side. One at the torso or head has no side.
  • It becomes treated when a Treat Injury roll succeeds on it. Treating stabilizes a lethal one, ends an "until treated" Down, and gives back its box (unless you still hold at least as many untreated Critical Injuries as Health). It never removes effects or permanent effects. A treated Critical Injury keeps its healing time and still counts toward worsening.
  • Penalties stack with each other and with Stress Responses and Scars. They never take a pool below 1 base die and never remove Gear Dice or Stress Dice.
  • No Critical Injury damages or removes gear.

Lost limbs

You lose an arm or a leg at a side when you gain that table's 13 or more row there. The side stays lost after the Critical Injury heals. A lost-limb grade applies from the moment you meet it, for as long as you live, on top of the row's own permanent effects. Losing the second arm moves you from the one-arm grade to the both-arms grade. Legs work the same way.

Penalties stack. Every lost side keeps its row's permanent penalty, and they add up. With both legs lost you dodge, Fly, Ride, and Leap Clear at a 4-die penalty. With both arms lost and one Prosthetic arm fitted, you can strike again, at a 4-die penalty.

Lost-limb grades
GradeAddsCannot takeMovesKeeps
One arm lost1-die penalty on Fly, Fight, BlocknothingunchangedEverything else: Ride, the dodge, Read, Rally, Break Attention, Draw Attention, Help, and command from the saddle.
Both arms lostno Gear Dice from ODM Gear on the dodge or Leap ClearNape strike, Body Part strike, Break Free, Fly, Field Repair, Treat Injury, Lift Comrade, Heave, Pass Item, Take Item, Swap Blade Set, Change Canister, Restock Medical Kit, Fight, Block, Shoot, Reload; the Feint, Flare, and Thrown cloak decoysno ODM moves; Mount or Dismount only with a comrade's helpShed Load; Mount or Dismount with a comrade's help, and Ride while mounted; the dodge and Leap Clear, with the horse's Gear Dice while mounted; the riderless-horse decoy; Read; Call It; Rally; Draw Attention; Help; Cover; and Swap Initiative Card.
One leg lostnonenothingeach move on foot also spends your actionEverything else, flight included.
Both legs lostnoneFly, Ride, Lift Comradeno move of any kind: on foot, mounted, or ODMThe dodge and Leap Clear, with the rows' penalties, which stack; Heave; a strike at a Titan at In Reach; Read; Rally; Break Attention; Draw Attention; Treat Injury; Field Repair; and being carried by Lift Comrade, with your consent. You travel across a comrade's saddle or in the wagon.
  • Cannot take. You cannot take, make, or use the entry. A roll a rule calls for under it is not made, and whatever needs that roll cannot be done, such as an ODM move, which is a Flight and is always rolled. A Talent on that entry does nothing for you. A named decoy cannot be named for Break Attention.
  • A move that also spends your action works as an Overloaded soldier's ODM move does (see Carrying): you cannot make it if your action is already spent. Outside a Titan Engagement this does nothing unless the procedure under way says so.
  • A move you cannot make: you hold the Position you are placed at.
  • No Gear Dice from an item: the entry is still rolled, with Gear Dice from any other item it allows.
  • A comrade's help to Mount or Dismount. In a Titan Engagement, a comrade in your zone who is not Down spends their action. In a Skirmish, a comrade taking part who is not Down spends their action. Anywhere else, a comrade in the Squad who is not Down and is on the same Expedition as you, or in the Squad when no Expedition is under way, helps and spends nothing. Without that help you do not mount or dismount.
  • Carried. A soldier the both-legs grade forbids to move may be lifted and carried with Lift Comrade, with their consent, and keeps their action for entries that need no move. A Pinned soldier is never lifted.
  • Medical Retirement. A soldier who has lost both arms or both legs may retire, at the player's choice, whatever Prosthetics are fitted. The choice is offered again at the end of every Downtime while the loss stands (see Retirement).

Prosthetics. A Prosthetic is a workshop-made arm or leg (see Prosthetics). It is fitted to one lost side of its kind, at most one per side, during a Downtime once the Critical Injury that lost that side has healed (28 days, or 56 for a Burn). Fitting needs no roll and spends nothing. It stays fitted for as long as you live.

  • Each Prosthetic lowers the grade one step. Count your lost sides of a kind, minus the Prosthetics of that kind fitted. One lost arm with a Prosthetic meets no arm grade. Both arms lost with one Prosthetic meet the one-arm grade.
  • Two never go past the one-limb grade. Both sides of a kind lost, with two Prosthetics of that kind fitted, still meet that kind's one-limb grade.
  • The rows' own penalties stay at every lost side, Prosthetic or not, and stack.
  • A Prosthetic gives no dice to any roll and is never a weapon. It cannot be obtained before the loss at that side has healed. A player character may obtain one for a Squadmate (see Requisition).

Example: two lost legs, one Prosthetic

Tomas has lost both legs, and both Critical Injuries have healed. At the next Downtime he is fitted with one Prosthetic leg. He now meets the one-leg grade: he can Fly and Ride again, and each move on foot also spends his action. Both lost-leg rows keep their penalties, so he still Flies, Rides, dodges, and makes Leap Clear at a 4-die penalty.

Down

A Down soldier can do little more than crawl. You are Down while either is true:

  • Your current Health is 0.
  • You hold an untreated Critical Injury whose row says Down until treated.

Check Down whenever Health lost changes, a Critical Injury is gained or heals, or a Critical Injury becomes treated.

While Down:

  • Your turns still happen and count for every rule that counts turns, such as a Turn limit or a Grab. Each has a move and no action. Your move can make only one zone step on foot, out of a zone that holds a Titan or a Corpse into an adjacent zone that holds none, while you are not Grabbed, carried, or Pinned. It cannot leave the Titan Engagement.
  • You make Death Rolls, and you can be treated, Rallied, and lifted with Lift Comrade.
  • You cannot take any action, Push, Help, Cover, make a Reaction, make any attribute roll but a Death Roll, or make a Fear Roll. A Titan attack you cannot react to lands on 1 or more successes.
  • Your Stress, Stress Responses, Grief, Scars, and a Grabbed state stay as they are, and you can still gain Stress, Critical Injuries, Scars, and Grief.
  • A Grabbed soldier who becomes Down stays Grabbed and cannot take Break Free. A comrade can still free them.

Down and this round. Becoming Down after this round's turn has begun or ended takes any action of that turn you still hold, even if Down ends before the round does. Becoming Down before your turn comes up takes nothing from it. A turn that begins while you are Down has its move, and its action counts as spent, even if Down ends during or after that turn. Ending Down restores nothing already spent.

Ending Down. Down ends at once when your current Health is above 0 and no untreated Down row holds you. Only treating or healing a Critical Injury, a revive, or a day passing can bring that about.

Lethal Critical Injuries and Death Rolls

A lethal Critical Injury has a time limit. Each time it runs out, make a Death Roll. A limit that slows one step moves to the next row of this table.

Time limits, fastest first
LimitRuns out
TurnAt the end of each of your turns in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, starting with your first turn that begins after you gained it. Every turn counts: turns spent in advance, turns spent by a result, a Down soldier's turns, and the turns of a soldier who has left the Titan Engagement while it goes on. When the Titan Engagement or the Skirmish ends, it becomes an Engagement limit.
EngagementOnce, when the Titan Engagement or the Skirmish in which you gained it ends, after its aftermath rolls and before its care window. Only treatment during the fight or a successful aftermath roll prevents that Death Roll.
DayEach time a day passes, after that day's care window. On a Downtime day, after that day's infirmary roll.
StabilizedNever. It is no longer lethal.

Outside both a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish there are no turns. A Turn or Engagement limit gained there runs out once, right after the care window held for that harm. After any Death Roll made outside both, including every Death Roll at the end steps of a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, a Turn or Engagement limit you survived becomes a Day limit. A Death Roll made during a Skirmish, at the end of your turn, reads its row as one made during a Titan Engagement does.

The Death Roll

  • Pool: Strength, plus one Talent that names the Death Roll (such as Hard to Kill), minus the Critical Injury's Death Roll penalty. No Stress Dice, no Help, no Push, no Gear Dice, and no Bonus Dice.
  • Needs: 1 success.
  • 0 successes: you die.
  • 1 success: you live, and the time limit runs out again as it states.
  • 2 or more successes: you live, and the limit slows one step. A Day limit that slows is stabilized.
  • Make one Death Roll for each Critical Injury whose limit runs out. If several run out at once, you choose the order. The first failed roll kills you, and no more are made. A Death Roll is not an action and spends nothing.

Dying. A soldier dies from a failed Death Roll, an instant-death row, or a rule that names death, such as the Grab's devour. A dead soldier leaves play and holds no Position. A dead player character is replaced by promotion or a new character when the procedure in which the death happened ends. A dead Squadmate leaves the Squad Pool. Every Drive that named the dead soldier can never trigger again. The death is a Fear Roll trigger and gives Grief. What happens to their gear is in Gear.

Treat Injury

When you take Treat Injury, roll Wits with your medical kit's Gear Dice, or Wits alone without one. Declare one use and one patient first. It needs 1 success, and a failure does nothing.

  • Revive: the patient is Down at 0 current Health with Health lost above 0. Erase 1 damage mark per success, up to their Health lost. Crossed-off boxes do not come back. If an untreated Down row still holds them, Down goes on, and the roll still counts as your roll.
  • Treat: name one of the patient's untreated Critical Injuries. It becomes treated and gives back its box (unless the patient still holds at least as many untreated Critical Injuries as Health). A lethal one is stabilized: it is no longer lethal and has no time limit.

The players choose which use a roll makes. When damage brought a soldier to 0 and the Critical Injury it gave crossed off a box marked by damage, one success on a revive raises current Health to 1 and ends Down, unless a Down row holds them.

Patients. Any other living soldier, player character or Squadmate. You can treat your own Critical Injury with a 2-die penalty, but you can never revive yourself.

Talents. Field Medicine adds dice. Sure Hands allows a second Push. Careful Nursing shortens healing (see Healing time).

Burn and Pierce

  • A Burn is treated only by a treater who has a medical kit that counts as had, or who spends 1 medical unit of Squad Supply on the roll. Without either, the roll is not made: Wits alone is not allowed. A medical unit is spent on a roll only for the Bonus Die in a care window, so in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish only a kit will do.
  • A Pierce from the 7 row up gives a 1-die penalty to every treat use on it, in a fight, as an aftermath roll, and in a care window. When you treat yourself, it adds to the self-treatment penalty.
  • Neither applies to a revive.

In a Titan Engagement

Treat Injury is an action. The patient must be in your zone. It can be Helped, Pushed, and Covered, and a failure can be tried again on a later turn. Two soldiers who have both left the Titan Engagement count as holding the same Position, for the roll and for Help and Covering on it.

In a Skirmish

Treat Injury is an action with no Position requirement. The patient is any other soldier taking part, or yourself. Up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down may Help, each spending their action, whether Engaged or Apart. It can be Pushed and Covered, and a failure can be tried again on a later turn (see Skirmishes).

Care windows

Outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, Treat Injury is rolled only in a care window or as an aftermath roll. Each care window has a scope: the soldiers who take part in it. A care window is held:

  • When a Titan Engagement ends, after its Death Rolls. Scope: every soldier in the Squad who held a Position at any point during it.
  • When a Skirmish ends, after its Death Rolls. Scope: every soldier who took part.
  • After an event outside both a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish that makes a soldier Down or gives a lethal Critical Injury with a Turn or Engagement limit. One window is held per event, however many soldiers it harms, as soon as the event is resolved unless its rule names a later point. The Death Rolls follow it. Scope: the soldiers that rule names as taking part; if it names none, every soldier in the Squad on the same Expedition as a harmed soldier, or every soldier in the Squad when no Expedition is under way. Rolls in it may only treat a Critical Injury the event gave, or revive a soldier the event made Down. Harm during a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish never holds this window.
  • Each time a day passes, before Day limits run out. At a Night Camp, scope: every soldier on the Expedition. On the interim day, scope: every soldier in the Squad. No window is held on the days that pass in Downtime.
  • Once in each Downtime, at the infirmary, before its seven days pass. Scope: every soldier in the Squad.

In a care window:

  • The patient must be a living soldier in the scope.
  • Every living soldier in the scope who is not Down may make one Treat Injury roll, for any patient in the scope. There is no Position requirement.
  • A living soldier in the scope who is not Down, is not rolling, and has not yet made or Helped a roll in this window may Help. Help is declared before the roll and spends their roll for the window. At most 3 soldiers Help one roll.
  • Anyone who qualifies to Help a roll can Cover its Push, even after rolling or Helping. Covering spends nothing.
  • Before rolling, you may spend 1 medical unit of Squad Supply for 1 Bonus Die, 1 unit per roll. That spend also lets you treat a Burn.
  • Players choose the order of rolls, with the roll-off if they disagree. Squadmates take part.
  • A failed roll can be tried again only in a later care window.
  • A medical kit can be restocked in every care window (see Squad Supply). Field Repair is rolled in the windows at the end of a Titan Engagement, at a Night Camp, and on the interim day (see Field Repair).

Aftermath rolls

When a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish ends, each dying soldier gets one chance before the end-of-fight Death Rolls (see When a Titan Engagement ends).

  • Patient: a living soldier with an untreated lethal Critical Injury with an Engagement limit, including a Turn limit that has just become one. The patient may be Down.
  • One roll per patient, by a treater the players choose: a living soldier who is not Down and who was in the patient's zone when the fight ended. Or the patient, if not Down, treating themselves with the 2-die penalty.
  • Zones at the end. Compare the zones the soldiers were in as the fight ended. Two soldiers who had both left count as holding the same Position, and a soldier who had left is never at or one step from a soldier who still held a Position.
  • In a Skirmish there are no Positions: every soldier who took part counts as holding the patient's Position.
  • One roll per treater.
  • The roll treats one such Critical Injury, chosen by the players. On a success it is treated, stabilized, and gives back its box, which can end Down before the Death Rolls. A failure uses up the patient's attempt: no other treater, and no other Critical Injury of theirs, can reopen it.
  • No Help and no Cover. It can be Pushed, and Sure Hands allows a second Push. It does not use up the treater's roll in the care window that follows.
  • Players choose the order of rolls, with the roll-off if they disagree.

Healing time

Healing is counted in days. No day passes inside a Titan Engagement. A day passes:

  • On an Expedition, at each Night Camp. Its care window has every soldier on the Expedition in scope, and Field Repair is rolled in it.
  • In Downtime, seven times at the infirmary Playtest rule. Each of those days has an infirmary roll before its Death Rolls and no care window of its own (see Downtime).
  • On the interim day Playtest rule. While no Expedition and no Downtime is under way, one day passes at the start of each session, before the interim issue. If a session begins partway through a procedure, the day passes once that procedure ends. Stress and Grief do not change on this day. Its care window includes every soldier in the Squad, and Field Repair is rolled in it. The interim day never happens while an Expedition or a Downtime is under way.

Each time a day passes

  1. Hold a care window.
  2. Every lethal Critical Injury with a Day limit causes a Death Roll.
  3. Every living soldier erases every damage mark. Crossed-off boxes stay.
  4. Every held Critical Injury's healing time drops by 1 day. One that reaches 0 heals. An untreated Pierce from the 7 row up does not drop.

On a Downtime day, the infirmary's steps take the place of these (see Downtime).

  • A lethal Critical Injury cannot heal. Its healing time stops at 1 day until it is stabilized.
  • An untreated Pierce from the 7 row up keeps its healing time. It heals only after a Treat Injury roll has succeeded on it, and until then it keeps its effects and its crossed-off box. A lethal one that Death Rolls stabilize is still untreated, so it still does not heal.
  • A Burn takes double its row's healing days.
  • When one heals, you stop holding it. Its effects end and, if it was untreated, it gives back its box (unless you still hold at least as many untreated Critical Injuries as Health). Permanent effects stay, and a healed Critical Injury with permanent effects keeps counting toward worsening at its Injury Location and side. Any other healed Critical Injury stops counting.
  • Careful Nursing. When a Treat Injury roll by a soldier with Careful Nursing succeeds on a comrade's Critical Injury, its remaining healing time is halved, rounding up. Each Critical Injury is halved only once, and the sheet records it. It never applies to the soldier's own Critical Injury.

Resolve

Resolve is your steadiness: half of Instinct plus Empathy, rounded up, plus 1 per Scar, minus 1 per point of Grief. It counts against both mind rolls. A Stress Response and a Fear Roll are each D6 + Stress − Resolve.

Resolve has no floor. With enough Grief it falls to 0 or below, which raises both totals.

  • Iron Nerve counts your Resolve as 1 higher on your own Stress Response roll.
  • Unshaken Command lets the Fear Roll of a comrade in your zone or an adjacent zone use your Resolve in place of theirs, if yours is higher and you are not Down, once per Titan Engagement (see Talents).

Effects that change rolls, turns, and Reactions

Every Critical Injury, Stress Response, Fear Roll, and Scar row names its effects with the words below. Each has exactly one reading.

Dice, successes, and Stress

  • 1-die penalty on named actions. Every roll you make for a listed action loses that many base dice. Penalties add up. They never take your base dice below 1 and never remove Gear Dice or Stress Dice.
  • 1-die penalty on the next roll. Your next attribute roll, other than a Death Roll, loses that many base dice. The penalty is then used up.
  • Gain Stress. You gain that much Stress once each time the row takes effect: a Critical Injury when you record it, a Stress Response or Fear Roll result when it applies, a Scar each time its trigger happens. Holding a Critical Injury gains nothing more.
  • Extra Stress on every Push. Each time you Push, you gain that much Stress on top of the Push's own, whether or not the Push is Covered. It adds no Stress Die. A Squadmate never Pushes, so it does nothing for one.
  • Lose successes. The roll that caused the result loses that many successes, to a minimum of 0, as soon as its successes are counted. The reduced count is the roll's count for every later use. So a dodge that loses a success cancels one fewer success of every later attack from that Titan this round.
  • The roll fails. The roll that caused the result has 0 successes.
  • Fear Roll total raised. The named Fear Roll's total rises by that amount before you read the row.
  • Gain a Scar. See Scars.
  • Actions you cannot take. You cannot take, make, or use a listed action. A roll a rule calls for under it is not made, and whatever needs that roll cannot be done. A Talent on it does nothing for you. It removes no die from any other roll. Lost limbs use it (see Lost limbs).

Turns and Reactions

  • Next turn spent spends your earliest turn, starting with this round's, whose move and action are both unspent, exactly as a Reaction does (see Turns). "Next 2 turns spent" then spends the earliest such turn after that one. A spent turn still happens and counts as a turn for every rule that counts turns, but it begins its round with its move and action spent, so you cannot Help that round.
  • Next action spent spends the action of your earliest turn, starting with this round's, whose action is unspent. That turn keeps its move. You cannot Help that round or make a Reaction with that turn.
  • No Reactions. You cannot make a Reaction from when the result takes effect until the end of your first turn that begins after that. On a row that spends two turns, the ban lasts until the end of the second such turn. Every turn counts, including a turn spent in advance. Which turn the row spends does not change how long the ban lasts. The ban ends when the Titan Engagement ends.

A card against a soldier who cannot react. While you cannot make a Reaction, a Titan's card against you lands on 1 or more Titan successes. A card that scores 0 successes whiffs and spares you.

Fear Roll results

Six effects appear only on Fear Roll rows. An effect that acts on a Titan acts on the event's Titan, which Fear Rolls defines. The GM names one only for a Fear Roll the GM calls outside a Titan Engagement, and never in a fight.

  • Marked as loud. You are marked as loud against the event's Titan, exactly as Draw Attention marks you, until the end of that Titan's next card that resolves a behavior. If that Titan is not in your zone, nothing happens.
  • Comrades nearby gain Stress. Every comrade, player character or Squadmate, in your zone or an adjacent zone gains that much Stress once. A comrade who has left the Titan Engagement never counts. It is Stress, not a roll, and causes no Fear Roll. It lands with the event's other results, after every rolling soldier's total has been found, so it counts from that comrade's next roll.
  • A forced step toward Distant. At the start of your next turn, before anything else in it, you make one step toward Distant, by the first option of a retreat: from On Body or Blind Spot, an attachment step to free, and from free in a zone that holds a Titan or a Corpse, a zone step into an adjacent zone one ring further out. The step happens even when that turn was spent in advance, because it is a result, not your move. It spends nothing and never meets a Drive's own-move trigger. It is a step of the kind your move makes, on foot, mounted, or ODM, and it is never a Flight: a Flight is your own ODM move, and a forced step is not one. So it is not rolled, it pays no Carry, it gives no Momentum, and it sets no loud mark, whichever kind of step it is. Momentum is the reward for choosing to fly, and a soldier flung back by fear has chosen nothing. An ODM forced step is still ODM use, so it makes that round's Gas Roll, and you are airborne after it as after any ODM move. No step is made if you are Down, Grabbed, Pinned, or carried, have left the Titan Engagement, are already free in a zone that holds no body Playtest rule, or have no step that qualifies. It never makes you leave the Titan Engagement or let go. Until it is made, Rally can clear it.
  • A forced strike. Your next action must be a Nape strike or a Body Part strike against the event's Titan, with every requirement those actions have. Until you make it, you take no other action except Swap Blade Set, so you can refit and strike, and you cannot Help or Cover. If the strike falls short and you are allowed to Push it, you must Push it. Your moves are your own.
    • It waits while you are Grabbed, Down, or Pinned. If the event's Titan's body pins your limb, your Body Part strike against the Body Part that pins you is the forced strike.
    • A strike whose requirements you do not meet does not end it.
    • It ends only when you make the strike, whatever its result; when Rally clears it; when the event's Titan dies; or when the Titan Engagement ends. The ban on Help and Covering lasts exactly as long.
  • Drop the Blade Set. The Blade Set in your handles falls, and your handles are empty. If they were already empty, nothing happens. The set lies in your zone. No one takes it during the Titan Engagement and no rule moves it. When the Titan Engagement ends, it is shared out with the left items (see Gear). Refit from a carried Blade Set with Swap Blade Set, which spends your move in a Titan Engagement and can be made on a turn spent in advance.
  • A Gas Roll at once. If a canister with a Gas Rating above 0 is fitted to your ODM Gear, make a Gas Roll on it at once: two dice, whatever you have Pushed this round. Each 1 lowers the Gas Rating by 1, to a minimum of 0. It is not the round's Gas Roll and not ODM use, so it neither replaces nor adds to the round's own Gas Roll. With no canister fitted, or a Gas Rating of 0, nothing happens.

Pinned

Pinned is a state, not a table row. The full rule is in Pinned.

  • A limb-pinned soldier cannot Cover or make a Reaction. They keep their action, but only for the actions the Pinned list allows.
  • A body-pinned soldier cannot Help, Cover, or make a Reaction, and has no action.
  • A Titan's card lands on a Pinned soldier as on any soldier who cannot make a Reaction.
  • Rolls a rule calls for without your choosing them, such as a Death Roll, a Fear Roll, or a Gas Roll, are never forbidden.

Outside a Titan Engagement

Outside a Titan Engagement there are no turns or Reactions. Every effect above applies there except these:

Effects outside a Titan Engagement
EffectOutside a Titan Engagement
Next turn spent, next action spent, No ReactionsNothing, unless the rule that called for the roll says what they do.
Marked as loud, a forced stepNothing.
Comrades nearby gain StressIn a Skirmish, every other soldier in it gains the Stress. Anywhere else, nothing.
A forced strikeIn a Skirmish, your next action must be a Fight or a Shoot against a Foe, with the same Push, Help, and Covering rules, until you take it, Rally clears it, or the Skirmish ends. Anywhere else, nothing.
Drop the Blade SetApplies. The set is shared out when the procedure in which you made the Fear Roll ends.

What no row does. No row damages or wears gear. Only the forced strike row forbids Help and Covering; the Down state forbids both on its own. No row causes a Fear Roll, or any roll by a comrade.

Stress Responses

When a Stress Die shows 1 on an attribute roll, or after you Push it, you suffer a Stress Response. A roll never causes more than one. Resolve it once the roll's successes are counted and before the roll takes effect.

Roll D6 + Stress − Resolve, using your Stress after any Push and your current Resolve, and find the row. It is not a dice pool: no Push, no Help, no Stress Dice. Each row's name and "What happens" line are fiction; its Duration and Effects are the rules.

A roll whose own rule names another resolution for its Stress Response, such as a Graduation Exam Trial, still has the Stress Response and still cannot be Pushed after it, but does not use this table.

Stress Responses (D6 + Stress − Resolve)
TotalStress ResponseDurationEffectsWhat happens
0 or lessDeep BreathinstantnoneYou breathe out through your teeth, and the moment passes.
1Scattered Thoughtslasting1-die penalty on Read, Treat Injury, Field RepairEvery plan you start falls apart before you finish thinking it.
2Tunnel Visionlasting1-die penalty on Break Attention, RallyThe edges of the world go grey. You see the danger in front of you and nothing beside it.
3Blade Chatterlasting1-die penalty on Nape strike, Body Part strike, Break FreeYour hands will not stop shaking, and the grip you drilled for three years is gone.
4Leaden Legslasting1-die penalty on Fly, Ride, DodgeYour body answers a heartbeat late, as if your legs belong to someone else.
5Wound Tightlasting1 extra Stress on every PushEverything in you is pulled taut. Each time you reach for more, something tears a little.
6Flinchinstantlose 1 successSomething moves at the edge of your sight, and your whole body jerks away from it.
7Seized Upinstantlose 1 success; next turn spentEvery muscle locks at once. For a long moment you can only watch.
8 or moreEverything Slipsinstantthe roll fails; gain 1 StressYour grip, your footing, and your nerve all give way in the same instant.
  • An instant result applies once, to the roll that caused it.
  • A lasting result is yours to hold. It applies from your next roll on, never to the roll that caused it. You can hold several different ones.
  • Already held. If you roll a lasting result you already hold, use the next row down instead. Repeat while that row is also one you hold.
  • A Down soldier keeps the lasting results they hold.

When a lasting result ends

  • When Rally clears it.
  • When the Titan Engagement in which you gained it ends. A result gained at the end steps, such as on a care window roll, was gained outside it.
  • For one gained outside a Titan Engagement, when the rule that called for the roll says. If that rule says nothing, it ends when a day passes (see Healing time).

Your sheet records each lasting result and which of these ends it.

Rally

When you Rally, roll Empathy for a comrade, player character or Squadmate, who holds at least one lasting Stress Response or a pending Fear result: a forced step or forced strike not yet made. You cannot Rally yourself. The target may be Down.

Rally needs 1 success. Each success clears one of the target's lasting Stress Responses or pending Fear results, of your choice. On a failure nothing happens. Rally never lowers Stress.

  • In a Titan Engagement, Rally is an action. The target must be in your zone or an adjacent zone; Carrying Voice adds one more zone. It can be Helped, and a failed Rally can be tried again on a later turn.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement, any soldier who is not Down may Rally a comrade, with no Help and no Covering. You can Rally a given comrade once, and again only after they gain another lasting Stress Response or pending Fear result. The sheet records who has Rallied them.
  • Two soldiers who have both left a Titan Engagement that is still under way count as holding the same Position, including for Help and Covering on the roll.
  • Steady Voice adds dice.

Fear Rolls

A Fear Roll is never caused by the dice. You make one only when one of these events happens:

Fear Roll triggers
EventWho rolls
Your first Titan EngagementYou, when you first hold a Position in one
An Abnormal is a Focus Titan when a Titan Engagement starts, or becomes one during itEvery soldier who holds a Position in it
A Background Titan enters as a second Focus TitanEvery soldier who holds a Position in it
A comrade becomes GrabbedEvery witness, never the Grabbed soldier
A comrade diesEvery witness, including a Grabbed one. In a Skirmish, every other soldier taking part who is alive
Your first human kill: Cut, Pierce, or Burn damage you deal brings a Foe to 0 HealthYou, when the kill resolves
Outside a Titan Engagement, an event the GM judges at least as horrifying as a listed trigger, such as a village found devoured, a comrade's body found days after, or a Titan seen from the Wall tearing through a breachThe witnesses the GM names
  • A witness is every soldier who, once the event is fully resolved, is alive and holds a Position in the Titan Engagement, other than the Grabbed or dead soldier. A soldier who has left the Titan Engagement is not a witness.
  • First times are marked. Your sheet marks that you have faced a Titan when you first hold a Position in a Titan Engagement, and that you have killed a person when your first human kill resolves. Each counts even if you make no Fear Roll, for example because you are Down or your Drive shrugs it off. Neither trigger applies to you again.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement, a comrade Grabbed or killed causes Fear Rolls only where the rule for that scene says who rolls, as the Skirmish does.
  • The GM's trigger. It is the one trigger the GM reads, and the GM calls it rarely. The event is at least as horrifying as a listed trigger. It is never called inside a Titan Engagement, where only the listed triggers call Fear Rolls, and never for an event a listed trigger covers, such as a comrade's death. It is never called for an outcome a called roll's stakes named, since a stake is never a Fear Roll; a listed trigger that outcome later meets still applies. Its roll is every Fear Roll's: the same table, the limits below, and your Drive's shrug-off.

Limits

  • One Fear Roll per soldier per event, even if the event matches several triggers, such as a first Titan Engagement against an Abnormal.
  • A Down soldier makes no Fear Roll.
  • A death at the end steps of a Titan Engagement causes no Fear Roll.
  • One snapshot per event. Fear Rolls are made as soon as the event is fully resolved. Everyone who rolls for one event rolls together, and each total uses that soldier's Stress, Resolve, and Scars as they stood when the event was resolved. First find every rolling soldier's row. Then each player makes their Drive decision. Then every result that was not shrugged off applies. No result of the event, such as Stress to comrades nearby, a Scar, or a spent action, changes another soldier's total for it, so the order of the rolls changes nothing. Grief from a death waits until every Fear Roll it causes is resolved.

The roll

Roll D6 + Stress − Resolve and find the row. It is not a dice pool: no Push, no Help, no Stress Dice, and no Stress Response. A Squadmate makes Fear Rolls like a player character. Unshaken Command and two Scars (Cannot Look Away, Old Nightmare) can change the total; Unshaken Command reads the comrade's Resolve at the same snapshot.

Each row's name and "What happens" line are fiction; its Effects and Forbids are the rules. The effects are read as written above.

Fear Rolls (D6 + Stress − Resolve)
TotalResultEffectsForbidsWhat happens
0 or lessSteelednonenoneYou breathe out slowly. Your hands stay where they belong.
1Heart in Your Throatgain 1 StressnoneYour pulse drowns out the wind. You swallow, and it will not go down.
2Cold Sweatgain 1 Stress; 1-die penalty on the next rollnoneSweat runs into your eyes, and everything in your hands turns slick.
3Clenched Triggernext action spent; a Gas Roll at oncenoneYour hand clamps down, and gas roars out of your gear for nothing. It takes a moment to make your fingers let go.
4Retchingnext action spent; gain 1 StressnoneThe smell reaches you before the sight does, blood and steam and worse. You double over and heave.
5Someone Is Shouting Your Namenext turn spentnoneSomeone is shouting your name from very far away. Your body does not answer.
6Screamnext turn spent; No Reactions; marked as loud against the event's Titan, only in its zoneReactionsThe scream is out of you before you know it is yours. Somewhere above you, a huge head turns.
7Run for the Wallnext turn spent; No Reactions; a forced step toward Distant at the start of your next turn; gain a ScarReactionsEvery part of you turns toward home and goes. Behind you, someone is still fighting.
8Kill Itnext 2 turns spent; No Reactions; a forced strike on the event's Titan, Pushed if short; gain a ScarReactions, Help, CoveringThe world goes quiet and red. When it comes back, you are already going for it.
9 or moreNothing Leftnext 2 turns spent; No Reactions; drop the Blade Set in your handles; comrades in your zone or an adjacent zone gain 1 Stress; gain a ScarReactionsYou open your hands and let the blades fall. Everyone near you sees it in your face.
  • No Reactions under cancellation. From 6 up, you cannot react until the ban ends. A Titan's card against you then lands on 1 or more Titan successes, with nothing to cancel them. A card that scores 0 successes whiffs and spares you.
  • A forced step on a spent turn. Run for the Wall spends your next turn, and its step still happens at the start of that turn, because the step is a result, not your move.
  • Kill It. Its ban on Help and Covering lasts until the forced strike ends. Its ban on Reactions lasts as No Reactions does.

The event's Titan

  • An Abnormal: the Abnormal.
  • A second Focus Titan: the Titan that entered.
  • A comrade Grabbed: the Focus Titan that holds the comrade.
  • A comrade dies: the Focus Titan whose card or Grab killed them.
  • Your first Titan Engagement, or a death no card or Grab caused (such as a Death Roll): the nearest Focus Titan. That is the living Focus Titan you hold the closest Position to, in the order On Body, Blind Spot, In Reach, Distant; on a tie, the one with the earliest label.
  • A Fear Roll the GM calls: none, or the nearest Titan in sight if the GM names one. The event is outside a Titan Engagement, so each effect does what Outside a Titan Engagement gives.

If the event's Titan has died when the result applies, the nearest Focus Titan takes its place. If no Focus Titan is alive, those effects do nothing.

Drives and the result

A Fear Roll's result is every effect on its row: any Stress gain, to you or to comrades nearby, spent action or turn, ban on Reactions, loud mark, forced step, forced strike, dropped Blade Set, Gas Roll, and Scar. If your Drive shrugs off the result, none of those effects happen, including the Stress to comrades. The Fear Roll still counts as made. A Squadmate has no Drive.

Example: Run for the Wall

A Titan Grabs Private Brandt. Private Hale stands on the Ground in the Titan's zone, the centre, and is a witness. He has Stress 4, and his Resolve is 2 after 1 Grief. His turn this round has already come up and used its move and action.

Hale rolls D6 and shows 5: 5 + 4 − 2 = 7, Run for the Wall. His Drive's trigger is not met. The row takes effect:

  • This round's turn has parts spent, so the result spends next round's turn.
  • He cannot make a Reaction until the end of next round's turn.
  • He gains a Scar at once. His minimum Stress and his Resolve each rise by 1.

Next round, before Hale's card comes up, the Titan's card resolves an attack on him. It rolls its 6 Attack Dice and scores Severity 0: a whiff, and Hale is spared. Had it scored 1 or more, the attack would have landed with nothing to cancel it. Then Hale's spent turn comes up. His forced step still happens at its start: he is free in the Titan's zone, so he steps into an adjacent zone one ring further out and holds Distant. A comrade's Rally before that turn could have cleared the step.

Scars

You gain a Scar only when:

  • a Fear Roll row with a Scar takes effect (not one your Drive shrugs off); or
  • a lethal Critical Injury you hold is stabilized while you live, by a Treat Injury success or by a Death Roll that slows a Day limit. Each Critical Injury gives this Scar at most once.

Gaining a Scar

  1. Roll D66 on the Scar table. If you already hold that Scar, roll again. A Squadmate also rolls again on a row marked yes.
  2. Record it. No rule removes a Scar.
  3. Your minimum Stress rises by 1. If your Stress is below the new minimum, it rises to it at once.
  4. Your Resolve rises by 1.
  5. At five Scars you must retire (see Retirement).

You never hold more than five Scars. A Scar gained at five does nothing.

Every Scar raises your minimum Stress and Resolve by 1, so a veteran always rolls Stress Dice and resists Stress Responses and Fear Rolls better. Each Scar also names a trigger and an effect: a trauma that bites in one kind of moment, for as long as you live.

Scars (D66)

Retirement

At five Scars you must retire.

Medical Retirement. When you have lost both arms or both legs (see Lost limbs), you may retire, at your choice, whatever Prosthetics are fitted, since the loss stands.

  • The choice is offered when the procedure in which you lost the second limb ends, or at once if none is under way.
  • It is offered again at the end of every Downtime while the loss stands. Declining is never final.
  • The players choose for a Squadmate as one table, with the roll-off if they disagree.
  • If you choose it, mark your sheet as retiring. You retire when a fifth Scar gained at that moment would retire you, with the same effect.

The alternative is a Prosthetic. Fitting one does not end the offer, since the loss still stands.

When you retire. Retirement never interrupts a procedure. Until it happens, you follow every rule as normal and your sheet is marked as retiring.

  • A fifth Scar gained during a Titan Engagement, or at any of its end steps, retires you at the last end step.
  • One gained while a day passes retires you after that day's last step.
  • One gained in a care window for harm outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish retires you after that window and any Death Rolls that follow it.
  • Any other fifth Scar retires you when the procedure in which you gained it ends, as that procedure's rule states. If no procedure is under way, you retire at once.

What happens. You leave play and become an NPC instructor or Squad contact; in this playtest, instructors and contacts have no rules effect. Your gear leaves play with you. A retiring player character is replaced by promotion or a new character. A retiring Squadmate leaves the Squad Pool. Every Drive that named you can never trigger again. Retirement gives no Grief and causes no Fear Roll.

Grief

Grief is the weight you carry after a comrade dies. Each point lowers your Resolve by 1, with no floor. Grief has no other effect.

  • Who gains it. A death during a Titan Engagement, including at its end steps, gives Grief to every other living soldier in the Squad who held a Position at any point during it. A death during a Skirmish, including at its end steps, gives Grief to every other living soldier who took part. Any other death gives Grief to every other living soldier in the Squad. Squadmates gain Grief like player characters.
  • How much. All the deaths of one Titan Engagement give each soldier 1 Grief in total, however many comrades died. So do the deaths of one Skirmish, the deaths of one day passing, and the deaths of one event outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish together with the Death Rolls after its care window. Any other death gives 1.
  • On top of that, you gain 1 more for each dead soldier your Drive named, and 1 more for each death if you hold Numb. These go only to a soldier who gains Grief from that death.
  • When. For a death during a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, at step 8 of its end steps, so Grief never changes Resolve during the fight in which the comrade died. For any other death, right after every Fear Roll it causes is resolved, including each Drive decision, or at once if it causes none. A death's Grief never applies before its own Fear Rolls.
  • Limit. You hold at most 3 Grief. Grief beyond 3 is lost.
  • Losing Grief. Nothing in the field lowers Grief. In Downtime, Visit Haven lowers yours by 2, Honoring the Fallen every soldier's by 1, and the Squadmate relief each Squadmate's by 1, never below 0.
  • Retirement, promotion, and a Squadmate leaving the Squad Pool give no Grief.

Stress in the field

Your Stress changes only through these rows. Stress has no maximum.

Stress gains
