A table row or Talent names a lossAs it statesAs it states

Minimum. Your Stress never drops below your number of Scars, and it rises to a new minimum at once. Rally clears a lasting Stress Response but never lowers Stress.

When a Titan Engagement ends

A Titan Engagement ends only by the tests in Leaving and the end of the fight. If it ends during a round, make that round's Gas Rolls at once. Then resolve these steps in order:

The end steps

  1. Turns. Cancel every turn spent in advance that has not come up, and every turn and action a result would have spent. Every ban on Reactions ends. Every pending Fear result, a forced step or forced strike not yet made, is cancelled.
  2. Stress. Every soldier who held a Position at any point loses 1 Stress. A Nape kill that ended the Titan Engagement already gave its own loss when it happened.
  3. Stress Responses. End every lasting Stress Response gained during the Titan Engagement.
  4. Time limits. Every Turn limit becomes an Engagement limit.
  5. Aftermath rolls. Each patient with an untreated lethal Critical Injury with an Engagement limit gets at most one aftermath roll, from a treater the players choose, and each soldier makes at most one (see Treat Injury). A success gives back the Critical Injury's box as treating does and can end Down.
  6. Death Rolls. Every lethal Critical Injury that still has an Engagement limit runs out and causes a Death Roll. These are made outside the Titan Engagement, so a limit you survive becomes a Day limit. A death here causes no Fear Roll.
  7. Care window. Hold a care window. A lethal Critical Injury stabilized here or at step 5 gives its Scar.
  8. Grief. Every soldier gains Grief for the deaths during the Titan Engagement, including deaths at step 6.
  9. Retirement, gear, and promotion. Every soldier with five Scars retires, including one who gained the fifth at an earlier step, and a retiring Squadmate leaves the Squad Pool. Left items, and every Blade Set a Fear Roll result dropped, are shared out (see Gear). Then promotion replaces, all together, every player character who died or retired during the Titan Engagement or at any end step.

The steps are resolved outside the Titan Engagement: a roll made at them is not made in a Titan Engagement. No soldier retires, and no promotion happens, before step 9.

Pinned soldiers

  • The end waits while anyone is Pinned. A Titan Engagement does not end because no Focus Titan is alive while a soldier lies Pinned. The rounds go on until the last Pinned soldier is freed or dies.
  • The end frees no one. While a soldier is Pinned, only "no soldier standing" can end the Titan Engagement. A body-pinned soldier is not standing; a limb-pinned soldier who is not Down is.
  • Left under the body. Every soldier still Pinned when the Titan Engagement ends dies under the body, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive. They are Down first. Their deaths have no witnesses and cause no Fear Roll, count as deaths during the Titan Engagement for Grief, and their left items leave play.

Positions at the end

A step that reads a Position, such as the Stress loss, a care window, or Grief, reads the Positions each soldier's zone and attachment give as the Titan Engagement ends: relative to each living Focus Titan, and relative to each Corpse, which lies in the zone its Titan died in. A step that compares two soldiers reads their zones. The field, with every zone and attachment on it, is cleared only after step 9.

Soldiers who left before the end

A living soldier who left the Titan Engagement while it went on holds no Position in it, but is still in it for these rules until it ends:

  • They still take a turn each round. Each of those turns counts for every rule that counts turns, so a Turn limit keeps running out, and a result can spend the turn.
  • For Treat Injury and Rally, Help and Covering on those rolls, and aftermath rolls, two soldiers who have both left count as holding the same Position. A soldier who has left never counts as holding the same Position as, or one step from, a soldier who still holds one.
  • Every end step applies to them.

The end of a Skirmish

A Skirmish ends with the same steps, with "Skirmish" in place of "Titan Engagement". Every soldier who took part stands in for every soldier who held a Position. Its Stress loss is the end of a Skirmish's 1, and it has no Nape kill. For aftermath rolls, every soldier who took part counts as holding the patient's Position, and the care window covers every soldier who took part.

Gear

"Count your canisters before you count your friends. Your friends will still be there when you land."Quartermaster, Trost district depot

This chapter covers what you take into the field and what using it costs: gear items and their Gear Dice, ODM Gear and gas, Blade Sets, horses, falls, carrying, Field Repair, Standard Issue, Squad Supply, Kept Items, firearms, and Prosthetics.

Gear items and Gear Dice

These are the gear items: ODM Gear, gas canisters, Blade Sets, horses, medical kits, tool kits, flintlock pistols, muskets, and Prosthetic arms and legs. Nothing else is a gear item. A rope or a lantern you describe adds no Gear Dice and counts as no item. A thrown cloak is a decoy for Break Attention. Any other object used well may count toward the Circumstances the GM names for a roll, so a rope and a grapnel might make a climb Easy, but it never gives Gear Dice. On a called roll made with an attribute alone, a gear item that helps counts the same way and gives no Gear Dice.

Ratings

  • Every item except a gas canister and a Prosthetic has a rating from 1 to 3, set when you receive it, and a current rating from 0 up to that rating.
  • An item's Gear Dice equal its current rating. A roll takes Gear Dice from one item at most; if more than one qualifies, choose before you roll.
  • The rating never changes. Only the current rating goes down and up.

Wear

  • When a Pushed roll is final, an item whose Gear Dice show one or more 1s wears by 1 point, one point per Pushed roll, however many of its Gear Dice show 1. A roll that was not Pushed never wears anything. No ruling wears an item: a called roll's stakes may lose a carried item, never a point of wear.
  • A Talent that ignores wear, such as Well-Kept Rig, Blade Discipline, or Sure Seat, removes points before they apply.
  • Each point left lowers the current rating by 1, never below 0. A Blade Set is the exception: any wear ruins it (see Blade Sets).

At 0, and counting as not had

At current rating 0, ODM Gear is Jammed, a horse is lame, a medical kit is spent, and a tool kit or a firearm is worn out. An item at 0 adds no Gear Dice and counts as not had for every action that needs it.

These also count as not had:

  • ODM Gear while your Gas Rating is 0. This never applies to the Graduation Exam's training ODM Gear, which has no canister.
  • A Blade Set that is not in your handles.
  • Your horse, for the dodge, Ride, and Leap Clear, while you are not mounted on it; and for Break Attention, while you are neither mounted on it nor in its zone.
  • A flintlock pistol or musket that is empty (see Firearms).

Restoring

A current rating rises only through Field Repair, restocking a medical kit, Standard Issue replacing the item, or the Maintain Gear Downtime Action (see Downtime), and never above the rating. Standard Issue never restores a firearm.

ODM Gear

Your ODM Gear supplies Gear Dice to four rolls: the dodge, Break Attention, Fly, and Leap Clear. A Nape strike, a Body Part strike, and Break Free take their Gear Dice from the Blade Set in your handles, never from ODM Gear.

Strikes need working ODM Gear

In a Titan Engagement, a Nape strike, and a Body Part strike made from On Body or Blind Spot, need your ODM Gear to work: not Jammed, and with a Gas Rating above 0. While you are Jammed or have run dry you cannot make them. The strike still takes its Gear Dice from your Blade Set, and it is not ODM use, so it makes no Gas Roll. Only a rule that names this requirement lifts it, such as a Titan grounded by a Broken leg. Outside a Titan Engagement the requirement does not apply.

ODM moves

  • An ODM move is a move you make on your own ODM Gear. The steps table in Fighting Titans shows which steps need one, and every ODM move in a Titan Engagement is a Flight.
  • In a Titan Engagement every ODM move is a Flight: you roll Fly with your ODM Gear, the step happens whatever the roll gives, each success is 1 Momentum, and no successes marks you as loud.
  • An ODM move needs working ODM Gear, so you cannot make one while Jammed or dry.
  • If you are mounted, an ODM move dismounts you before its first step.
  • While you are Overloaded, an ODM move also spends your action, and you cannot make one once your action is spent.
  • A soldier being carried makes no ODM move.

Using ODM Gear

You use ODM Gear in a round if, during that round, you make an ODM move, make a roll whose gear item is your ODM Gear (such as a dodge or a Fly roll made with it), or take an act that Fighting Titans names as ODM use. A Flight is both at once, and a soldier who used ODM Gear makes one Gas Roll for the round however many times they used it.

Holding a Position without moving is not ODM use, even while airborne. Neither is a roll made with a Blade Set, a horse, or a kit (every Nape strike included), nor a mounted charge, nor being carried. Outside a Titan Engagement there are no rounds, and ODM use makes a Gas Roll only when the rule that calls for it says so. Travel on horseback never spends gas, and the Graduation Exam makes no Gas Rolls.

Airborne

You are airborne while you are in the air on your own ODM Gear. Your sheet records it.

  • You become airborne when you make an ODM move, or when a rule calls for a Fly roll with your ODM Gear and says the roll makes you airborne. If you are mounted, you are dismounted first, with no fall.
  • You stop being airborne when you make a move that is not an ODM move, fall, become Grabbed, the zone you are Anchored in becomes Open (you land with no fall), or the Titan Engagement ends (everyone airborne lands with no fall). Outside a Titan Engagement, a rule that made you airborne lands you when its procedure ends, unless it names another point.
  • You are never airborne while carried by a comrade, while Grabbed, or while mounted. In an Urban zone you are not airborne while you hold Blind Spot either: you stand anchored to a roof (see Terrain Traits).
  • Running out of gas while airborne leaves you airborne.

The Freedom Drive's trigger is met while you are airborne under this definition, in or outside a Titan Engagement.

Jam

When your ODM Gear reaches current rating 0, it Jams

  1. It counts as not had for every action and adds no Gear Dice.
  2. You cannot make an ODM move while it is Jammed, so you make no Gas Roll for any later round. The Gas Roll for the round in which it Jammed is still made.
  3. Your Gas Rating does not change.
  4. You cannot make a Nape strike, or a Body Part strike from On Body or Blind Spot, unless the Titan is grounded (see A grounded Titan).
  5. If you are airborne, you fall once the roll that Jammed it is finished. In an Urban zone a soldier who holds Blind Spot is anchored to a roof and is not airborne, so a Jam does not drop them (see Terrain Traits).

A Jam ends when the current rating rises above 0: through Field Repair, through Maintain Gear, or through Standard Issue replacing the Jammed ODM Gear. The interim issue replaces it only if it is rated below the Funding row, and you may decline the exchange.

A Titan's Attack Dice never wear your gear. Only your own Pushed roll does, so a Jam comes from the dodge, Fly, Break Attention, or Leap Clear you Push with your ODM Gear.

Example: a Jam at On Body

Jonas is airborne at On Body on a Medium Titan, in a Wooded zone. His ODM Gear is rated 3 but worn to 1, and his Stress is 1. A behavior targets him. The Titan rolls its Attack Dice and scores Severity 2.

Jonas dodges with his ODM Gear: Agility 3, 1 Gear Die, and 1 Stress Die. The base dice show 6, 2, and 4, the Gear Die shows 1, and the Stress Die shows 3. That is one success. No Stress Die shows 1, so he Pushes, and his Stress rises to 2. The re-rolled base dice show 6 and 5, and the two Stress Dice show 3 and 2. His two successes cancel both of the attack's: 0 Net Successes, and it does not land.

The Pushed roll is final with a Gear Die showing 1, so his ODM Gear takes 1 wear, drops to 0, and Jams. He falls. On Body is high, and neither Wooded nor a Medium Titan raises it. The D6 shows 4, plus 2 makes 6: a hard landing, 2 damage. At the end of the round he made a Pushed roll with his ODM Gear, so his Gas Roll is three dice: 1, 1, and 5. His Gas Rating drops from 3 to 1.

Gas

  • Your Gas Rating is the gas left in the canister fitted to your ODM Gear, from 0 to 3. A full canister has Gas Rating 3. With no canister fitted, your Gas Rating is 0.
  • You can carry spare canisters. Each records its own Gas Rating, and one received from Standard Issue is full.
  • A canister at Gas Rating 0 is discarded when it comes off your ODM Gear, so a spare always has gas in it.

The Gas Roll

  1. Who. Every soldier, player character or Squadmate, who used ODM Gear during the round.
  2. When. At the end of the round, once for each such soldier however often they used it. A soldier who spends Momentum on a clean line makes none for that round. If the Titan Engagement ends partway through a round, that round's Gas Rolls are made at once, before the steps that follow the end of a Titan Engagement.
  3. Dice. Two dice. Three dice if any Pushed roll you made that round had your ODM Gear as its gear item, however many there were. A Pushed dodge made with your horse does not make it three. Light Trigger can make a three-die Gas Roll two dice.
  4. Read. Count the dice showing 1. The dice are not added, and a 6 does nothing.
  5. Effect. Lower your Gas Rating by 1 for each 1, to a minimum of 0.

A Flight is ODM use twice over and still makes one Gas Roll. A Push never adds a second Gas Roll; its cost is the third die of that round's one roll. The Gas Roll has no attribute, Talent dice, Bonus Dice, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice. It cannot be Pushed, Helped, or Covered, and it never causes a Stress Response. A Squadmate never Pushes, so its Gas Roll is always two dice.

A Titan's Attack Dice spend no gas. Gas goes only on the Gas Roll, and on a Fear Roll result that calls for one.

When a Fear Roll result calls for a Gas Roll (see Fear Rolls), roll two dice at once on the canister fitted to your ODM Gear, whatever you have Pushed this round, and read them as above. With no canister fitted, or at Gas Rating 0, nothing happens. It is not the round's Gas Roll and it is not ODM use, so it neither replaces nor adds to your Gas Roll at the end of the round.

A canister about to change hands

No canister moves before the gas it owes is rolled.

  1. If you have used ODM Gear this round and not yet made this round's Gas Roll, you make it before your fitted canister can be passed or taken: when you declare that you will pass it, or, if you are Down, when a comrade declares that they will take it. It has the dice your rolls so far this round give, and it is your Gas Roll for the round. You make none at the round's end, whatever canister you fit later.
  2. If that roll leaves the canister at Gas Rating 0, it is discarded. Nothing moves, and the pass or take spends nothing, so that soldier may still take an action that turn. If gas remains, the canister moves with its Gas Rating.
  3. A soldier who dies after using ODM Gear this round, before that round's Gas Roll, makes it at once, before their fitted canister is left behind. A canister that roll empties is discarded.

Change Canister

When you take Change Canister, you fit one of your spare canisters, and its Gas Rating becomes yours. You need a spare canister. In a Titan Engagement it spends your action (Quick Refit lets you spend your move instead); outside one it spends nothing. The canister you take off becomes a spare if it has gas, and is discarded if it has none. You can change a canister while airborne. It is not rolled, so it is never Helped or Covered.

Running dry

At Gas Rating 0 you have run dry:

  • Your ODM Gear counts as not had and adds no Gear Dice.
  • You cannot make an ODM move, so you make no Gas Roll for a later round until your Gas Rating is above 0.
  • You cannot make a Nape strike, or a Body Part strike from On Body or Blind Spot, unless the Titan is grounded (see A grounded Titan).
  • Running dry does not make you fall. If you are airborne, you stay airborne in your zone, with your attachment, until you stop being airborne. At On Body or Blind Spot you can let go (see What a move can do).
  • It ends when your Gas Rating rises above 0: by Change Canister, by Standard Issue, or by receiving or taking a canister and then changing to it.

Blade Sets

A Blade Set is one pair of ODM blades. It is the only unit in which they are carried, counted, passed, and lost.

The handles

  • Your handles hold one Blade Set at most, the set in use.
  • Only the Blade Set in your handles supplies Gear Dice, or counts as had, for the Nape strike, the Body Part strike, Break Free, and, in a Skirmish, Fight and Block. With empty handles you cannot strike until you swap.
  • When a Titan Engagement begins, if your handles are empty you fit a carried Blade Set, if you have one.
  • The set in your handles counts as no item. Every other Blade Set counts as 1 item.

Ruin

When a Pushed roll made with the Blade Set in your handles is final, and at least 1 point of wear is left after any Talent that ignores wear (such as Blade Discipline), that Blade Set is ruined. Discard it at once; your handles are empty. A Blade Set's current rating never falls, because ruin takes the place of wear. A Blade Set rated 2 or 3 is ruined the same way. Otherwise a Blade Set is lost only when it is dropped, passed, or named by a rule.

A dropped Blade Set

When a Fear Roll result drops your Blade Set (see Fear Rolls), the set in your handles falls, and your handles are empty. If they were already empty, nothing happens.

  • The set lies in your zone. No one takes it during the Titan Engagement, and no rule moves it. Your sheet counts the sets dropped.
  • When the Titan Engagement ends, it is shared out with the left items (see When a soldier dies or retires). Outside a Titan Engagement it is shared out when the procedure in which you made the Fear Roll ends.
  • You refit from a carried Blade Set with the swap, which you can make during a turn spent in advance.

The swap

  • If your handles are empty, fit one carried Blade Set into them.
  • In a Titan Engagement: once during each of your own turns, at any point, spending your move and never your action. A move can change no Position, so you stay where you are and still have your action: what a ruined blade costs you is that turn's Flight, and the Momentum it would have given. A turn spent in advance still happens, so you can swap during it, spending that turn's move.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement: at any time, as often as you like.
  • A Down soldier cannot swap. A Squadmate swaps exactly as a player character does.

Horses

Your horse

  • Your horse is the one on your sheet, from Standard Issue. You can mount only your own horse.
  • In a Titan Engagement your horse is in a zone. While you are mounted it is in your zone and moves with you. After you dismount, it stays in that zone until you mount it again, and your sheet records the zone's number. A Stride never moves it.
  • A horse changes Position only with its rider's move. No Titan chooses a horse, and no behavior affects one.
  • A horse sent as a successful riderless-horse decoy leaves the Titan Engagement: it holds no Position and cannot be mounted or give Gear Dice until the fight ends.
  • When a Titan Engagement ends, no horse holds a Position. Every living soldier's horse is still their own, lame or not, including one that bolted as a decoy. Mounted soldiers stay mounted and dismounted soldiers stay dismounted.

Mounted and dismounted

You are mounted while riding your own horse. A mounted soldier is never airborne.

  • Mount, with no roll.
    • In a Titan Engagement, as part of a move. Your horse is in your zone and not lame, you are free (on the Ground or Anchored), and you are not airborne, Down, Grabbed, or carried.
    • Outside a Titan Engagement, your horse is not lame, and you are not airborne, Down, or carried.
  • Dismount, with no roll. In a Titan Engagement it is part of a move, and the horse stays at the Position you hold when you dismount.
  • Both arms lost. If you have lost both arms (see Lost limbs), you mount and dismount only with a comrade's help. In a Titan Engagement, a comrade in your zone who is not Down spends their action to help. In a Skirmish, a comrade taking part who is not Down spends their action. Anywhere else, a comrade in the Squad who is not Down helps, spending nothing: one on the same Expedition, or any comrade when no Expedition is under way. Without help you do not mount or dismount.
  • Within a move, you can mount or dismount once, at the Position you hold before or after the move's step. A mounted soldier's ODM move dismounts before its step.
  • Taking to the air. If a rule makes you airborne while mounted, you are dismounted first, with no fall. In a Titan Engagement the horse stays in your zone.
  • Forced dismounts.
    • Your horse goes lame while you ride it: you are dismounted and fall from a horse. The horse stays at the Position.
    • You become Down while mounted: you are dismounted and fall from a horse. The horse stays at the Position.
    • You become Grabbed while mounted: you are dismounted with no fall, and the horse stays at the Position.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement there are no moves, except in a Skirmish, where a move may include one mount or one dismount (see Skirmishes). On an Expedition, every soldier who is not Down and whose horse is not lame is mounted on each Leg and dismounted at each Night Camp (see Expeditions). Otherwise you mount or dismount when the procedure under way says so, or at any time if it says nothing, within the requirements above.

What a horse rates

  • Your horse supplies Gear Dice to the dodge, Ride, and Leap Clear only while you are mounted on it. Horsemanship adds its dice to that dodge. Ride is the Leg roll on a Hard Ride (see Expeditions).
  • It supplies Gear Dice to Break Attention while you are mounted on it, or while it is in your zone.
  • A mounted soldier's dodge or Break Attention may use ODM Gear instead, one item per roll. That roll is ODM use and does not make you airborne.
  • A Pushed roll made with your horse wears the horse, not your ODM Gear, and does not by itself make the Gas Roll three dice. Sure Seat can ignore 1 point of that wear.

Lame

A horse at current rating 0 is lame. It cannot be mounted, adds no Gear Dice, and counts as not had for every action. If you are riding it, you are dismounted and fall from a horse.

Lameness ends in two ways only. Any Standard Issue, the interim issue included, exchanges a lame or worn horse unless you decline the exchange. The Maintain Gear Downtime Action returns your horse to its rating (see Downtime).

Falls

A fall inflicts damage, the kind of harm that marks Health boxes. A fall is never a Titan attack.

When you fall

You fall only when one of these happens:

  • Jam while airborne: your ODM Gear Jams, once the roll that Jammed it is finished.
  • Down while airborne: you become Down, once the harm that caused it, including any Critical Injury it gives, is resolved.
  • Down while mounted: a fall from a horse, once that harm is resolved.
  • Lame while mounted: your horse goes lame, a fall from a horse, once the roll that wore it is finished.
  • Carried: the comrade carrying you falls for any reason, becomes Grabbed while airborne, or dies while airborne or mounted. You fall from the carrier's band at the same moment.
  • Named by a rule, such as letting go, a knock-loose effect, or a release after the lift, or by a ruling, as the stakes of a called roll you fail. The rule names the band; for a ruling the GM names it from the height before the roll, low or high and never extreme, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one, and no height step raises it. Otherwise the band is low. When the stakes name a fall, the GM says your current Health with them.

A Grab's hold ends airborne and mounted before its crush, so a soldier the crush puts Down does not fall. Running dry, landing with a move, and being set down are never falls. A fall never causes a second fall for the same soldier.

The fall

  1. Find the band.
    1. A band already named. A fall a ruling names takes the band the GM named with the stakes, low or high and never extreme, or low if the GM named none, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one. A fall another rule names takes the band that rule names. No later step changes a band this step gives. A rule that makes you fall and names no band, such as a knock loose, letting go, or a release from a lift, takes the steps below.
    2. In a Titan Engagement, find the fall's reference body (see Where a fall lands). The later steps use it. You land in the zone you fell in.
    3. A fall from a horse is low, and nothing changes that.
    4. Otherwise, use your Position relative to that body when you fell: Distant or In Reach is low; On Body or Blind Spot is high.
    5. For a fall not from a horse, raise the band one step, to extreme at most, if the zone you fall in is Giant Forest or the reference body is Large. Both together still raise it only one step.
    Outside a Titan Engagement there is no reference body and no Position to read, so the fall takes the band the first step gives, and is low if neither a rule nor the GM names one.
  2. You stop being airborne, mounted, or carried. If you carry a comrade, carrying ends and they fall at the same moment from the same band.
  3. Roll D6 and add the band's value. Find the row on the fall damage table.
  4. If the row's damage is 0, the fall does no harm.
  5. Otherwise you take that damage. Any Critical Injury it gives has a rolled Injury Location and Injury Type Crush.

Step 2 comes before the damage, so a soldier the fall itself puts Down is no longer airborne or mounted and does not fall again.

Fall bands
BandAdds to the D6
Low+0
High+2
Extreme+4
Fall damage
D6 + the band's valueLandingDamage
2 or lessNo harm0
3–4Jarred1
5–6Hard landing2
7–8Heavy landing3
9 or moreShattering landing4

After the fall. Where you land is set in Fighting Titans. A fall changes no gear rating, Gas Rating, or item. It is not a behavior, so no Reaction answers it, and it is not a Fear Roll trigger.

Carrying

Items and the carrying limit

You can carry Strength + 4 items before you are Overloaded. Your attributes never rise, so your limit never changes. Only these count:

What counts as an item
ThingItems
Your worn ODM Gear, with its fitted canister, handles, and scabbards0
The Blade Set in your handles0
Each spare canister1
Each other Blade Set1
Each medical kit1
Each tool kit1
Each flintlock pistol1
Each musket2
Each Prosthetic0
A carried comrade5, plus every item that comrade carries (Strong Back removes the 5)
A horse0
Squad Supply0

Overloaded

While you carry more items than your limit, you are Overloaded:

  • Every ODM move you make also spends your action.
  • If your action this round is already spent, you cannot make an ODM move.
  • Nothing else changes: not your rolls, Gas Rolls, Reactions, or moves that are not ODM moves.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement it has no effect unless the procedure under way says so.

If you have lost one leg, every move you make on foot spends your action in the same way (see Lost limbs).

Shedding load

  • Drop any carried item that counts as 1 or more items, other than a carried comrade. A dropped item is gone.
  • Set down a comrade you carry. They stop being carried and stand on the Ground in your zone.
  • If you are carried and not Down, you can stop being carried, and hold the carrier's Position.

In a Titan Engagement you shed load at any point in your own turn, as often as you like, spending neither move nor action. An airborne carrier cannot set a comrade down, and a carried soldier cannot leave an airborne carrier. Outside a Titan Engagement you shed load at any time. A Down soldier cannot shed load.

Lift Comrade

When you take Lift Comrade, you pick up a comrade and carry them. The comrade is Down, or is not Down, consents, and has lost both legs (see Lost limbs).

  • In a Titan Engagement: the comrade is in your zone and is not Grabbed, Pinned, or already carried. You are not Down, carry no one else, and are not carried. It spends your action, or, with Rescue Ride while mounted, happens as part of your move.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement: the comrade is not Grabbed, not carried, and takes part in the same procedure as you: the soldiers that procedure's rule names, or else every soldier in the Squad on the same Expedition, or the whole Squad when no Expedition is under way. You are not Down, carry no one else, and are not carried. It spends nothing.
  • A Pinned soldier is never lifted (see Pinned).
  • A carried comrade is no longer airborne.

While you carry a comrade:

  • Their zone and attachment are yours, and they move whenever you do.
  • They are never airborne on their own ODM Gear, and being carried is not ODM use.
  • Their turns still happen, but their move changes nothing. If they are not Down, they keep their action for anything that needs no move.
  • They count for you as 5 items plus every item they carry. Those items stay theirs and also count against their own limit. Only they can drop or pass them, and while they are Down, any soldier, you included, can take one.
  • You carry one comrade at most, and a carried soldier carries no one.
  • Carrying goes on after a Titan Engagement ends.

When a Titan falls on you while you carry a comrade (see The Falling Titan), your comrade makes no Leap Clear roll and follows you. If you clear, you both hold In Reach. If you fail Leap Clear, you are Pinned, carrying ends, and your comrade is Pinned too.

Carrying ends when:

  • you set the comrade down, or they stop being carried;
  • you become Down while neither airborne nor mounted: they are set down in your zone;
  • you become Down while airborne or mounted: carrying goes on until step 2 of your fall, where it ends and they fall with you from the same band;
  • you become Grabbed: they are set down in your zone, and fall if you were airborne;
  • you fall for any other reason: they fall with you;
  • you become Pinned: they are Pinned too, with their own Crush Critical Injury at their own rolled Injury Location and their own pinning Body Part;
  • you die: they are set down in your zone, and fall if you were airborne or mounted;
  • you retire: they stop being carried, with no fall;
  • the carried comrade becomes Grabbed: carrying ends with no fall;
  • the carried comrade dies.

Outside a Titan Engagement no one holds a Position, so a comrade set down there holds none.

Pass Item

When you take Pass Item, one item moves from you to a comrade.

  • In a Titan Engagement: you are not Down, and the receiver is a living comrade in your zone (they may be Down or Grabbed). It spends your action. Two soldiers who have both left the Titan Engagement count as holding the same Position.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement: you are not Down, and the receiver is a living comrade in the same procedure. It spends nothing.
  • You can pass: a spare canister; your fitted canister, if it still has gas after any Gas Roll the pass makes (you are left at Gas Rating 0 with no canister, and it reaches them as a spare); a Blade Set not in your handles; a medical kit; a tool kit; or a flintlock pistol or musket, loaded or empty. A kit or a firearm keeps its rating and current rating.
  • You cannot pass: ODM Gear, the Blade Set in your handles, a horse, a Prosthetic, a carried comrade (set them down, then a comrade lifts them), or Squad Supply.
  • The item counts against the receiver's limit from then on, and against their carrier's if they are carried.
  • It is not rolled, so it is never Helped or Covered. You cannot pass to a comrade at another Position.

Take Item

When you take Take Item, one item moves to you.

  • From a Down comrade who is not Grabbed: one item they could pass if they were not Down.
  • From a dead comrade's left items, in a Titan Engagement only, where they lie: one of them.
  • In a Titan Engagement: you are not Down, and the Down comrade is in your zone, or the left items lie in it. Two soldiers who have both left count as holding the same Position. It spends your action.
  • Outside a Titan Engagement: you are not Down, and the Down comrade takes part in the same procedure. It spends nothing. Left items are never taken outside a Titan Engagement; they are shared out. If players disagree about who takes from a Down comrade, or in what order, use the roll-off. The Down comrade's player has no veto.
  • A fitted canister taken from a Down comrade leaves them at Gas Rating 0 with no canister and reaches you as a spare. If they owe a Gas Roll this round, they make it when you declare the take, before it moves; if that empties it, it is discarded, nothing is taken, and the take spends nothing.
  • It is not rolled, so it is never Helped or Covered. You cannot take an item at another Position.

Field Repair

When you take Field Repair, roll Wits with Gear Dice from a tool kit. Without a tool kit you roll Wits alone, unless Make Do applies. Gearwright adds dice.

  • Target: name one item before rolling: ODM Gear, a tool kit, a flintlock pistol, or a musket. A Blade Set, a horse, or a medical kit cannot be repaired. The tool kit that supplies the dice cannot be the one it repairs. A tool kit at 0 supplies no dice, so it can be repaired with another tool kit or with Wits alone.
  • Needs: 1 success.
  • On a success: the item's current rating rises by 1 for each success, up to its rating. ODM Gear raised above 0 is no longer Jammed, and a firearm raised above 0 is no longer worn out.
  • On a failure: nothing happens.

In a Titan Engagement

  • The item is yours, or belongs to a comrade in your zone. Two soldiers who have both left count as holding the same Position.
  • It spends your action. It can be Helped, Pushed, and Covered.
  • A failed roll can be tried again on a later turn.

Outside a Titan Engagement

  • Field Repair is rolled only in a care window held when a Titan Engagement ends, when a day passes on an Expedition (at the Night Camp), or on the interim day, within that window's scope. It is never rolled in Downtime, where Maintain Gear restores gear, never in a care window held after harm outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, and never at any other time.
  • The item belongs to any soldier in the window's scope.
  • Each living soldier in the scope who is not Down may make one Field Repair roll in each such window, separate from their Treat Injury roll.
  • A living soldier in the scope who is not Down, and is not rolling, may Help if they have not yet made or Helped a Field Repair roll in that window. Help is declared before the roll and spends their Field Repair roll for the window. At most 3 soldiers Help one roll.
  • Any soldier who could Help can Cover the roll's Push, even one who has already rolled or Helped. Covering spends nothing.
  • The players choose the order, using the roll-off if they disagree. Squadmates take part like player characters.
  • A failed roll can be tried again only in a later such window.

Standard Issue

Standard Issue is the gear every soldier receives on joining the Squad and whenever a rule issues it, scaled by Funding.

Funding

The Survey Corps' Funding, from 1 to 6, picks the row. In this playtest, Funding is 3. Playtest rule

When you receive it

  • A new player character, at the finish of the Lifepath.
  • A starting Squadmate, and any new character who joins the Squad later, including a Squadmate the Recruit Squad Action brings in.
  • Before each Expedition and on arriving at a Depot, every soldier in the Squad receives a full issue together (see Expeditions).
  • The interim issue. While no Expedition and no Downtime is under way, every soldier in the Squad receives it together at the start of each session, after the interim day and its care window, including that window's Field Repair rolls. If a session begins partway through a procedure, they receive it once that procedure has fully ended: after sharing out a dead soldier's gear, Retirement, and promotion. During an Expedition or a Downtime it never happens; the issue before each Expedition and a Depot's issue take its place. Playtest rule
  • Promotion gives no Standard Issue. A promoted Squadmate keeps its gear, and from then on receives every issue the Squad receives, including an interim issue that waits until after its promotion.

Every issue except the interim issue is a full issue. Standard Issue never gives a firearm or a Prosthetic.

What it holds

Every row fits one full canister to your ODM Gear, and every Blade Set it gives is rated 1.

Standard Issue by Funding
FundingODM Gear ratingSpare canistersBlade Sets, counting the one in the handlesHorse rating

Standard Issue by Specialty
SpecialtyItemRating
MedicMedical kit1
EngineerTool kit1

No other Specialty receives an item. A Squadmate uses its template's Specialty.

Receiving Standard Issue

  1. ODM Gear. If you have none, or yours is Jammed, worn (current rating below its rating), or rated below the row, you receive ODM Gear at the row's rating with a full current rating. The old one is gone. Otherwise you keep yours as it is.
  2. Canisters. Every canister you have is refilled, a full one is fitted if none is, and full spares are added up to the row's number. None is taken away.
  3. Blade Sets. Blade Sets are added up to the row's number, counting the one in your handles, and one is fitted if your handles are empty. None is taken away.
  4. Horse. If you have no horse, or yours is lame, worn, or rated below the row, you receive one at the row's rating with a full current rating. The old one is gone, and if you were mounted on it you are mounted on the new one. Otherwise you keep yours.
  5. Specialty item. If your Specialty has a row and you hold no item of that kind above current rating 0, you receive one, and every item of that kind you hold at 0 is gone.
  6. Declining. You may decline any spare canister, Blade Set, or Specialty item you would receive. You may also decline the exchange of Jammed or worn ODM Gear at step 1, or of a lame or worn horse at step 4, and keep it as it is until a rule restores it. Each choice is made at its step.

The interim issue differs in two steps Playtest rule

  • Step 1: it replaces ODM Gear only if you have none or yours is rated below the row. Jammed or worn ODM Gear rated at least the row's is kept as it is, for Field Repair to fix.
  • Step 5: it gives a Specialty item only if you hold none of that kind, so a kit at 0 is kept.

Its horse step is the same as a full issue's.

Standard Issue never restores the current rating of an item you keep. At a step that would replace a Kept Item or make it leave play, you keep it unless you hand it in (see Kept Items). When every soldier in the Squad receives Standard Issue together, Squad Supply is restocked too.

Squad Supply

Squad Supply is the shared pool of rations, flares, medical supplies, and Shot the Squad draws on in the field. The Squad as a whole holds it: no soldier carries it, and it counts as no item.

  • Rations: a Leg spends rations by its Leg roll and its Pace, a Night Camp spends 1, and some hazard rows spend or lose them. A Squad that holds fewer than it must spend meets hunger (see Expeditions and The Night Camp).
  • Flares: a rule that names a flare spends 1. The flare decoy for Break Attention is one. So are the Signal Relay Post's flare and a hazard row's flare on an Expedition. In a Skirmish you may fire a flare at a person: a Shoot roll with no Gear Dice that deals Burn damage, spending 1 flare and no Shot (see Skirmish weapons). A flare is a signal first.
  • Medical supplies: the uses below.
  • Shot: Reload spends 1 Shot to load one flintlock pistol or musket (see Firearms). No other rule spends Shot, though a failed called roll's stakes may name 1 unit of it.

Each kind is a whole number of units that never drops below 0. A use needs a unit of its kind and cannot be made with none left, except where a Leg or a Night Camp with too few rations meets hunger. A unit is spent only by a rule that names its kind, or by a failed called roll whose stakes named 1 unit of one kind. A unit is added only by a restock, a Requisition, or a successful called search (a Spot or a Survive) of a place the GM names with the stakes, on which the GM may add at most 1 unit per success, of one kind that place could plausibly hold. A place yields Squad Supply to one called search only: once a called search of it has been rolled, whatever the result, no later roll finds Squad Supply there, and the other soldiers present search it by Helping that roll. A place is one building, wagon, cache, or the like the Squad can search in one go, named by the GM with the stakes. A Waypoint scene yields Squad Supply to at most two called searches, whatever the number of places the GM names in it; no later search in that scene finds Squad Supply, whatever its result. No called search is made in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish; a place at hand is searched once the fight has ended, under these caps. The GM spends, adds, or removes units in no other way. The Squad sheet records each kind.

Stock

  • When the Squad forms, each kind is set to its Funding row.
  • Whenever every soldier in the Squad receives Standard Issue together, including the interim issue, each kind below its row rises to it. None is lowered, so units a Requisition added above the row stay.
Squad Supply stock by Funding

Medical supplies

  • On a care-window roll. When you declare a Treat Injury roll in a care window, before rolling, you may spend 1 medical unit for 1 Bonus Die on it, 1 unit per roll at most. Never in a Titan Engagement or on an aftermath roll.
  • Restocking a medical kit. At any point in a care window, including before its first roll and between rolls, any living soldier in its scope who is not Down may spend 1 medical unit to raise one medical kit held by a soldier in the scope to its full rating. Each kit can be restocked once per window. It is not a roll and spends no one's roll.

Kept Items

An item you receive by Requisition is a Kept Item (see Requisition). Any gear item can be one. Your sheet records which of your items are kept.

  • Standard Issue never takes a Kept Item away. At a step that would replace it or make it leave play, you may hand it in and take the issue in its place. If you do not, it stays as it is, as if you had declined that exchange.
  • A Kept Item stays kept when it is passed, taken, or shared out.
  • In every other way it is an item of its kind. It counts as items, wears, and is restored as that kind is. A Blade Set rated 2 or 3 is still ruined by any wear.
  • A granted ODM Gear or horse replaces your own, which leaves play.
  • Units of Squad Supply a Requisition adds are not Kept Items.

Firearms

The flintlock pistol and the musket are the gear items for Shoot, a Skirmish action (see Skirmishes). They have no use in a Titan Engagement: you never Shoot or Reload there, and a firearm gives Gear Dice to no roll made there.

Firearms (the full list is in Skirmish weapons)
FirearmItemsDamageInjury TypeNotes
Flintlock pistol12PierceFired at a Foe you are Engaged with or Apart from.
Musket24PierceFired only at a Foe you are Apart from. In a Skirmish, Reload also spends your move.
  • Received only by Requisition, rated 1 and empty. A firearm you receive is a Kept Item. Standard Issue never gives one.
  • Gear Dice. On a Shoot roll a firearm gives Gear Dice equal to its current rating. A Pushed Gear Die showing 1 wears it. At 0 it is worn out and cannot be fired until Field Repair or Maintain Gear restores it.
  • Damage. A shot that lands deals the damage above, plus 1 for each Net Success beyond the first, with Injury Type Pierce.
  • Loaded or empty. Your sheet records which. A Shoot roll empties the firearm, and an empty firearm counts as not had, so you cannot Shoot with it.
  • Pass, take, and share out a firearm like any other item, loaded or empty.

Shot and Reload

Shot is the Squad Supply kind a firearm's Reload spends, one unit per shot.

When you Reload, you load one empty flintlock pistol or musket and spend 1 Shot. Squad Supply must hold at least 1 Shot. Reload is never rolled.

  • In a Skirmish, Reload also spends your action, and, for a musket, your move as well.
  • Outside a Skirmish, you may Reload at any time for that 1 Shot alone. It spends nothing else, and the firearm stays loaded until it is fired.
  • In a Titan Engagement, you never Reload.

Prosthetics

A Prosthetic is workshop work fitted to a side you have lost. A Prosthetic arm is a hook or an iron hand on a leather socket. A Prosthetic leg is a peg or a jointed wooden leg on a leather socket.

  • Obtained only by Requisition at Limited Scarcity, one per Requisition. It must be of the kind of a side the named soldier has lost with no Prosthetic fitted, and that side's loss must have healed. A player character may obtain one for a Squadmate. Playtest rule
  • It is a Kept Item. Standard Issue never gives one or takes one away.
  • No rating. It has no Gear Dice, never wears, adds no die to any roll, and is never a weapon.
  • No item. It counts as no item, and it is never dropped, passed, or taken.

Fitting a Prosthetic

At a Downtime, once the Critical Injury that lost that side has healed (28 days, or 56 for a Burn), a Prosthetic is fitted to that side (see Downtime). It is fitted after granted items arrive, so one obtained that Downtime can be fitted at once. Fitting needs no roll and spends nothing. Each side takes one Prosthetic at most. It stays fitted for as long as you live, and your sheet records its kind and side.

What a Prosthetic does

  • It lowers your lost-limb grade one step. Count your lost sides of that kind less the Prosthetics of that kind fitted (see Lost limbs). One lost arm with a Prosthetic arm meets no arm grade. Both arms lost with one Prosthetic arm meet the one-arm grade.
  • Never past the one-limb grade. Two Prosthetics of a kind still leave you at that kind's one-limb grade.
  • The lost-limb rows' own penalties stay at every lost side, with a Prosthetic or without, and they stack.
  • Medical Retirement stays open beside it (see Retirement).

When a soldier dies or retires

  • Death. The dead soldier's ODM Gear, horse, and every Prosthetic fitted to them are gone. Every other item is left for the Squad: spare canisters, the fitted canister as a spare if it has gas, every Blade Set including the one in the handles, every kit, and every firearm. A soldier who dies owing a Gas Roll makes it first; a canister that roll empties is discarded.
  • Where left items lie. If the soldier dies in a Titan Engagement while holding a Position and not Grabbed, the left items lie at that Position until it ends. The sheet records the Position, its Focus Titan, and which items are still there. Otherwise they lie at no Position. No rule moves them.
  • Taking a left item. Before the procedure in which the death happened ends, a left item can be taken only with Take Item, by a soldier at the Position where it lies.
  • Sharing out. When that procedure ends, before any promotion, share out the left items and every Blade Set a Fear Roll result dropped during it:
    1. The players give each one not yet taken to a living soldier who took part. In a Titan Engagement, that is a soldier who held a Position at any point during it. Use the roll-off if the players disagree.
    2. Every one not given to a soldier is gone.
  • Left behind. If a soldier dies because a Titan Engagement ended with them left behind (see Leaving and the end of the fight), their left items are not shared out: they are lost with the field. An item they passed to a comrade before the end stays with that comrade.
  • Retirement. A retiring soldier's gear leaves with them (see Retirement).
  • A soldier who dies or retires while carried stops being carried, and a carrier who dies or retires sets their comrade down. A promoted Squadmate keeps its own gear. Squad Supply belongs to the Squad, so no death or Retirement takes any of it.

The gear sheet and the Squad sheet row

Your character sheet's gear field holds:

  • ODM Gear's rating and current rating, your Gas Rating, and each spare canister's Gas Rating;
  • the rating of the Blade Set in your handles, or that they are empty, and the rating of each other Blade Set you carry;
  • your horse's rating and current rating, whether you are mounted, and in a Titan Engagement the zone where your dismounted horse stands, or that it has left as a decoy;
  • each medical kit's and tool kit's rating and current rating;
  • for each flintlock pistol or musket, its rating, its current rating, and whether it is loaded;
  • which of your items are Kept Items, each named by its field and rating, such as "Blade Set carried, rating 2";
  • whether you are airborne, the comrade you carry, and the comrade carrying you;
  • in a Titan Engagement, your zone and your attachment, or that you have left, and the Momentum you hold, which is 0 outside a Titan Engagement and is cleared when one ends;
  • after you die in a Titan Engagement, the zone where your left items lie and which are still there.

Your sheet's harm fields also record each Prosthetic fitted, by kind and side, and how many Blade Sets a Fear Roll result has dropped from your handles in the current Titan Engagement.

Items carried, your limit, Overloaded, Jammed, and lame are worked out from those fields. You are never both airborne and mounted, and a carried soldier is neither. An attachment that names a body names it by its label: the first Focus Titan in a Titan Engagement is A, the next is B, and so on, and a Corpse keeps its Titan's label. Your Positions are never written down; read them from your zone and attachment.

The Squad sheet

The Squad sheet gives every soldier one line, in this column order. In a Titan Engagement, every soldier who takes part has a line, and its zone and attachment columns are the one record of where everyone is: every Position the Attention Ladder reads is read from them. A player character's line may show its gear, or a dash in each gear column its character sheet already holds. The zone column is always filled. Kept Items are not marked on the line.
