Fighting Titans

"Hold its eyes, cut its legs, take its neck. In that order, and never alone."Squad leader's briefing before the gate opens

A Titan Engagement is a scene in which the Squad confronts one or more Titans. The Focus Titan is tracked in full: its Body Parts, its Attention, and its Behavior Table. A Background Titan is still on its way, a clock that fills until it arrives. The fight runs in rounds, and it always ends: with a kill, with no soldier left standing, or with a retreat.

The GM runs the Titans from their tables and procedures. This chapter is the soldier's side of the fight.

Circumstances in a fight. Your rolls in a Titan Engagement are Standard. Each zone's Anchor Rating, Positions, Openings, a grounded Titan, Call It, and every penalty you carry already price the fight, so the GM names another step only for something no rule prices, such as rain on the rooftops, smoke over a burning district, full dark, ice, or a gale. A step named for a condition applies to every soldier's roll it touches while it lasts, the dodge and the Nape strike included, and the GM says so once: "the rain makes every roll with ODM Gear Hard until it stops", which is every roll whose Gear Dice come from ODM Gear. A Nape strike and a Body Part strike take their Gear Dice from a Blade Set, so that step does not reach them unless the GM names the condition for them too. When a further condition touches a roll already under a lasting step, the GM names one step for that roll weighing both, and steps never add. Your dodge takes the step in force when the Titan's card comes up, and no step is named or changed for it after. Circumstances never touch a Titan's Attack Dice, its cards, its Attention, or any table. A condition that hampers a Titan reaches the fight only through your dice: a Titan mired to the knee makes your dodge against it Easy, named when it is mired and before its card comes up, or, in rain already named Hard, one step the GM names weighing both; it keeps its Attack Dice.

Improvised acts. An act you describe that is not an entry taken as written is modelled on one entry the GM names, an action you could take in this fight, at that entry's size. Only a Nape strike taken as its entry is written kills a Titan, and a dive behind a chimney stack is a move, never a dodge (see When the steps do not resolve an action).

Starting a Titan Engagement

A Titan Engagement begins from a rule, such as a row of the Leg Hazard table or the Night table on an Expedition (see Expeditions), or from the GM's scene framing. The rule names what it sets: the field (its size, its field rating, and the rating of any zone), the Focus Titan, any Background Titans with their clock lengths, the length of the retreat clock, and sometimes where soldiers start and whether they ride. On an Expedition, the kind of Waypoint names the field rating.

Framing. Before any setup roll, the GM may name what a Mission Brief could for anything the rule leaves unnamed: the Focus Titan, the field size, the field rating, the rating of any zone, and the Background Titans, which are none, one, or two, each on a clock of 4, 6, or 8 segments, the counts and lengths the setup table uses. Anything neither names, the GM rolls on the setup table. A value the rule or the GM names is never rolled, and a value the rule names is never swapped. The GM never names a soldier's zone or attachment, a Titan's zone, a Stride's route, a tie, a Carry cost, or which zone a wreck takes, and the retreat clock is 8 segments unless a rule names its length.

  • Who takes part: the soldiers the rule names. If it names none, every living soldier of the Squad on the Expedition, or every living soldier of the Squad when no Expedition is under way.
  • Where you start: Focus Titan A, the first Focus Titan, stands in the centre zone. Every soldier who takes part stands on the Ground in the Squad's start zone, the zone directly below the centre. A rule that names a starting Position for a soldier places them by it: In Reach is the centre zone, on the Ground, and Distant is the start zone. A Straggler starts its victim in the centre zone and everyone else in the start zone. An Overrun starts every soldier in the centre zone.
  • Mounted or not: mounted soldiers stay mounted and dismounted soldiers stay dismounted, unless the rule says otherwise; an Abnormal at night starts every soldier dismounted. No one is airborne. A dismounted soldier's horse stands in the start zone.
  • Drives: a starting zone is placed by a rule, so it never meets a Drive's Position trigger until your own move changes it.

The start

  1. The field is laid out: its size, its field rating, and every zone's rating, which is also that zone's start rating. Everyone's Momentum is 0.
  2. The Focus Titan is labelled A. Its Body Parts start Intact, it has no Openings and no previous behavior, its Regeneration clock is empty, its Frenzy is 0, and the GM rolls its hidden Next Behavior. The retreat clock starts empty.
  3. Titan A stands in the centre zone, and soldiers and horses take their starting zones.
  4. The Titan checks its Attention Ladder. No cards are dealt yet, so unless a rung picks out one soldier, a Squad of two or more that starts in one zone starts with nothing holding its Attention until its first card.
  5. Make the Fear Rolls for a first Titan Engagement and for an Abnormal.
  6. Your Squad Tactics become unused.
  7. Round 1 begins.

The field

A Titan Engagement is fought over a field of hexagonal zones. A zone is about a Titan's reach across. Each zone has a number, its own Anchor Rating, whatever stands in it, and its effects. The numbers run column by column from the left, and top to bottom within a column, so one number names one zone on the table, on the tracker, and on the printable field.

  • Three sizes. The Standard field of 13 zones is the default. The Skirmish field of 7 zones and the Set piece of 19 zones are used only when the starting rule or the GM's framing names them.
  • Adjacent zones share an edge. The distance between two zones is the fewest zone steps from one to the other, and a zone's ring is its distance from the centre zone.
  • Edge zones have fewer than six neighbours on the field: every zone but the centre on the Standard and Skirmish fields, and the twelve outer zones of the Set piece. Off field is everywhere beyond them. A soldier who steps off field has left the Titan Engagement.
  • A zone holds anything. Any number of soldiers, horses, Focus Titans, and Corpses share a zone, and nothing in a zone blocks a move, a Flight, or a Stride into it, across it, or out of it. Two Focus Titans may stand in one zone.
  • A zone has no height. Height comes from your attachment, since On Body and Blind Spot are high, from a Giant Forest zone, and from a Large Titan (see Falls).
  • Only a Titan Engagement has a field. A Skirmish keeps Engaged and Apart.

Attachments and Positions

On the field you are in exactly one zone and hold exactly one attachment, which says where you are within that zone. Four attachments name a body, a Focus Titan or a Corpse, which must be in your zone. Ground and Anchored name none, and while you hold one of them you are free: only a free soldier can step into another zone.

Attachments
AttachmentLetterNames a bodyWhere you are
GroundGnoOn the ground in your zone, on foot or mounted.
AnchoredAnoOn your lines in the terrain of your zone. Only a Flight that ends free gives it.
On BodyByesHooked into the body or standing on it, anywhere but the Nape.
Blind SpotSyesAnchored to terrain in the body's zone, out of its sight with its Nape in reach, and not on it.
GrabbedXyesHeld in the hand of the Focus Titan it names.
PinnedPyesPinned under the body it names.
  • Airborne and mounted are not attachments. They sit beside it, as Gear defines them: an airborne soldier at Blind Spot is still at Blind Spot, and a mounted soldier is on the Ground. A carried soldier's zone and attachment are their carrier's.
  • The detach rule. When an attachment to a body ends by a rule that is not a fall or a move, you become Anchored if you are airborne and Ground if you are not, in the same zone, and nothing else about you changes. A Stride that leaves you at its Blind Spot, a Titan's death, and Fall Back all use it.

Your Position is read from where you are

You hold a Position relative to each Focus Titan and each Corpse: Distant, In Reach, On Body, or Blind Spot. A Position is never written down. Whenever a rule asks for one, read it from your zone and attachment, taking the first row that applies.

Reading your Position relative to one body
OrderIfPosition
1your attachment is On Body or Grabbed, naming that bodyOn Body
2your attachment is Blind Spot, naming that bodyBlind Spot
3your attachment is Pinned, naming that bodyIn Reach
4you are in that body's zoneIn Reach
5you are in another zoneDistant

While you are on the field you hold a Position, and that is what it means to be in the Titan Engagement. A soldier who has left, or is dead, holds none.

  • Distant is any other zone than the Titan's, out of its reach. Even so, a Behavior Table entry whose needed Positions include Distant ("Any" includes it) still happens while its holder is there: the Titan closes on them.
  • In Reach is the Titan's own zone: on the ground or on your lines beside it, or Pinned under it.
  • On Body means literally on it: hooked into the Titan's body or standing on it, anywhere but the Nape, or held in its hand.
  • Blind Spot is not on the Titan at all. You are anchored to terrain in its zone, a tree or a roof, hanging from it on your lines or standing on it, out of its sight with its Nape within reach. You are not touching the Titan. The name sounds like a place on the monster and it is a place in the world beside the monster. Whether you are airborne follows the move that brought you there: an ODM move leaves you airborne, as anywhere, so a Jam drops you and a Titan's falling body misses you. Only the Urban Terrain Trait says the roof holds you and you are not airborne. It is the only Position a Nape strike can be made from.

Blind Spot has no facing. A Titan on the field faces no direction any rule reads. Blind Spot exists in a zone whose rating has a step that reaches it, or beside a grounded Titan, and any number of soldiers may hold it. "Behind the Titan" is only how it looks. And because the tree does not walk with the Titan, a Blind Spot stays behind when the Titan strides away.

Two soldiers. Where a rule asks whether two soldiers hold the same Position, it asks whether they are in the same zone. Where it counts Position steps between two soldiers, it counts zones: one step is the same zone or an adjacent zone, and two steps is within two zones. So Help, Covering, Rally, swapping cards, and the Stress a Fear Roll gives comrades nearby read your zone or an adjacent one; Treat Injury, Lift Comrade, Pass Item, Take Item, Field Repair on a comrade's item, Pry Loose, a comrade's help to mount, and aftermath rolls read your own zone. Soldiers who have left follow Leaving.

An action never changes a Position

Your Position changes exactly when your zone or attachment changes, or when a body's zone does. Your own move changes your zone and attachment, and otherwise only the rules listed here do. No action moves you. The Nape strike, the Body Part strike, Break Attention, Draw Attention, Read, Heave, Treat Injury, Rally, and every other entry in the Action Catalog that is an action leave you exactly where you stand. Taking a Titan's Attention does not move you either, and neither does losing it or its Frenzy. Three Catalog entries are not actions, and they belong to the list below rather than to this paragraph: Fly, which is your own move; Leap Clear, which belongs to a Titan's body coming down; and Mount or Dismount, which is part of a move.

Everything that changes a zone, an attachment, or a Position
WhatWhere it leaves you
Your own moveIts zone steps and attachment steps, a mount or a dismount, or leaving. An ODM move is a Flight.
Letting go, in place of your moveYou fall from that height and land on the Ground in your zone.
Any fall, a knock loose includedYou stay in your zone and land on the Ground, so you hold In Reach relative to a body there.
A Fear Roll's forced stepOne step at the start of your next turn.
A Grab landingGrabbed, naming the Titan holding you: On Body relative to it, and you move with it.
Being freed from a GrabOn the Ground in the holding Titan's zone, with a fall first if you had been lifted.
A Focus Titan's StrideThe Titan changes zone. On Body or Grabbed, you go with it. At its Blind Spot, you stay and detach. Everyone else's Position relative to it is read again from the new zones.
A zone becoming Open under a standing Focus TitanBlind Spot relative to it becomes On Body, and everyone Anchored in that zone lands on the Ground and stops being airborne, with no fall.
A Titan becoming a Focus TitanIt stands in its entry zone, an edge zone no soldier stands in, so everyone holds Distant relative to it.
A Focus Titan dyingIts body becomes a Corpse in its zone, and every attachment naming it detaches, except Pinned.
A Titan's body coming down, by its death or by a living Titan becoming groundedEveryone On Body or at Blind Spot relative to it who is not airborne lands on the Ground in its zone, whether they Leap Clear or are Pinned. An airborne soldier swings clear and keeps their attachment.
Being carriedYour zone and attachment are your carrier's.
The Fall Back Squad TacticAt the wings step, you detach from On Body or Blind Spot: Anchored if airborne, Ground if not, with no fall.
A grounded Titan standing again, in an Open zoneBlind Spot relative to it becomes On Body, with no fall.
Starting placement, leaving, returning, and a retreat's forced movesAs each of those rules states.

That is the whole list. If a rule is not on it, it does not move you. A wreck moves no one, unless it makes a standing Titan's zone Open.

Anchor Ratings and steps

Every zone carries one Anchor Rating: Open, Sparse, Wooded, Urban, or Giant Forest. It decides which steps exist there and which kinds of move can make them: on foot, mounted, or ODM (on your own ODM Gear). The Distant to In Reach row is read for a zone step into a zone of that rating: on foot and mounted say whether that kind of move may enter it, and ODM says whether a Flight may end free in it. Every other row is an attachment step inside a zone of that rating, with In Reach read as free. A step is made in either direction, and no step calls for a roll of its own.

Steps by the zone's Anchor Rating
Zone's ratingStep (either way)Read asOn footMountedODM
OpenDistant to In Reachzone step into ityesyesno
OpenIn Reach to On Bodyattachment step in itnonoyes
SparseDistant to In Reachzone step into ityesyesyes
SparseIn Reach to On Bodyattachment step in itnonoyes
SparseOn Body to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
WoodedDistant to In Reachzone step into ityesyesyes
WoodedIn Reach to On Bodyattachment step in itnonoyes
WoodedOn Body to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
WoodedIn Reach to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
UrbanDistant to In Reachzone step into ityesnoyes
UrbanIn Reach to On Bodyattachment step in itnonoyes
UrbanOn Body to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
UrbanIn Reach to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
Giant ForestDistant to In Reachzone step into ityesyesyes
Giant ForestIn Reach to On Bodyattachment step in itnonoyes
Giant ForestOn Body to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes
Giant ForestIn Reach to Blind Spotattachment step in itnonoyes

No rating reaches Blind Spot from another zone in one step, so a Nape is never less than two steps from the next zone. In an Open zone nothing reaches Blind Spot at all while the Titan stands, because there is nothing to anchor to but the Titan. On a mixed field, one zone can have a Blind Spot and the next none.

Inside a zone: three shapes

The table above is the rule. These three pictures are the same rule, drawn for one zone. The shape is the zone's Anchor Rating, not the Titan's, so the first thing to ask is which of the three the Titan is standing in. Distant is any other zone, and the line to it is a zone step. Every step is made in either direction.

A grounded Titan changes every shape. While a Focus Titan has a Broken leg, every step joining two of In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot relative to it can also be made on foot, and none of them mounted. The shape does not change, only the ways of making it, with one exception: in an Open zone a grounded Titan gains an On Body to Blind Spot step, so the Open shape becomes the Sparse chain and the Nape is reachable on a plain at last. A Corpse counts as a grounded Titan for as long as it lies on the field.

What a move can do

A move names where it ends: a zone, or off field, and an attachment. It is made of steps, and it is the only way you change your own zone or attachment; everything else that moves you is on the list in An action never changes a Position.

  • A zone step goes from free in one zone to free in an adjacent zone, or off field from an edge zone, which is leaving. Which kind of move may make it reads the zone it enters: on foot, always; mounted, into any zone but an Urban one, and off field; a Flight, always, though a Flight never ends free in an Open zone.
  • An attachment step stays in one zone and follows that zone's rows among In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot, with In Reach read as free. A grounded Titan's and a Corpse's extra steps apply. There is no step from one body's attachment straight to another's: to go from one Titan's back to a second Titan's Blind Spot in the same zone, you detach first.
  • On foot: one step, a zone step or an attachment step. If you were airborne, you stop being airborne.
  • Mounted: up to two zone steps, and no attachment step. The move ends as you enter a zone that holds a standing Focus Titan, unless that zone is Open, where you may ride on through. A move can include one mount or one dismount, before or after its steps.
  • ODM: a Flight. If you are mounted, it dismounts you first.
  • A move may change nothing, as when you stay where you are or only mount.
  • Swapping a Blade Set spends your move in a Titan Engagement (see Blade Sets). A move can change nothing, so you refit and still strike on that turn; what a ruined blade costs you is that turn's Flight, and the Momentum it would have given.
  • Down: one on-foot zone step, only out of a zone that holds a body into an adjacent zone that holds none, never off field, and not while Grabbed, carried, or Pinned.
  • Grabbed or carried: your move changes nothing.
  • Pinned: your move changes nothing, and no rule moves you, not even a Stride (see Pinned).
  • Letting go. At On Body or Blind Spot, and not Grabbed or carried, you may use your move to let go. You fall from that height and land on the Ground in your zone. It is not an ODM move, so it is never rolled. It is how a soldier with a Jammed or empty harness gets off a Titan.
  • A forced step. A Fear Roll result can move you one step at the start of your next turn: toward Distant, as the retreat's first option (see Retreats), or toward the nearest comrade, a step that lowers the number of zones between you. It is a result, not your move: it spends nothing, it happens even on a turn spent in advance, and it never makes you leave or let go. It is a step of the kind your move makes, and it is never a Flight, so it is not rolled, pays no Carry, gives no Momentum, and sets no mark. No step is made for a soldier who is Down, Grabbed, carried, or Pinned, or who has no such step to make. A soldier already free in a zone that holds no body makes no step toward Distant. Playtest rule
  • Leaving. See Leaving.

Flight

Every ODM move you make in a Titan Engagement is a Flight. Roll Fly with your own ODM Gear. The roll needs nothing: the first step happens whatever the dice give, so a Flight never leaves you short of the step an ODM move always makes. What the dice decide is how far the Flight carries you, and whether you arrive loud.

Making a Flight

  1. Roll Fly with your ODM Gear. It is a roll like any other: it takes Circumstances, Stress Dice, and the Talents that name Fly, and it can be Helped, Pushed, and Covered.
  2. Each success is 1 Momentum, up to the anchors of the zone you start in.
  3. The first step is free. It may be a zone step or an attachment step.
  4. Each further step is a Carry, paid from your Momentum at the Carry cost in the table below. A Flight makes at most 2 Carries.
  5. Where it ends. A Flight never ends free in an Open zone: there its last step must be onto a body, or the Flight must go on out of the zone. A Flight that ends free ends Anchored. You are airborne after it.
  6. Trim. At the end of the move, Momentum above the anchors of the zone you end in is lost.
Carry costs
A Flight's stepMomentum it costs
The first step, of any kind0
A zone step into an Open zone2
A zone step into a Sparse zone1
A zone step into a Wooded, Urban, or Giant Forest zone0
A step off field, from an edge zone1
An attachment step1

A step that costs 0 is still a Carry, and still counts toward the limit of 2.

  • Crossing a Titan's zone is seen. A Flight crosses a zone when it enters that zone and leaves it again in the same move; the zone it starts in and the zone it ends in are never crossed. You are marked as loud on every Focus Titan standing in a zone your Flight crosses, whatever the roll gave, unless you spend Momentum on Quiet. A Corpse takes no mark.
  • On no successes you come in loud: you are marked as loud on every Focus Titan in the zone your Flight ends in, or, when there is none, on the Focus Titan nearest that zone, a tie going to the earliest label. Quiet stops it.
  • Gas. A Flight is ODM use twice over: it is an ODM move, and it is a roll whose gear item is your ODM Gear. However many times you use ODM Gear in a round, you make one Gas Roll for it. A Pushed Flight wears your ODM Gear as any Pushed roll with it does, and makes that round's Gas Roll three dice.
  • No gear, no Flight. While your ODM Gear is Jammed or your Gas Rating is 0 you make no ODM move at all, so you make no Flight.
  • A move that is not an ODM move is never a Flight and is never rolled: a step on foot, a mounted move, letting go, and a Fear Roll's forced step.

Momentum

Momentum is the speed you carry out of a good Flight. It is public, you record it on your sheet, and it runs from 0 to your cap: the anchors of the zone you are in, which its Anchor Rating gives.

  • You gain 1 for each success on a Flight, up to the anchors of the zone the Flight starts in.
  • Your cap moves with you. When your zone changes, or its rating falls, you lose any Momentum above the new cap at once.
  • You lose all of it at the end of any round in which you made no ODM move. Keep flying, or it is gone.
  • You hold no Momentum while Down, Grabbed, carried, or Pinned, or once you have left the Titan Engagement, and none is carried out of it.

Spend it at any point in your own turn, any amount at once. Carry is spent as part of the Flight it extends; every other spend is declared before the roll it names.

Spending Momentum
SpendCostWhat it does
Carrythe step's Carry costOne more step on this Flight after its free first step. At most 2 Carries on one Flight.
Bite11 Bonus Die on a strike or a Break Attention you take this turn against a Focus Titan in the zone your Flight ended in.
Brace11 Bonus Die on your next dodge this round.
Quiet1You set no mark this turn: not the loud mark of a Flight with no successes, and not the mark of crossing a Titan's zone. A mark already standing is not cleared.
Clean line1You make no Gas Roll for this round.

Bite and Brace give Bonus Dice, so they are declared with every other source before the roll, and they count against the cap of 4.

Example: a Flight to the Nape

On the Standard field, a standard Medium Titan stands in zone 7, Wooded. Private Mila Kraus stands on the Ground in zone 8, the Squad's start zone, also Wooded, with 0 Momentum. A comrade holds the Titan's Attention. Mila has Agility 4, Wirework 1, ODM Gear rated 3, and Stress 1.

The roll. She rolls Fly: 4 Agility dice, 1 Wirework die, 3 Gear Dice, and 1 Stress Die. The base dice show 6, 3, 6, 2, 4; the Gear Dice show 5, 1, and 2; the Stress Die shows 4. Two successes. She does not Push, so the Gear Die showing 1 wears nothing. She gains 2 Momentum, exactly the anchors of zone 8, where the Flight starts.

The steps. Her first step, from zone 8 into zone 7, is free. A Wooded zone joins In Reach and Blind Spot by an ODM step, so her second step is an attachment step to the Titan's Blind Spot: a Carry that costs 1. She ends at Blind Spot, airborne, with 1 Momentum. Zone 7's anchors are 2, so nothing is trimmed.

The spend. She spends her last point on Bite, for 1 Bonus Die on her Nape strike this turn. Had she scored no successes, her free first step would still have taken her into zone 7, Anchored, but she would have had nothing to pay the Carry to the Blind Spot, and she would have come in loud on the Titan.

A different line. Had she started in zone 5 and flown through zone 7 to zone 9, she would have crossed the Titan's zone and been marked loud on it whatever she rolled, unless she spent 1 Momentum on Quiet.

At the end of the round she made an ODM move, so she would keep any Momentum she still held, and she makes one Gas Roll of two dice for the round, however much flying she did in it.

Anchors, wrecking, and Terrain Traits

Each zone's Anchor Rating gives it its anchors, the trees, roofs, and beams a line can bite into, which cap the Momentum of everyone standing in it; the Carry cost a Flight pays to enter it; and one Terrain Trait. Every zone's rating is public, written on the field, and kept on the tracker. Each zone also keeps its start rating, the rating it began the fight with.

Anchor Ratings by zone
RatingLetterAnchors (Momentum cap)Carry cost to enterA wreck makes itTerrain Trait
OpenO02Open (no further step)A mounted soldier's Break Attention gains 1 Bonus Die when the soldier is in an Open zone. The plain is the horse's.
SparseS11OpenThe first wreck a zone takes while it is Sparse does nothing, once per zone. One good tree survives.
WoodedW20SparseNone. Ordinary woodland is the plain baseline.
UrbanU30WoodedA soldier who holds Blind Spot relative to a Focus Titan in an Urban zone is anchored to a roof and is not airborne, so a Jam does not drop them. A mounted move cannot enter an Urban zone.
Giant ForestG30WoodedEvery fall that is not from a horse, in a Giant Forest zone, is raised one band. The Corps fights best here.

Wrecking. A zone's rating can fall during the fight, and it never rises.

  • A Wreck effect on a Behavior Table entry takes one rating step off the Titan's own zone, whether the card landed or whiffed: it is the Titan going through the place, not an attack on anyone. It touches no soldier and no horse.
  • A Titan's falling body takes one rating step off the zone it lands in, which is its own.
  • A Stride never wrecks. A Titan walking across the field leaves the zones it passes as they were.
  • A zone becoming Open under a standing Focus Titan: every soldier who holds Blind Spot relative to that Titan holds On Body instead, and every soldier Anchored in that zone lands on the Ground and stops being airborne, with no fall.

The route to the Nape. A wreck can close the Blind Spot in one zone, but never the fight: a standing Titan follows its Attention holder into other zones, and a grounded Titan opens its Nape in every zone. Flights are still rolled, and still make their first step, in a zone with no anchors. The GM never wrecks, restores, or chooses a zone's rating during play.

Zone effects. A zone can hold Steam, in a Corpse's zone and in a living Titan's zone for the moment its steam rises, and dust, in a zone whose rating is below its start rating and in a falling Titan's zone as it falls. In this packet no zone effect harms anyone: Steam still burns soldiers by where they cling (see Steam), and Corpse Heat still burns the Pinned. Playtest rule

The mounted charge

A mounted move that enters or leaves a zone holding a Focus Titan may also mark you as loud on one such Titan, your choice, once per move. It is never rolled, spends nothing beyond the move, and is not ODM use, so it costs no gas. You must not be Grabbed or carried. The mounted pace lets a rider come in through an Open zone that holds a Titan and out again in one move; it is still one charge.

A grounded Titan

While a Focus Titan has a Broken leg it is grounded, and its Stride is 0. Every step joining In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot relative to it can be made on foot, and none of them mounted. No new steps appear, except in an Open zone, where a grounded Titan gains an On Body to Blind Spot step, on foot or by ODM.

When a Titan becomes grounded, its body comes down on the soldiers in its path (see The Falling Titan). When it stands again, it frees every soldier it pins, its Heave count returns to 0, and in an Open zone a soldier holding Blind Spot relative to it holds On Body instead, with no fall.

Where a fall lands

Every fall reads one reference body: the body your attachment named when you fell (On Body, Blind Spot, or Grabbed); otherwise the Focus Titan whose card, Grab, or effect made you fall; otherwise the Focus Titan nearest your zone, a tie going to the earliest label. The fall's height reads your Position relative to it (see Falls). You stay in the zone you are in and land on the Ground, so you then hold In Reach relative to any body there. A fall is never your own move.

Two Focus Titans

  • A second Focus Titan arrives from outside the fight at its entry zone, an edge zone no soldier stands in, so every soldier holds Distant relative to it.
  • One place, two Positions. Your one zone and one attachment give your Position relative to each Focus Titan. An attachment names one body at most, so you are never hooked into two Titans at once.
  • A Grabbed soldier holds On Body relative to the Titan holding them, and a Position read from their zone relative to any other.
  • Each Titan strides on its own cards, toward its own Attention holder.
  • When a Focus Titan dies, its body becomes a Corpse in its zone.

Horses and left items

A horse is in a zone. While you ride it, it is in your zone and moves with you; after you dismount, it stays in that zone until you mount it again. To mount, your horse must be in your zone and you must be free. Horse Whistle places your own horse in your zone before a move that mounts it. No other rule moves a horse, and a Stride never does. Titans never choose a horse, and no behavior affects one. A dead soldier's left items lie in the zone they died in, and no rule moves them.

The round

Each round

  1. Wings. Assign or keep Wings.
  2. Deal. Shuffle twenty cards numbered 1 to 20 (a D20 with repeats re-rolled works). Deal one to each living player character, one to each living Squadmate not on a Wing, and as many as its Tempo to each living Focus Titan. Soldiers who are Down, Grabbed, carried, or have left are still dealt a card. Cards are face up.
  3. Swap. Soldiers may swap cards.
  4. Play. Cards come up lowest first. On a soldier's card, that soldier takes their turn, then the Squadmate on their Wing takes its turn. On a Focus Titan's card, the Titan acts: it may stride toward the soldier holding its Attention, then it resolves a behavior.
  5. End steps, in order:
    1. Every soldier who used ODM Gear this round makes a Gas Roll. If the Titan Engagement ends mid-round, these Gas Rolls are made at once.
    2. Each living Focus Titan's Regeneration clock fills 1 segment.
    3. Each living Focus Titan's Frenzy rises by 1 at the end of every third round (the third, the sixth, the ninth), never above 3, and not at all at the end of any other round. It rises during a retreat as well.
    4. Each Background Titan's clock fills 1 segment, and full clocks resolve. Then the retreat clock fills 1 segment; if it is full, the Titan Engagement becomes a retreat. During a retreat no Background Titan's clock fills and the retreat clock does not fill; steps 2 and 3, the Regeneration clocks and Frenzy, still happen.
    5. Every soldier who made no ODM move this round loses all their Momentum. A soldier who flew keeps what they hold, to the cap the anchors of their zone give.
    6. The round ends. Unspent moves and actions are lost.

Your turn is one move and one action, in either order. You never split the move around the action. Under a retreat, the forced move comes first, with two exceptions: staying with a comrade comes after the action taken for them, and a move on a turn whose action is Lift Comrade comes after the lift (see Retreats).

Swapping cards

At the swap step, before any card comes up, two soldiers may exchange cards if each holds a card, neither is Down or Grabbed, they are in the same zone or adjacent zones (or both have left), and neither has swapped this round. A Titan's card is never swapped, and a Squadmate on a Wing moves with its player character's card. Swapping is not an action.

Wings

A Wing attaches one Squadmate to one player character. At the first wings step of a Titan Engagement, the players assign each living Squadmate to a living player character's Wing, or to none. At a later wings step, they may change Wings only if, since the previous wings step, a soldier died, left, became Down, or became Grabbed, or a Focus Titan entered or died. A Squadmate whose player character died or left is assigned again at the next wings step. A Wing holds one Squadmate. Settle disagreements with the roll-off.

  • On a Wing, a Squadmate is dealt no card and takes its turn right after its player character's turn, even when that turn was spent in advance.
  • On no Wing, it is dealt its own card.
  • A Wing only sets when the Squadmate acts. It adds no dice and has no Position requirement.

What every soldier knows about Titans

A standard Titan's numbers come from its Size Class, and they are public. An Abnormal lists its own values, and keeps its Toughness, Nape Depth, Regeneration clock, and Attention Ladder hidden until a Read reveals them.

Size Classes

Small (3 to 5 m)21321, 1, 1
Medium (6 to 10 m)12432, 2, 2
Large (11 to 15 m)12442, 3, 2
Attack Dice and Heave rating by Size Class
Size ClassTerrorizeControl and ThrashKillHeave rating
Small3662
Medium3693
Large69124

Attack Dice are the Titan Dice an entry of each tier rolls; Thrash rolls the control pool. The Heave rating is the Heave count that lifts the body off the soldiers it pins (see Heave). An arm that holds a Grabbed soldier has Toughness 1 while it holds them, whatever its Size Class. A fall whose reference body is a Large Titan is raised one band. A grounded Titan's Stride is 0, and an Abnormal lists its own. A standard Titan's Body Parts are its eyes, left arm, right arm, left leg, and right leg, in that order.

When a Titan acts

A Titan never takes a turn. When one of its cards comes up it checks its Attention Ladder, strides toward the soldier holding its Attention if they are in another zone, and only then resolves a behavior. Each of its cards resolves one behavior from its Behavior Table, chosen in advance by a hidden roll called its Next Behavior. Each entry prints its Attack Dice: how many Titan Dice it rolls. Titan Dice are a die kind of their own, in a colour no soldier's pool uses. Each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success. Titan Dice are rolled in the open, never Push, and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, Stress Dice, or Circumstances.

When a Titan's card resolves a behavior

  1. Announce. The GM names the behavior, its tier, and its targets.
  2. Roll. The GM rolls its Attack Dice as Titan Dice in the open and announces the successes. That number is the card's Severity, the same for every target. An entry with no Attack Dice rolls nothing and harms no one.
  3. Whiff or dodge. On 0 successes the card whiffs against every target, including a soldier who cannot make a Reaction, and no one dodges. Otherwise each target, knowing the Severity, chooses whether to dodge, or cancels with a dodge already made against this Titan this round (see Dodging a Titan).
  4. Land. The successes your dodge leaves standing are the card's Net Successes against you. On 1 or more the behavior lands on you; on 0 it whiffs against you. One card can land on one target and whiff against another.
  5. Effects. The effects apply in order to each target the card landed on, targets in card order, a Wing Squadmate just after its player character. A target who dies receives none of the card's later effects; every other target still does.

A card that whiffs has still resolved a behavior. It becomes the previous behavior and clears the Titan's marks, just as a card that lands does.

  • Targets: the soldier holding the Titan's Attention, or that soldier and everyone else in their zone who holds the same Position relative to the Titan and is not Grabbed.
  • Tiers. Low results terrorize (Stress, and perhaps a telegraph), middle results control (falls and Critical Injuries that cannot be lethal), and high results kill (lethal Critical Injuries and the Grab). Thrash is what a Titan does when nothing else fits. It is never rolled, works at any Position, uses no Body Parts, and is never lethal.
  • Effects: Stress (you gain that much, once, whatever the Net Successes); Critical Injury (a Titan attack at the Injury Location and of the Injury Type listed, lethal or not as listed; see Dodging a Titan for what its Net Successes add); knock loose (if you are airborne, On Body, or at Blind Spot, you fall, whatever the Net Successes; a mounted target is not affected); Grab (see The Grab); telegraph (the Titan's new Next Behavior is revealed to every soldier, whether the card landed or whiffed); Wreck (the Anchor Rating of the Titan's own zone drops one step, whether the card landed or whiffed, and whatever the Net Successes: it is the Titan going through the place, not an attack on anyone, so it touches no soldier and no horse).

It turns on whoever it can reach

When the soldier holding a Titan's Attention is not in a Position the rolled behavior needs, the Titan does not simply flail at the air. It turns on whoever it can reach. The GM reads its Attention Ladder again over only those soldiers who are in a Position the behavior needs, and its Attention moves to the one the ladder picks. The behavior then resolves against that soldier. Only when nobody in the Titan Engagement is in a Position the behavior needs does it fall back as its table says, and the fallback is read the same way: the Titan turns on whoever the fallback can reach, and Thrashes only when the fallback reaches nobody either.

Retargeting reads the Positions the Titan's Stride left: In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot exist only in the Titan's own zone, whatever its size, and an entry that names Distant reaches a holder in any other zone.

So standing where a Titan cannot hurt you no longer keeps it from hurting anyone. It keeps it from hurting you, and it hands the thing to the comrade standing closest to its hands. Cover them.

The Stride

A Titan on the field walks. Its Stride is how many zones it moves toward the soldier holding its Attention on each of its cards that resolves a behavior: Small 1, Medium 2, Large 2, and the Sprinting Abnormal 3. A grounded Titan's Stride is 0, and a Corpse never moves.

When a Titan strides

  1. When. Its card has checked the Attention Ladder, a soldier holds its Attention, and that soldier is in another zone. A card that resolves nothing does not stride: not while a decoy holds it, not while it holds a Grabbed soldier, and not while nothing holds its Attention.
  2. The route. It moves one zone at a time, up to its Stride, each time into an adjacent zone one closer to the holder's zone. When two zones are one closer, it enters the lower-numbered. It stops as soon as it enters the holder's zone, and it never goes off field. Nothing in a zone blocks it.
  3. What it carries. Every soldier On Body or Grabbed relative to it moves with it, zone by zone, with any comrade they carry. A soldier at its Blind Spot is anchored to the terrain: they stay in the zone it leaves, detach, and now hold Distant relative to it. A Pinned soldier stays where they lie. Horses, left items, other soldiers, and Corpses never move with a Titan.
  4. Then the behavior. Every soldier in a zone it enters now holds In Reach relative to it, and every soldier in a zone it leaves holds Distant. Its behavior is chosen against those Positions, retargeting included (see It turns on whoever it can reach).

The Stride is not a roll, an entry, or an action. It harms nobody, sets or clears no mark, wrecks nothing, and causes no Fear Roll. The GM chooses nothing in it: the ladder already chose where it goes, and the rule above chose the way. A Titan never strides toward a soldier who has left. An airborne holder is in their zone like anyone, and a carried holder is in their carrier's zone.

Example: the Stride

A standard Medium Titan, Stride 2, stands in zone 7 of the Standard field. Its Attention holder, a rider, is in zone 2. Zones 4 and 5 are both one zone closer to 2, so it enters 4, the lower number, then 2, and stops: two zones, and the rider now holds In Reach. Private Kurt Adler was On Body, so he rides along to zone 2. Private Hanne Roth was at its Blind Spot, airborne, so she stays in zone 7, Anchored, and now holds Distant relative to it, her Nape strike undone.

The Sprinting Abnormal, Stride 3, stands in zone 8 with its holder in zone 11. It enters 7 (7 and 10 are both one closer, and 7 is lower), then 9, then 11.

Frenzy

A Titan gets worse the longer you let it live. Each Focus Titan holds Frenzy, a public number the GM keeps on the tracker.

  • It starts at 0 when the Titan becomes a Focus Titan.
  • It rises by 1 at the end of every third round (rounds 3, 6, and 9), and stops at 3.
  • Its behavior roll is D6 plus its Frenzy. A total above the highest result on its table reads as that highest result.
  • The roll takes the Frenzy standing at the moment it is made. A Titan rolls its next behavior in advance, so an escalation reaches the table one card late, and a roll already made never changes.
  • Frenzy rises during a retreat too. It never falls, and a Corpse holds none.

Every Behavior Table is ordered the same way: the low results terrorize, the middle results control, the high results kill. Adding to the roll walks the Titan up its own table, toward the kill tier. The first rise reaches its table about round 5, the next about round 8, and none of them ever goes away.

Finish the fight, or it gets worse. Every round you spend not killing it is a round it spends becoming more dangerous, and by the time the retreat clock fills you are running from something worse than the thing you closed with.

Dodging a Titan

When a Titan's card scores 1 or more successes against you, you may dodge. Roll Agility with your ODM Gear, or with your horse while mounted. Horsemanship adds its dice to a dodge made with your horse. Declare Help and Bonus Dice before you roll, and Push as usual.

  • Cancel. Each success of your finished dodge cancels one of the card's successes. The card lands on you on 1 or more Net Successes and whiffs against you on 0.
  • Not dodging. If you do not dodge, every success is net against you. You can still dodge a later card from that Titan this round.
  • A whiff. No dodge is made against a card that scored 0 successes, so you keep your dodge for a later card.
  • One dodge per Titan per round. Your dodge's successes cancel against the card it answered and, separately, against every later card that Titan resolves against you this round, with no new roll. Cancelling never uses them up.
  • The turn it spends. A dodge is a Reaction. It spends your earliest turn whose move and action are both unspent, starting with this round's. A dodge against a Grab can have a later turn given back (see The Grab).
  • Who cannot dodge. A Down soldier cannot, and neither can a Grabbed or Pinned soldier, or one holding a Fear Roll result that forbids Reactions, such as Scream. They cancel nothing, so the card lands on them on 1 or more successes and whiffs on 0.
  • Call It. A Called behavior gives your dodge against its card 2 Bonus Dice (see Read and Call It). A dodge made earlier this round that only cancels against it gains nothing.
  • Circumstances. Your dodge takes Circumstances like any roll, at the step in force when the Titan's card comes up: Standard unless the GM named a step before then for something no rule prices, and no step is named or changed for your dodge once the card has come up (see Circumstances in a fight). A Titan mired to the knee makes your dodge against it Easy, named when it is mired and before its card comes up. The Titan's roll takes none.

Critical Injuries. A Critical Injury from a landed card adds 1 to its 2D6 roll for each Net Success beyond the first. A Critical Injury that cannot be lethal still cannot be. The Grab, knock loose, and Stress read no Net Successes.

Example: dodging a Bite

Private Jonas Keller holds In Reach and a standard Medium Titan's Attention, with Agility 3, ODM Gear 3, and Stress 1. The Titan's card resolves Bite: kill tier, 9 Attack Dice, a torso Critical Injury of Injury Type Bite that can be lethal.

The roll. The GM rolls nine Titan Dice in the open: 6, 5, 5, 4, 3, 3, 2, 1, 1. Severity 3.

The dodge. Jonas's turn this round is unspent, so his dodge spends it. He rolls 3 base dice, 3 Gear Dice, and 1 Stress Die: base 6, 4, 2; Gear 3, 2, 4; Stress 5. One success. No Stress Die shows 1, so he Pushes: Stress 2. The base dice 4 and 2 come up 5 and 3, the three Gear Dice come up 4, 5, and 3, the Stress Die comes up 3, and his new Stress Die shows 2. Still one success, and no Gear Die shows 1, so nothing wears.

The result. His 1 success cancels 1 of the Bite's 3, leaving 2 Net Successes: the Bite lands. Its 2D6 shows 6, and the second Net Success adds 1: a total of 7, Squeezed Between the Teeth, a 1-die penalty on Fly, Dodge, and Break Free. He dodged with ODM Gear, so he makes a Gas Roll at the end of the round.

Against a Tempo 2 Titan, his 1 success would cancel against its second card this round too, with no new roll.

Attention

A Focus Titan's Attention is held by one soldier, by a decoy, or by nothing, and everyone can see which. Its Behavior Table acts against the holder. The soldier holding a Titan's Attention cannot make a Nape strike against it.

Attention changes only at these moments. Between them, nothing soldiers do changes who holds it:

  • when the Titan becomes a Focus Titan;
  • when one of its cards comes up (but not a card that leaves a decoy's hold with cards to go, or one that comes up while it holds a Grabbed soldier);
  • when Break Attention places a decoy;
  • when its Grab lands;
  • when the holder dies, leaves, is freed, is Grabbed by another Focus Titan, or stops holding a Position relative to it. Then nothing holds its Attention until its next card.

The Attention Ladder

Each time a Titan checks its Attention Ladder, it turns to the soldier on the highest rung. The standard ladder, highest first:

  1. Hooked into its body: at On Body, or marked as having struck its Nape.
  2. Nearest person in reach: at In Reach.
  3. Just hurt it: marked as having hurt it.
  4. Loudest or brightest: marked as loud.
  5. Nearest: at the closest Position held by any soldier the Titan can choose, in the order On Body, In Reach, Blind Spot, Distant. It counts no zones: every soldier in another zone holds Distant alike.
  • A soldier marked as loud matches the ladder. While you hold the loud mark you also count, for that Titan, as meeting every rung any other candidate meets, except hooked into its body. So a shout, or a flight that came in loud, takes the Titan off any comrade who is not hooked into it or On Body, and never off a blade at its Nape. You are added to the tied set whatever Position you hold, Distant included, and the lower rungs then narrow the set as they always do, the loudest rung keeping you in it.
  • A soldier marked as having struck its Nape comes before a soldier who is only On Body.
  • Ties are narrowed by each lower rung; the nearest rung keeps the tied soldiers at the closest Position any of them holds. If still tied, the soldier already holding its Attention keeps it. Otherwise the lowest card this round takes it (a Wing Squadmate counts just after its player character), unless no cards have been dealt this round or the ladder is checked at an end step, as when a Background Titan enters. Otherwise nothing holds its Attention until its next card.
  • Down and carried soldiers meet only the nearest rung. A Titan turns to a Down soldier only when no one is closer, hooked in, hurting it, or louder.
  • Soldiers who have left, soldiers held by another Titan, and horses are never chosen. No ladder reads a Position relative to a Corpse.
  • An Abnormal may have its own ladder. Every ladder begins with hooked into its body and ends with nearest, so a soldier who strikes a Titan's Nape and falls short always draws its Attention. They take its next behavior only when that behavior's Position requirement includes Blind Spot. When it does not, the Titan retargets to a comrade it can reach, and its Attention goes with them.

What a Titan remembers

A Titan remembers three things about soldiers, shown as marks on its tracker row:

  • Struck its Nape: any Nape strike against it, whatever the result.
  • Hurt it: a Body Part strike against it with at least 1 success.
  • Loud: Draw Attention against it, or a Fear Roll result that draws its Attention, such as Scream, each only in its zone; a Flight that crosses its zone, whatever the roll gave; a Flight that scored no successes and ends in its zone, or ends nearest it when no Titan stands in that zone; or a mounted charge into or out of its zone. Momentum spent on Quiet sets no mark that turn.

Each mark lasts until the end of that Titan's next card that resolves a behavior, whether that card lands or whiffs. A card that resolves nothing (a card spent by a decoy, a card while it holds a Grabbed soldier, or a card with no one holding its Attention) leaves every mark standing, so the marks are always read by the card that acts.

Draw Attention

When you take Draw Attention against a Focus Titan, you shout, wave, or flash steel to make yourself the loudest or brightest thing it can see. You must be in its zone, holding a Position other than Distant relative to it, and not be Grabbed. It is not rolled. It spends your action (Shout Them Off lets you spend your move instead). You are marked as loud until the end of its next card that resolves a behavior.

While you hold that mark you match every rung of its ladder that any other candidate meets, except hooked into its body. The shout takes the Titan off any comrade who is not hooked into it or On Body, and it never takes it off a comrade with a blade at its Nape. It moves Attention only when the Titan next reads its ladder.

Break Attention and decoys

When you take Break Attention, you put a decoy in the Titan's eyes: a flare, your horse, your thrown cloak, or your own Feint across its face. Roll Perception with Gear Dice from your ODM Gear or your horse (a Feint on foot uses none). You can take it if you hold a Position relative to the Titan, are not Grabbed, and meet the requirement of the decoy you name, while no decoy already holds that Titan's Attention.

Successes needed

  • 1 if you hold the Titan's Attention;
  • 2 for anyone else, including while nothing holds its Attention;
  • 2 for anyone against a Titan that holds a Grabbed soldier;
  • +1 if the decoy is a Feint;
  • +1 for each decoy in a row: each decoy that has held this Titan's Attention since the last of its cards that resolved a behavior, whoever placed it. The count starts at 0, rises by 1 each time a decoy takes its Attention, and returns to 0 when one of its cards resolves a behavior.

On a success:

  • The decoy holds the Titan's Attention for as many of its next cards as its Tempo: one round of its cards, however the deal falls. Each of those cards resolves nothing and uses up its Next Behavior. The last of them ends the hold, and the Titan checks its ladder. The marks stand until its next card that resolves a behavior. No decoy can be laid over one that holds.
  • Each success beyond what you needed creates 1 Opening, created by you.
  • A soldier the Titan holds is freed.

On a failure nothing happens, but anything the decoy spends when declared is still spent.

Decoys
DecoyRequirementSpendsAlso
FlareThe Squad Supply holds a flare, and the Titan's eyes are not Broken.1 flare, when declaredOnce the Break Attention, and any Hook and Cut it allows, is resolved, every Background Titan's clock fills 1 segment, success or not.
Riderless horseYour own horse is not lame, and you are mounted on it or it is in your zone. Works against Broken eyes.nothingOn a success you dismount with no fall, and the horse bolts. It leaves the Titan Engagement: it is in no zone, cannot be mounted or sent again, and gives no Gear Dice until the fight ends.
