On foot against a grounded Titan it has no gear item: Perception alone, no Gas Roll, no wear. If more than one way qualifies, name one before rolling.

Timing. A decoy holds for as many of the Titan's cards as its Tempo, and the last of them ends the hold. Any of its cards still to come this round counts toward the hold. Break its Attention once all of its cards this round have come up, and the hold lasts until its last card next round: one card for a Tempo 1 Titan, both cards for a Tempo 2 Titan. Every turn before that card, this round and next, is taken while the decoy holds.

Example: breaking Attention with a Feint

Round 2 against a standard Medium Titan (Tempo 1), in a Wooded zone. The Titan's card 5 has already come up and resolved Snap Short against Private Hanne Roth, who holds In Reach and its Attention. Snap Short rolls 3 Attack Dice; the GM rolled 5, 3, 1, Severity 1. Hanne chose not to dodge, so its 1 success was net: it landed, and she gained 1 Stress, going to Stress 2.

Card 8: Hanne. She takes Break Attention and names a Feint: she is at In Reach with working ODM Gear. She needs 1 (she holds Attention), +1 for the Feint, +0 decoys in a row (the Titan's last card resolved a behavior): 2 successes. Perception 3, ODM Gear 3, and Stress 2 give 3 base dice, 3 Gear Dice, and 2 Stress Dice. She rolls base 6, 3, 1; Gear 6, 2, 3; Stress 4, 5. Two successes. Her Feint holds the Titan's Attention for its next card, and its decoys in a row is now 1. She made a roll with ODM Gear, so she makes a Gas Roll at the end of the round.

Round 3. Hanne draws 3 and the Titan draws 11. On card 3 the Feint still holds, so Hanne makes a Flight to the Blind Spot in the Titan's zone and takes a Nape strike: she does not hold the Titan's Attention. She falls short with 2 successes, which become 2 Openings her comrades can spend, and she is marked as having struck its Nape.

Card 11: the Titan. The Feint's hold ends on this card, which resolves nothing and uses up the Next Behavior. The Titan checks its ladder: Hanne is on the top rung. She holds its Attention, and her mark still stands, so the Titan's next card that resolves a behavior will come for her at Blind Spot. Until then, a second Break Attention against it needs 1 more success for the decoy already in a row.

Harming Titans

Titans have no Health. You harm them by breaking Body Parts, and you kill them only at the Nape.

Body Parts and Body Part strikes

Each Body Part has a Body Part State: Intact, Wounded, or Broken. It also has a count of successes toward its next state. Each kind is struck from certain Positions: eyes from On Body or Blind Spot, arms and legs from In Reach or On Body.

When you make a Body Part strike, roll Strength with your Blade Set. You need the Position the part names, the part must not be Broken, you must not be Grabbed, and from On Body or Blind Spot your ODM Gear must work unless the Titan is grounded.

  1. Take the successes one at a time. Each adds 1 to the part's count.
  2. When the count reaches the part's Toughness, the part moves one state toward Broken and the count returns to 0.
  3. Once it is Broken, each remaining success creates 1 Opening, created by you.

A strike with at least 1 success marks you as having hurt the Titan. Body Part States and counts are public.

What Broken does.

  • An entry cannot happen while the Titan lacks the unbroken Body Parts it uses.
  • Broken eyes: flares and thrown cloaks no longer work as decoys against it.
  • A Broken arm frees the soldier it holds.
  • A Broken leg grounds the Titan until no leg is Broken, and its body comes down on the soldiers in its path (see The Falling Titan). Nape strikes against a grounded Titan gain 2 Bonus Dice. Nape strikes and Body Part strikes against it need no working ODM Gear. Every Body Part can be struck from In Reach, On Body, or Blind Spot, except an arm holding a Grabbed soldier. Its attachment steps can be made on foot, its Stride is 0, and a Feint against it can be made on foot.

Nape strikes

When you make a Nape strike, roll Strength with your Blade Set. You need all of these:

  • you hold Blind Spot relative to the Titan;
  • you do not hold its Attention, and you are not Grabbed;
  • your ODM Gear works, unless the Titan is grounded;
  • no retreat is under way.

Your Bonus Dice can come from Openings, from a grounded Titan, and from Help, 4 at most. Every Nape strike, whatever its result, marks you as having struck the Titan's Nape.

Where the strike leaves you

A Nape strike does not move you. You began at Blind Spot and you are still at Blind Spot when it is over, anchored to your tree or your roof behind the Titan, whether you killed it or fell short.

The mark counts as hooking into the Titan, for the Attention Ladder and nothing else. It puts you on the ladder's top rung, ahead of a comrade who is merely On Body. It does not put you on the Titan, and no other rule reads it: Steam at a Regeneration fill still passes you by, because you are not on the body.

So a striker who pulls the Attention onto themselves cannot strike again. The strike needs a soldier who does not hold that Titan's Attention, and the mark you just set is what turned it toward you. You are standing in exactly the right place and you are locked out of using it. Getting the Attention off you takes a comrade's Draw Attention, a Break Attention with a decoy, or the Titan itself turning on someone it can reach. That is the engine of the whole fight: the blade at the Nape is never the soldier the Titan is watching.

  • Reach its Nape Depth and the Titan dies.
  • Fall short and each success becomes 1 Opening, created by you. Relentless adds 1 more.

A strike's successes never add to a later Nape strike, except as Openings.

Openings

An Opening is a public token on a Titan that records who created it. Openings come from successes beyond Breaking a Body Part, extra successes on Break Attention, each success of a Nape strike that falls short, Relentless, and any rule that names one. A soldier making a Nape strike spends Openings as Bonus Dice, 1 die each. You can never spend an Opening you created; it waits for a comrade, even if you die or leave. Regeneration and the Titan's death erase Openings.

Regeneration and death

Each Focus Titan's Regeneration clock fills 1 segment at the end of each round, retreat or not: a retreat stops only the Background Titans' clocks and the retreat clock. When it is full:

  1. Erase every Opening on the Titan.
  2. Every Body Part's count returns to 0.
  3. The most damaged Body Part moves one state toward Intact: a Broken part before a Wounded one, then the one listed first.
  4. If a Body Part moved, every soldier who holds On Body relative to the Titan is scalded by Steam. If it was the Titan's last Broken leg, the Titan stands: it frees every soldier it pins, and its Heave count returns to 0.
  5. The clock empties.

When a Titan dies

  1. Every soldier holding a Position loses 1 Stress.
  2. A soldier the Titan holds is freed. For the next two steps they hold On Body relative to the dying body and are not airborne; a fall the release makes them take is resolved first, and they then land on the Ground in its zone.
  3. Steam. Every soldier at On Body or Blind Spot relative to it is scalded (see Steam).
  4. The fall. Its body comes down (see The Falling Titan), unless it was already grounded. Then it falls no second time: every soldier it pins stays Pinned, now under its Corpse, and Corpse Heat begins.
  5. Its Openings, Next Behavior, clock, marks, and remaining cards are removed.
  6. Every attachment naming it detaches, except Pinned (see the detach rule).
  7. Its body stays on the field as a Corpse, in the zone it died in, and every Position relative to it is read from where everyone is.
  8. If no Focus Titan is alive and no soldier is Pinned, the Titan Engagement ends.

Steam

A Titan's body gives off scalding Steam. It is damage of Injury Type Burn: it marks Health lost like any damage, and reaches the Critical Injury tables only when it brings you to 0 current Health (see Health). It rolls no Attack Dice, and no Reaction answers it.

  • When a Titan dies, every soldier who holds On Body or Blind Spot relative to it is scalded, including a soldier its death freed from its hand.
  • When a Titan's Regeneration clock fills and a Body Part moves toward Intact, every soldier who holds On Body relative to it is scalded. A fill that moves no Body Part releases no steam.
  • A soldier at Blind Spot is not on the body, so a Regeneration fill never scalds them. Only the Titan's death reaches them, because a dying body throws steam over the ground behind it as well.
  • A soldier at In Reach or Distant, or holding no Position relative to the Titan, takes nothing.

When you are scalded, roll D6 once on the steam table and take the damage its row gives; a 0 does no harm. It is a fixed roll: no Push, Help, Stress Dice, or Talent changes it. A Critical Injury the damage inflicts has a rolled Injury Location, and no Net Successes add to it.

Steam (D6)
D6Burn damage
1–30
4–51
62

Example: a Nape strike on a grounded Titan

A standard Medium Titan (Nape Depth 4) has a Broken right leg, so it is grounded. Private Dieter Lang broke it last round from In Reach and created 1 Opening with his extra success; no one was in the path when its body came down. Dieter holds In Reach and the Titan's Attention. Private Hanne Roth holds Blind Spot with Strength 4, Clean Cut 1, a Blade Set rated 1, and Stress 1. Private Kurt Adler holds Blind Spot beside her with his action unspent.

Hanne strikes the Nape. She holds Blind Spot, does not hold its Attention, and is not Grabbed. She declares 4 Bonus Dice, the cap: Dieter's Opening, 2 for the grounded Titan, and Kurt's Help. Her pool is Strength 4 + Clean Cut 1 + 4 Bonus Dice = 9 base dice, 1 Gear Die, and 1 Stress Die.

She rolls base 6, 6, 5, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, 1; Gear 2; Stress 6. Three successes, one short. No Stress Die shows 1, so she Pushes: Stress 2, and a new Stress Die. The seven base dice not showing 6 come up 6, 5, 4, 4, 3, 2, 1, and the new Stress Die shows 3. Four successes: the Titan dies. Dieter's Opening is spent, and her Gear Die did not show 1, so nothing wears.

The death. Every soldier holding a Position loses 1 Stress. Hanne and Kurt hold Blind Spot, so each rolls D6 on the steam table: Hanne's 5 deals her 1 Burn damage, and Kurt's 2 deals nothing. Dieter at In Reach takes none. The Titan was already grounded, so its body does not fall again. It lies as a Corpse, no soldier is Pinned, and no Focus Titan is alive: the Titan Engagement ends.

Had she stopped at three successes, they would have become 3 Openings for Dieter or Kurt, and she would have drawn the Titan's Next Behavior.

The Falling Titan and Leap Clear

A Focus Titan's body comes down on the soldiers under it. This is the Falling Titan. It rolls no Attack Dice, and no Reaction answers it. It happens:

  • when a Focus Titan dies, unless it was already grounded;
  • when a living Focus Titan becomes grounded by a Broken leg.

It wrecks its zone. The body takes one rating step off the zone it lands in, which is its own (see Wrecking), resolved with the fall and before any Leap Clear. It happens whether or not anyone stands in the path; a Sparse zone's first wreck does nothing, and an Open zone takes no further step.

The path

  • In the path: every soldier who holds On Body or Blind Spot relative to the falling Titan.
  • Airborne: an airborne soldier swings clear with no roll and keeps their attachment.
  • Outside the path: every soldier at In Reach or Distant; a soldier Grabbed in another Titan's hand, whatever Position they hold; and a soldier this Titan holds Grabbed when it becomes grounded, who stays Grabbed.
  • Freed at its death: a soldier the dying Titan held is in the path, at On Body and not airborne.

Leap Clear

When you are in the path and are not airborne, Down, or carried, roll Leap Clear: Agility, with Gear Dice from your ODM Gear, or from your horse while mounted. It needs 1 success.

  • Leap Clear is not an action and not a Reaction. It spends no turn and no action, and you make it whatever you have already spent.
  • Comrades in your zone or an adjacent zone may Help, each spending their action. You may Push; a Squadmate never Pushes.
  • On a success you clear, and land on the Ground in the Titan's zone, holding In Reach relative to it.
  • On a failure you are Pinned.
  • Down: a Down soldier in the path makes no roll and is Pinned.
  • Carried: a carried soldier makes no roll and follows their carrier. If the carrier clears, both land on the Ground in the Titan's zone. If the carrier is Pinned, carrying ends and the comrade is Pinned too, with their own Critical Injury at their own rolled Injury Location and their own pinning Body Part.

Pinned

A Pinned soldier is held under a Titan's body, living or a Corpse.

When you are Pinned

  1. The pin. You gain a Crush Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location. It cannot be lethal, and no Net Successes add to it. An arm or leg result is a limb pin of that limb. A torso or head result is a body pin.
  2. The pinning Body Part. A limb pin names the Titan's Body Part of the same kind and side: a left arm result is pinned by its left arm, a right leg result by its right leg. If that part is Broken, the other part of its kind pins instead. If both are Broken, no part pins; the pin is still a limb pin, and only Heave or the Titan standing frees you. A body pin names no part. Your Position at the fall plays no part.
  3. Where you are. Your attachment is Pinned, naming the body, in its zone: you hold In Reach relative to it, and you are not airborne.

While you are Pinned:

  • You cannot move or be moved. Your move changes nothing, and no rule changes your zone or attachment: no forced step, no fall, no Stride, and no carrying. Lift Comrade never takes you.
  • A limb pin forbids Covering and Reactions. You keep your action, for the entries the table below allows and nothing else.
  • A body pin forbids Help, Covering, and Reactions. You have no action: it counts as spent for every rule that asks. You are not standing.
  • A Titan's card lands on you as on any soldier who cannot make a Reaction: on 1 or more successes. A Grab on you lands only its crush (see The Grab).
  • A forced strike a Fear Roll result demands waits while you are Pinned.
  • Under a Corpse you take Corpse Heat at the start of each of your turns.
What a Pinned soldier may take
PinMay takeCannot take
Limb pinHeave, Body Part strike (only against the Body Part that pins you), Read, Call It, Rally, Draw Attention, Help, Swap Initiative CardNape strike, Break Attention, Break Free, Squad Tactic, Lift Comrade, Change Canister, Pass Item, Take Item, Mount or Dismount, Swap Blade Set, Shed Load, Restock Medical Kit, Fight, Shoot, Reload, Release, Treat Injury, Field Repair, Fly, Ride, Downtime Action, Squad Action
Body pinSwap Initiative CardHeave, Body Part strike, Read, Call It, Rally, Draw Attention, Nape strike, Break Attention, Break Free, Squad Tactic, Lift Comrade, Change Canister, Pass Item, Take Item, Mount or Dismount, Swap Blade Set, Shed Load, Restock Medical Kit, Fight, Shoot, Reload, Release, Treat Injury, Field Repair, Fly, Ride, Downtime Action, Squad Action

A pin ends when you are freed (see Heave and cutting free) or when you die. A freed soldier stops being Pinned and stands on the Ground in the body's zone, holding In Reach relative to it. The pin's Critical Injury stays, as every Critical Injury does. Losing the pinned arm or leg, as Corpse Heat can take it, frees no one: you stay Pinned by the same Body Part, Heave and cutting free still work, and the lost-limb grade applies at once.

Heave and cutting free

A Pinned soldier is freed in these three ways and no others. The end of the Titan Engagement frees no one.

When you Heave, you throw your weight under the body to lift it off a Pinned comrade. It is an action: roll Strength, with no Gear Dice.

  • Who: a soldier who holds In Reach or On Body relative to a body that pins at least one soldier, and is not Down, Grabbed, carried, or body-pinned. A limb-pinned soldier may Heave the body that pins them.
  • Comrades in your zone or an adjacent zone may Help, each spending their action. You may Push.
  • Each success adds 1 to the body's Heave count. The count is public and starts at 0. It carries over between Heaves by different soldiers, through a Regeneration fill, and from a Titan to its Corpse. It is cleared only when a living Titan stands.
  • When the Heave count reaches the body's Heave rating, every soldier the body pins is freed. A Heave with 0 successes frees no one.
  • Heaving a Corpse costs you 1 Burn damage after the roll, whatever its result. A Critical Injury this damage inflicts at 0 current Health lands at an arm, its side rolled.
Heave rating by Size Class
Size ClassHeave rating
Small2
Medium3
Large4

An Abnormal lists its own Heave rating in its stat block.

Cutting free. Make a Body Part strike against the pinning Body Part of a limb pin, from In Reach or On Body relative to the body. It needs no working ODM Gear, since the body is grounded or a Corpse. The limb-pinned soldier may strike it themselves, and comrades may Help as on any Body Part strike. Its successes count toward the part's Toughness as any strike's do, and when the part becomes Broken, every soldier it pins is freed.

  • On a living Titan the strike marks you as having hurt it, successes beyond Breaking the part create Openings, and the Broken part does what its kind does.
  • A Corpse's Body Parts keep their states, counts, and Toughness, and never regenerate. A strike on a Corpse marks no one and creates no Opening.
  • A body pin is never cut free.

The Titan stands. A living Titan that stops being grounded frees every soldier it pins, and its Heave count returns to 0.

The Corpse and Corpse Heat

A dead Focus Titan's body stays on the field as a Corpse for the rest of the Titan Engagement, under the Titan's label.

  • Where it lies. In the zone its Titan died in. It never moves and never strides, and Steam hangs over its zone.
  • Its Positions. At the death, every attachment naming the Titan detaches, except Pinned. Every Position relative to the Corpse is then read from where everyone is: In Reach in its zone, Distant elsewhere.
  • Steps. A Corpse counts as a grounded Titan for attachment steps, for as long as it lies on the field: every step joining In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot relative to it can be made on foot.
  • They are Positions. A Position relative to a Corpse is a Position in the Titan Engagement. It counts for every rule that reads one: the ending tests, witnesses, the care window, Stress relief, Grief, Heave, Corpse Heat, the retreat's forced moves, and returning. A Corpse's zone counts as a Focus Titan's zone for leaving and for the retreat.
  • What it does not do. A Corpse holds no Attention, plays no cards, has no clock, and takes no mark. Heave and a strike on a pinning Body Part are the only acts made against it: no Nape strike, no other Body Part strike, no Read, no Break Attention or Draw Attention, and no Openings.

Corpse Heat. An evaporating Corpse burns whoever lies under it. At the start of each of your turns while you are Pinned under a Corpse, beginning with your first turn that begins after the fall, you gain a Burn Critical Injury at the pinned limb and its side, or at the torso for a body pin. It can be lethal, counts toward worsening and takes it, and no Net Successes add to it. Every one of your turns counts, as for a time limit counted in turns.

  • Under a living grounded Titan there is no heat. If that Titan dies, heat begins at the start of your first turn that begins after its death.
  • A soldier who Heaves a Corpse takes 1 Burn damage (see Heave).

Example: under the Falling Titan

Round 4 against a standard Medium Titan (Heave rating 3) in a Wooded zone. Private Kurt Adler holds On Body with his action spent and is not airborne, with Agility 3, ODM Gear 3, and Stress 2. Private Dieter Lang and Private Hanne Roth stand on the Ground in its zone, In Reach.

Card 7: Dieter strikes the Wounded right leg with a count of 1 and scores 1 success. The leg Breaks, the Titan is grounded, and its body comes down.

Leap Clear. Kurt is in the path. He rolls 3 base dice, 3 Gear Dice, and 2 Stress Dice: base 5, 3, 1; Gear 4, 2, 3; Stress 3, 2. No success, and no Stress Die shows 1, so he Pushes: Stress 3. The base dice come up 4, 2, 5, the three Gear Dice come up 5, 3, and 2, the Stress Dice 4 and 5, and his new Stress Die shows 2. Still no success, and no Gear Die shows 1, so nothing wears: he is Pinned.

The pin. The Injury Location D6 shows 4, his right leg: a limb pin. The 2D6 on the leg table shows 7: Wrenched Knee, a 1-die penalty on Fly, Ride, and Dodge. The Titan's right leg is Broken, so its left leg pins him instead. Kurt lies Pinned in its zone, In Reach relative to the Titan.

Card 12: Hanne Heaves. Dieter's action is spent, so no one Helps. Strength 4 and Stress 1 give 4 base dice and 1 Stress Die: 6, 6, 3, 2; Stress 4. Two successes: the Heave count is 2 of 3.

Round 5. Dieter draws 2, Kurt 6, and Hanne 9. On card 2 Dieter, who does not hold its Attention, walks to Blind Spot, a step the grounded Titan allows on foot, and his Nape strike reaches its Nape Depth. The Titan dies. Dieter is at Blind Spot, so he rolls on the steam table: a 4, 1 Burn damage. The Titan was already grounded, so it does not fall again. Kurt stays Pinned, now under the Corpse, and the Heave count stays at 2. No Focus Titan is alive, but a soldier is Pinned, so the Titan Engagement runs on.

Card 6: Kurt. His turn begins after the death, so Corpse Heat gives him a Burn Critical Injury at his right leg, worsened by the Wrenched Knee he already holds there.

Card 9: Hanne Heaves the Corpse and scores 1 success. The Heave count reaches 3: Kurt is freed and stands on the Ground in the Corpse's zone. After the roll Hanne takes 1 Burn damage. No Focus Titan is alive and no soldier is Pinned, so the Titan Engagement ends.

Read and Call It

When you take a Read, roll Instinct against one Focus Titan, with no gear. You must hold a Position relative to it and not be Grabbed. It needs 1 success. Reading from Distant gains 2 Bonus Dice.

For each success, pick one fact. Each fact can be picked once per Read, and a success that picks no fact is lost unless Call It spends it. The GM reveals every picked fact to every soldier at once:

  • Any Focus Titan: its Next Behavior, in full with its Attack Dice, until a card resolves it or a decoy's card uses it up. Its Severity is known only when the card that resolves it rolls.
  • An Abnormal only: one named Body Part's Toughness, its Nape Depth, its Regeneration clock's length, or its Attention Ladder.

Everything else is public without a Read: each Titan's Size Class and whether it is an Abnormal, Body Part States and counts, filled clock segments, Openings, the Attention holder and marks, the previous behavior, Background clocks, and the state of a Grab. Against a standard Titan, its Next Behavior is the one thing a Read can learn.

Call It is an option of a Read that revealed the Next Behavior. It takes 2 of that Read's successes, including the one that revealed the behavior (Sharp Call makes it 1). Each target other than you who dodges the card that resolves the Called behavior gains 2 Bonus Dice, even if it resolves as its fallback or as Thrash, and even if the Titan retargets and the behavior lands on someone else. What was Called is the entry, not the soldier it lands on. You gain nothing from your own Call It, and a target who cancels with an earlier dodge gains nothing. A Next Behavior can be Called once, and the Call ends when that card is done, when a decoy's card uses the behavior up, or when the Titan dies.

The Grab

A Grabbed soldier is held in a Titan's hand.

  • You cannot Help, Cover, or make a Reaction. You can still Push.
  • Your attachment is Grabbed, naming the Titan holding you, so you hold On Body relative to it, and you move with it when it strides. This is a rule moving you, never your own move.
  • You stop being airborne, are dismounted with no fall, and stop carrying anyone or being carried.
  • Your move changes nothing, and your only action is Break Free.
  • You hold the holding Titan's Attention, and no other Titan can choose you. You stay Grabbed if you become Down.

When a Grab lands

A Grab lands on 1 or more Net Successes. No step reads the Net Successes.

  1. Hold. You are Grabbed by the first unbroken arm in the Titan's stat block. While it holds you, that arm's Toughness is 1 and its count 0; its state does not change. Being Grabbed ends airborne, dismounts you with no fall, and ends carrying; a comrade you carried is set down, and falls if you were airborne.
  2. Crush. You gain a torso Critical Injury of Injury Type Crush that cannot be lethal. No Net Successes add to its roll. It crosses off a box like any other. If it crosses off your last box, you are Down in the Titan's hand and do not fall.
  3. Attention. You hold the Titan's Attention.
  4. Witnesses. Every witness makes a Fear Roll.

A witness is every soldier who, once the event is fully resolved, is alive and holds a Position in the Titan Engagement, other than the victim. A soldier who has left is not a witness, a Down witness makes no Fear Roll, and one event gives each soldier one Fear Roll at most.

A Grab on a Pinned soldier cannot take hold. When a Grab lands on a Pinned soldier, from the Titan whose body pins them or from any other, only the crush lands. There is no hold, no Attention, no Fear Roll, and no countdown, and the soldier stays Pinned.

The countdown

The countdown counts your own turns that come up after the Grab lands, including a turn spent in advance and a turn taken while Down. The Grab spends the action of your first counted turn, whether or not something already spent it.

  • At the end of your first counted turn, if you are still Grabbed, you are lifted. Lifting harms nothing, but Break Free grows harder and fewer comrades can reach the hand.
  • At the end of your second counted turn, if you are still Grabbed, the Titan devours you. You die, and every witness makes a Fear Roll.

A dodge never costs you a second turn. If your dodge against the Grab's card, or an earlier dodge that cancelled against that card, spent a later turn because your first counted turn was already partly spent, that later turn is unspent again as the Grab lands, before the crush, and your first counted turn counts as spent instead.

Neither the lift nor the devour is a behavior. Neither rolls Attack Dice, and no Reaction answers either. While a Titan holds a living soldier, each of its cards resolves nothing and its Next Behavior waits. If the Grabbed soldier dies by any rule, a Death Roll included, the Grab ends at once. The dead soldier holds no Position and does not fall, the arm returns to normal, nothing holds the Titan's Attention until its next card, and that card can act again. Every witness makes one Fear Roll for the death.

Getting free

Ways out of a Grab
Way outWhoNeedsBefore the liftAfter the lift
Break FreeThe Grabbed soldier, not Down2 successesNo penalty2-die penalty
Pry LooseA comrade with the Talent, at On Body2 successesNo penalty2-die penalty
Strike the holding armAnyone not Grabbed2 successes in total while the arm is Intact, 1 while WoundedFrom In Reach, On Body, or Blind Spot, no penaltyFrom On Body or Blind Spot only, no penalty
Break AttentionAnyone who can take it2 successes, 1 more for a Feint, plus 1 per decoy in a row on the TitanFrees the soldierFrees the soldier
Kill the TitanA Nape strikeNape DepthFrees the soldierFrees the soldier

Break Free is Strength with Gear Dice from a Blade Set, and it can be Helped. Strikes from On Body or Blind Spot need working ODM Gear unless the Titan is grounded. Grounding never widens the reach in this table; only the Clear the Hand Squad Tactic does.

Once you are freed: before the lift you stand on the Ground in the holding Titan's zone, In Reach, with no fall; after the lift you fall from On Body's height and land on the Ground there. Either way the arm's Toughness returns to normal with a count of 0, and nothing holds the Titan's Attention until its next card, unless a decoy freed you.

Freed by the holding Titan's death: if you were lifted you still fall, and that fall is resolved first. For the death's steam and fall you hold On Body relative to the dying body and are not airborne, so you are scalded and in its path; then you stand on the Ground in its zone.

Example: a Grab and a rescue

A standard Medium Titan draws card 5, Private Lena Falk draws 9, and Private Ruben Hartmann draws 12. Lena stands in the Titan's zone, In Reach, and holds its Attention, with Agility 3, ODM Gear 3, and Stress 1. Ruben stands in the same zone, with Resolve 3 and Stress 1.

Card 5. The Next Behavior is Grab: kill tier, 9 Attack Dice. The GM rolls nine Titan Dice in the open: 6, 5, 5, 4, 3, 3, 2, 1, 1. Severity 3. Lena's turn this round is unspent, so her dodge spends it. She rolls base 6, 4, 2; Gear 3, 2, 4; Stress 5: one success. She Pushes to Stress 2: the base dice 4 and 2 come up 6 and 1, her three Gear Dice come up 2, 5, and 4, the Stress Die comes up 3, and her new Stress Die shows 4. Two successes cancel two of the Grab's three. One Net Success: the Grab lands.

Hold: the left arm, the first unbroken arm, takes her; while it holds her its Toughness is 1. Crush: 2D6 on the torso table shows 7, Deep Bruising: a 1-die penalty on Fly, Dodge, and Break Free. The Grab reads no Net Successes, so nothing adds to the roll. Witnesses: Ruben rolls D6 + Stress 1 − Resolve 3, and the die shows 3: a total of 1, Heart in Your Throat. He gains 1 Stress.

Card 9. Lena's first counted turn. Her dodge already spent it, and the Grab spends its action anyway. At its end she is still Grabbed, so she is lifted. Now the hand can be struck only from On Body or Blind Spot.

Card 12: Ruben. He makes a Flight to the Titan's body, an attachment step in its zone, and strikes the holding arm: Strength 3, Blade Set 1, and Stress 2 give 3 base dice, 1 Gear Die, and 2 Stress Dice. He rolls base 6, 6, 2; Gear 4; Stress 3, 5. Two successes: the first moves the arm to Wounded, the second Breaks it. Lena is free. She was lifted, so she falls from On Body's height (a high fall) and lands on the Ground in its zone, In Reach. Nothing holds the Titan's Attention until its next card, and Ruben, now On Body and marked as having hurt it, stands on its top rung.

Had no one freed her, the Titan would have devoured Lena at the end of her next turn.

Retreats

Every Titan Engagement carries a retreat clock. It starts empty and fills 1 segment at each round's end, after the Background Titans' clocks; a flare never fills it. The rule that begins the Titan Engagement names its length, and the setup table gives it 8 segments. Playtest rule

The Titan Engagement becomes a retreat when the retreat clock is full, or when a Background Titan's clock fills while two Focus Titans are alive. From then on, no Background Titan's clock fills, the retreat clock does not fill, and no Titan enters. Each Focus Titan's Regeneration clock still fills. The GM records the round in which the retreat began.

Forced moves

Every soldier who holds a Position and is not Down, Grabbed, or carried cannot decline their move, and must use each move on one of these, their choice. A retreat narrows your own move rather than replacing it, so an ODM move made under a retreat is a Flight like any other: it is rolled, each success is 1 Momentum, it can Carry, and it can come in loud. A Fear Roll's forced step is not your move and is never a Flight.

  1. Out. One step your move can make, the first of these that applies: from On Body or Blind Spot, an attachment step to free, or letting go if you cannot make one; from free in a zone that holds a body, a zone step into an adjacent zone one ring further out from the centre, or, from an edge zone, option 2; from free in a zone that holds no body, a zone step one ring further out. Among the zones a step may enter, those holding no body come first, then the one farthest from the nearest living Focus Titan (or from the nearest Corpse when none is alive); a tie left over is your choice.
  2. Leave, from an edge zone, while free, whatever body stands in that zone.
  3. Toward a fallen comrade: a step that lowers the number of zones between you and a Down, Grabbed, or Pinned comrade who is not carried.
  4. Stay: a move that changes nothing, if you are already in such a comrade's zone and have already taken, earlier this turn, an action for that comrade: Treat Injury; for a Grabbed comrade, Pry Loose, a strike on the holding arm, or Break Attention against the holding Titan; for a Pinned comrade, Heave against the pinning body or a strike on the pinning Body Part.

For options 3 and 4, a Grabbed comrade is in the holding Titan's zone and a Pinned one in the pinning body's zone. Lift Comrade does not allow option 4: a soldier who lifts a comrade carries them out by option 1 or 2. A soldier who can do none of these may let go, or change nothing.

The stay limit. Options 3 and 4 for a comrade the limit covers are open only on the retreat's first rounds: as many rounds as its retreat clock has segments, counted from the round after the one in which the retreat began. The limit covers:

  • a comrade Pinned under a Corpse, in every retreat, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive, even one that entered during the run-on;
  • every comrade, Pinned, Down, or Grabbed, while no Focus Titan is alive.

From the next round on, no soldier steps toward or stays with a comrade the limit covers. Each such move is option 1 or 2, or, for a soldier who can make neither, letting go or changing nothing. While a Focus Titan is alive, a Down or Grabbed comrade, or one Pinned under a living Titan, has no such limit. From the first round past the limit, a soldier whom a lost-limb grade forbids every move is not standing (see Past the stay limit).

Why a retreat always ends. A soldier who takes option 1 or 2 on every move is off the field within five moves: at most two to become free, at most two to reach an edge zone, and one to step off. A Stride never lengthens that, because it frees a soldier at its Blind Spot and changes no soldier's ring, and a Grab stops your moves only until it ends.

  • Move first. On each turn, your forced move comes before your action, except that staying put (option 4) comes after the action taken for that comrade, and a move on a turn whose action is Lift Comrade comes after the lift. A soldier beside a Down comrade can lift them and carry them out in the same turn.
  • No return. A soldier who has left cannot come back.
  • No Nape strikes. No Nape strike is made during a retreat, on a soldier's own turn or through Hook and Cut. Every other action is still allowed, including Body Part strikes, Break Attention, Pry Loose, Treat Injury, Heave, and Lift Comrade.

Leaving and the end of the fight

Leaving is a zone step off field from an edge zone. You may make it when you are free, are not Down, Grabbed, Pinned, or carried, and the edge zone you step from holds no Focus Titan and no Corpse; during a retreat that last condition does not apply.

  • It may be a move on foot, or the last step of a mounted move or of a Flight, where it is a Carry that costs 1. A Flight whose only step is the step off field pays nothing, since a Flight's first step is free. Playtest rule
  • A comrade you carry leaves with you, and so does a horse you ride. A dismounted horse stays in its zone.
  • Returning. A later move can bring you back, but not during a retreat. It enters an edge zone of your choice that holds no Focus Titan and no Corpse, on the Ground, mounted or not as you left; if every edge zone holds one, any edge zone.

After you leave, you still take turns, and they count for every rule that counts turns. Your move can only return you. Your action can be any entry that needs no Position relative to a Titan. All soldiers who have left count as holding the same Position as each other, and never the same as, or one step from, a soldier still in the fight. No Titan targets you or strides toward you, and your ODM use still makes a Gas Roll.

How a Titan Engagement ends

  • No Focus Titan is alive, and no soldier is Pinned: it ends at once. Background Titans that have not entered never do.
  • No soldier standing: no soldier who holds a Position is alive and not Down, including when everyone has left. A body-pinned soldier is not standing. A limb-pinned soldier who is not Down is standing.
  • Past the stay limit. In a retreat, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive, from the first round past the stay limit, a soldier whom a lost-limb grade forbids every kind of move, and who is not Down, Pinned, or carried, is not standing either. Today that is the both-legs grade. The test reads the grade, not the turn: a soldier who loses one turn's move to a Reaction or a result still stands. It is checked as that round begins, before its wings step, and after every event from then on. The soldier keeps their zone, their turns, and every action their Position allows, such as Heave, a strike on a pinning Body Part, Treat Injury, and Rally. A comrade in their zone may still take Lift Comrade for them, with their consent, and carry them out.

Check both tests as soon as the event that could make one true is fully resolved, including the Fear Rolls and Drive decisions it causes.

When no soldier is standing, check for a returner: a soldier who has left, is alive, and is neither Down nor carried, while no retreat is under way. If no soldier holds a Position at all, or there is no returner, the fight ends at once. Otherwise play goes on, and it ends at the end of the next round if still no soldier is standing, or at once if the last returner is gone. If a soldier stands again first, the fight goes on.

The run-on

While a soldier lies Pinned under a Corpse after the last Focus Titan dies, the Titan Engagement runs on. Rounds continue: the Background Titans' clocks and the retreat clock fill as usual, and a Background Titan may enter. The first test ends the Titan Engagement when the last Pinned soldier is freed or dies, unless no soldier standing ends it first.

  • When the retreat clock fills, the run-on becomes a retreat, and the stay limit applies (see Retreats). After the retreat's first rounds, every standing soldier walks out, even from a comrade Pinned under the Corpse, and even if a Background Titan has entered since.
  • A limb-pinned soldier left alone is still standing, so the rounds go on for them. Corpse Heat strikes at the start of each of their turns, and they cannot treat themselves. They go Down unless they free themselves first.
  • A soldier who cannot move. Past the stay limit, a soldier the both-legs grade forbids to move is not standing (see Past the stay limit). If no Focus Titan is alive when the Titan Engagement ends, they live and go through the end steps.

Left behind

If the fight ends with no soldier standing while a Titan lives, every soldier still holding a Position, each one Down or, past the stay limit, unable to move, dies, left to the Titans.

Whether or not a Titan lives, every soldier still Pinned when the fight ends with no soldier standing dies, left under the body.

These deaths have no witnesses and cause no Fear Roll, but they count for Grief, and the soldiers' left items are lost with the field. With no Focus Titan alive, every other soldier lives, a Down comrade beside the Corpse and a soldier past the stay limit who cannot move included. Then the end steps run.

Squad Tactics

A Squad Tactic belongs to the Squad, not to any soldier. Before its first Titan Engagement the Squad chooses two (roll-off if you disagree). Each can be used once per Titan Engagement when its condition is met. Using one is not an action, and a use is spent once declared. If several players could declare the same use and disagree, use the roll-off.

Squad Tactics
TacticConditionDeclared by, and whenEffect
Hook and CutA Break Attention against a Titan has just succeeded, a comrade at Blind Spot with an unspent action can make a Nape strike, and no retreat is under wayThat comrade's player, once every success step of the Break Attention is resolved (a freed soldier's release included) and before any other rule actsThat comrade strikes the Nape at once, and may spend the Break Attention's Openings
Hamstring LineA leg strike is Helped by at least one comradeThe striker's player, after Help and before the rollIf the strike has at least 1 success, it gains 1 more
Clear the HandA soldier has been Grabbed and lifted, and a comrade declares a strike on the holding arm that only this tactic allowsThat comrade's player, as the strike is declaredUntil that Grab ends, every strike on the holding arm can also be made from In Reach, and none needs working ODM Gear
Fall BackThe wings step begins, with a soldier who is not Down, Grabbed, or carried at On Body or Blind SpotAny such soldier's player, before Wings are assigned or keptEach such soldier may detach instead, Anchored if airborne and Ground if not, in the same zone, and so hold In Reach, with no fall; each player chooses for their own soldier

Hook and Cut opens the Nape to a comrade: a Break Attention made by the soldier holding the Titan's Attention needs only 1 success (plus 1 for a Feint, plus 1 per decoy in a row), and the cut follows before any card can intervene.

Playtest rules Playtest rule

"Outside the Walls, the ride decides who comes home. Inside them, the paperwork does."Supply sergeant, Trost district

These are the minimum rules for the first playtest: riding an Expedition, spending Downtime inside the Walls, asking Command for gear, and fighting people.

The GM writes the Mission Brief as Command, may frame a Waypoint scene between Legs, names the Circumstances of your rolls, may add a row to the Requisition list, and may direct a Foe that has a reason, said aloud. Everything else stands as written: the hazard and Night rolls and their rows, the Straggler's victim, the Leg roll, whether Command grants a Requisition, a Foe's Attack Dice and Guard, damage, and the tests that end a Skirmish. The GM rolls hazards, a Foe's Attack Dice, and Guard in the open, and never softens, swaps, or changes a roll, a row, or a result.

Expeditions

An Expedition is a Survey Corps operation beyond the Walls, ridden as a chain of Legs between Waypoints. The Squad always reaches the next Waypoint. A failed Leg roll costs more rations and makes the Leg's hazard worse, but it never stops the ride.

The route

The route is set once, before the Expedition begins, and nothing changes it afterwards. It is the route of the Mission Brief, which the GM writes as Command, and which names:

  • the Waypoints in order, each with its kind and whether it is a Depot, the last always a gate of the Walls;
  • for the Leg that leads to each Waypoint, its Distance Band (Near, Far, or Deep) and its Formation Post;
  • the days, counted from the Expedition's first, on which Command orders a Hard Ride;
  • the Abnormal the hazard tables' Abnormal rows name, when it is not the Sprinting Abnormal.

Bounds. A Brief's route has 4 to 6 Legs, the interim route's count. No Leg is deeper than the interim route gives its place: the first and last Legs are Near, the second and second-to-last are Near or Far, and every Leg between them is Near, Far, or Deep. At most one Waypoint is a Depot, and the gate never is. Formation Posts and Hard Ride days are Command's to name, a Hard Ride at most once a day and only as the day's third Leg. The route sets each Leg's Distance Band, Formation Post, and Pace, which the hazard roll then reads as written; once the Expedition begins, no ruling changes them. A route outside these bounds is not a Brief's route in this playtest: the GM writes within them, or rolls the interim route below.

The interim route: when there is no Brief, or the Brief names no route

  1. Roll D3+3 for the number of Legs, and so of Waypoints. A D3 is a D6 read 1 or 2 as 1, 3 or 4 as 2, and 5 or 6 as 3.
  2. Give each Leg a Distance Band by its place on the route: the first and last Legs are Near, the second and second-to-last are Far, and every Leg between them is Deep.
  3. Roll D6 on the Waypoint kinds table for each Waypoint but the last, which is the gate. No Waypoint is a Depot.
  4. Roll D6 on the Formation Post table for each Leg.
  5. Pace is not rolled now. After each day's second Leg, if a Leg is left, roll D6: on 5 or 6, Command orders a Hard Ride.
Formation Post (D6)
D6Formation Post
1Vanguard
2Flank Scout
3Signal Relay
4Center
5Supply Wagon
6Rear Guard
Waypoint kinds
Waypoint kindField ratingInterim route (D6)
Open plainOpen1
HedgerowsSparse2
ForestWooded3–4
Abandoned townUrban5
Giant forestGiant Forest6
Gate of the Wallsnone; roll it on the setup tablenever rolled; always the route's last Waypoint
  • Kind. A Waypoint's kind sets the field rating of a Titan Engagement on the Leg that leads to it, and the field's zones are generated from it. The gate sets none, so a Titan Engagement on the Leg to the gate rolls its field rating (see Setting up a Titan Engagement).
  • Depot. A Waypoint the Brief marks as a Depot keeps its kind. When the Squad arrives there, every soldier receives a full Standard Issue together, and Squad Supply is restocked (see Standard Issue and Squad Supply).
  • The gate. Reaching the gate, the route's last Waypoint, ends the Expedition at the end of that Leg, with no further Leg and no Night Camp. Downtime begins.

Before and during an Expedition

  • Before it begins, every soldier in the Squad receives a full Standard Issue together, and Squad Supply is restocked, rations included.
  • No interim day and no interim issue happen while an Expedition is under way. The Night Camp's day, the issue before the Expedition, and a Depot's issue take their place.
  • Gas and gear. No Gas Roll is made on a Leg or at a Night Camp; gas is spent only in a Titan Engagement. Canisters, Blade Sets, and Squad Supply come back only through a full Standard Issue, so what a Titan Engagement uses up stays used up until the next Depot or the next Expedition, except the Squad Supply a successful called search finds, which Squad Supply caps.
  • Horses. On a Leg, every soldier who is not Down and whose own horse is not lame is mounted. At a Night Camp every soldier is dismounted. A Down soldier, or a soldier whose horse is lame, rides in the wagon or across a comrade's saddle and is not mounted.
  • Down soldiers cannot be the Lead, Help a Leg roll or the camp roll, or make the camp roll.
  • When no one can roll. A Leg roll or a camp roll that no soldier on the Expedition can make fails, with no Help and no Push: when every living soldier is Down, or, on a Hard Ride, when no soldier who is not Down is mounted on their own horse. Nothing else changes. The Squad still reaches the Waypoint, the Down ride in the wagon, and a hazard row that names the Lead does nothing.
  • Who takes part. Every living soldier in the Squad. A new character who joins the Squad during an Expedition joins at the next Waypoint.
  • Promotion and Retirement that fall due on a Leg or at a Night Camp happen when that Leg or Night Camp ends, after any Titan Engagement, Skirmish, care window, or day passing inside it has ended.
  • Talents and Drives. A Talent limited to once per Titan Engagement can also be used once per Leg and once per Night Camp where its trigger can occur, such as Sure Seat on a Hard Ride. A Titan Engagement or a Skirmish that begins inside a Leg or a Night Camp has its own use: a use in the fight does not spend the Leg's or the Night Camp's, and a use in the Leg or the Night Camp does not spend the fight's. A Drive's turn tests count no Leg roll and no camp roll.

The day

A day holds two Legs and a Night Camp. Legs are never ridden at night.

  1. Ride the day's first Leg, then its second. After each, the GM may frame a Waypoint scene.
  2. If a Leg is left on the route after the second, and Command orders a Hard Ride, ride that Leg as the day's third. Command orders one on a day the Brief lists; on the interim route, roll D6 after the day's second Leg, and a 5 or 6 orders one. A day's first or second Leg is never a Hard Ride. After a Hard Ride, the GM may frame a Waypoint scene.
  3. Make the Night Camp.

Every Leg ridden counts toward the day's Legs, including a Leg ridden again after a retreat, whether the retreat came on a Leg, in a Waypoint scene, or at a Night Camp. A Leg ridden again as the next day's first, after a retreat in a Waypoint scene, counts toward that day's Legs.

Pace
PaceLeg rollRationsAdds to the hazard rollWhen
Steadythe Formation Post's entryas the Leg roll gives+0Every Leg that is not a Hard Ride.
Hard RideRide2 times what the Leg roll gives+1A third Leg squeezed in before dark, after the day's second Leg, when a Leg is left on the route and Command orders it.

Formation Posts

The whole Squad rides at the Formation Post the route gives each Leg, so the Post changes only at a Waypoint. The Post names the entry the Leg roll uses on a Steady Leg and what it adds to the hazard roll.

Formation Posts
Formation PostLeg roll on a Steady LegAdds to the hazard rollThe postSpecial
VanguardSpot+1The first to see, and the first seen.none
Flank ScoutSurvive+1Reading the land at the formation's edge.none
Signal RelaySpot+0Watching the sky for flares.Once per Leg, before the hazard roll, the Squad may spend 1 flare of Squad Supply to lower that Leg's hazard total by 2.
CenterEndure−1The long hours in the saddle.none
Supply WagonSurvive+0Keeping the wagons whole.The Leg Hazard table's Dropped supplies row loses 2 units in place of 1.
Rear GuardSpot+0The last to leave.none

Riding a Leg

  1. Take your post. The Squad rides at the Formation Post the route gives this Leg.
  2. The Leg roll (below).
  3. Arrive. The Squad reaches the Leg's Waypoint. On a success, spend 1 ration, and the hazard roll gets −1 for each success beyond the first. On a failure, spend 2 rations, and the hazard roll gets +1. A Hard Ride doubles the rations. A Squad that holds fewer rations than it must spend meets hunger.
  4. The hazard. At the Signal Relay Post the Squad may first spend its flare. Roll the hazard and resolve its row.
  5. Harm. Harm a row inflicts outside a Titan Engagement opens a care window, with every soldier on the Expedition in its scope (see Treat Injury).
  6. A Titan Engagement a row begins starts as When a hazard begins a Titan Engagement gives. If it became a retreat before it ended, the Squad falls back to the Waypoint this Leg started from and rides this Leg again from step 1, with a new Leg roll and a new hazard.
  7. The Waypoint. The Leg ends. At a Depot, every soldier receives a full Standard Issue together. At the gate, the Expedition ends.

Waypoint scenes Playtest rule

After a Leg ends at a Waypoint that is not the gate, and before the next Leg or the Night Camp, the GM may frame a Waypoint scene there: searching an abandoned town, finding survivors, crossing a flooded ford.

  • It runs on called rolls, each with its Circumstances and its stakes named before the dice (see When to roll). A successful called search adds Squad Supply only as Squad Supply allows, and a Waypoint scene yields Squad Supply to at most two called searches.
  • A Skirmish or a Titan Engagement may begin in it by the fiction, as scene framing starts one.
  • Harm a called roll's stakes inflict opens a care window, with every soldier on the Expedition in its scope (see Treat Injury).
  • A retreat. If a Titan Engagement begun in a Waypoint scene becomes a retreat before it ends, the scene ends and the Squad falls back as from a fight a hazard row of the Leg that reached this Waypoint began: to the Waypoint that Leg started from, riding that Leg again from its first step with a new Leg roll and a new hazard. Arriving at this Waypoint again works as any arrival, a Depot's Standard Issue included. The Leg ridden again takes the day's next Leg slot: after the day's first Leg it is the second, at Steady; after the second it is the third only if Command orders a Hard Ride that day, and it is then ridden as a Hard Ride. If no slot remains, the Squad falls back to the Waypoint that Leg started from and makes the Night Camp there as on any day, which is then the camp's Waypoint for the night hazard and for a Titan Engagement the Night table begins (see The Night Camp), and the next day's first Leg rides that Leg again at Steady.
  • What it never does. A Waypoint scene rides no Leg. It makes no Leg roll or camp roll, reads no Leg Hazard or Night table, spends no ration unless a called roll's stakes name one, and never stands in place of a hazard roll. A danger the GM authors belongs in a Waypoint scene; a rolled hazard row is described as it came up, never softened or swapped.

The Leg roll

  • Who rolls. The Lead: one soldier on the Expedition who is not Down, chosen by the players, using the roll-off if they disagree. On a Hard Ride the Lead must be mounted on their own horse.
  • Entry. On a Steady Leg, the Post's entry: Spot, Survive, or Endure. On a Hard Ride, Ride, with the horse's Gear Dice, so a Pushed Ride wears the horse.
  • Needs 1 success.
  • Help. Up to 3 comrades on the Expedition who are not Down. Help spends nothing, and each soldier Helps at most once per Leg.
  • Push and Cover as usual (see Pushing and Covering). The Leg roll is never tried again; a Leg ridden again after a retreat makes a new Leg roll.
  • With no soldier who can make the roll, on a Steady Leg or a Hard Ride, the Leg roll fails, with no Help and no Push.

The Leg Hazard table

Roll D6 in the open and add every line below. The hazard roll is a table roll: no pool, no Push, no Help, and it is never tried again. A row that lets the players choose is a shared choice, settled by the roll-off when they disagree.

What the hazard roll adds
The hazard roll addsAmount
The Leg's Distance BandNear +0, Far +1, Deep +2
Each Night Camp already made on this Expedition+1
The Formation Postas the Formation Posts table gives
The PaceSteady +0, Hard Ride +1
The Leg roll failed+1
The Leg roll succeeded−1 for each success beyond the first
The Squad spent the Signal Relay's flare−2
Leg Hazard table (D6, plus every line above)
TotalHazardWhat happens
1 or lessQuiet rideNothing happens.
2Broken groundThe Lead's horse takes 1 point of wear. If there is no Lead, or the Lead is not mounted, nothing happens.
3Dropped suppliesSquad Supply loses 1 unit of one kind that holds a unit, chosen by the players; at the Supply Wagon Post it loses 2 units of that kind, or all it holds if fewer. If no kind holds a unit, nothing happens.
4Long hoursEvery soldier on the Expedition gains 1 Stress.
5ThrownOne mounted soldier, chosen by the players, falls from a horse (see Falls). If no soldier is mounted, nothing happens.
6Titans far offThe players choose: the Squad spends 1 flare and 1 ration and rides around them, or a Titan Engagement begins with every soldier at Distant. If Squad Supply holds no flare or no ration, the Titan Engagement begins.
7Titan on the formationA Titan Engagement begins with every soldier at Distant.
8StragglerRoll the Straggler's victim. The victim makes an Endure roll; on a failure they gain a Bite Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location, which can be lethal. Then a Titan Engagement begins with the victim at In Reach and every other soldier at Distant.
9AbnormalA Titan Engagement begins with every soldier at Distant, against the Sprinting Abnormal, or the Abnormal the Mission Brief names.
10 or moreOverrunA Titan Engagement begins with every soldier at In Reach and two Background Titans, on clocks of 4 and 8.

The Straggler

The Straggler row picks its victim at random. The pick is a roll, never a choice.

  1. Kind. Roll D6: on 1 to 4 the victim is a Squadmate, on 5 or 6 a player character. Only living soldiers on the Expedition who are not Down count. If none of that kind counts, use the other kind. If neither kind has one, resolve the row as Titan on the formation instead.
  2. Soldier. Number the soldiers of that kind who count from 1 upward, in Squad sheet order, and roll D6, rolling again any result above the count.
  3. Endure. The victim makes an Endure roll. It needs 1 success, cannot be Helped or Covered, can be Pushed, and is never tried again.
  4. On a failure, the victim gains a Bite Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location, which can be lethal. It rolls no Attack Dice, no Reaction answers it, and its Critical Injury roll adds nothing for Net Successes. It counts as gained as the row's Titan Engagement begins: no separate care window is held for it, and its time limit runs as one gained in that Titan Engagement.
  5. Then the Titan Engagement begins, with the victim at In Reach and every other soldier at Distant.

When a hazard begins a Titan Engagement

  • Field rating. On a Leg, the kind of the Waypoint this Leg leads to sets it. From the Night table, the kind of the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached sets it. A gate sets none, so it is rolled.
  • What the row names is used as written: where soldiers start, the Focus Titan, and any Background Titans and their clocks. A soldier the row does not place starts at Distant, in the Squad's start zone.
  • Everything else the row leaves unnamed, such as the Focus Titan's Size Class, the GM rolls on the setup tables, and the retreat clock has 8 segments (see Setting up a Titan Engagement).
  • Who takes part. Every living soldier on the Expedition. Down soldiers take part where the row places them. On a Leg, soldiers stay mounted or dismounted as they rode. At night every soldier is dismounted.
  • Then the Titan Engagement starts as usual (see Starting a Titan Engagement).

The Night Camp

The Night Camp is the stop after a day's Legs.

The Night Camp, in order

  1. The camp roll. One soldier on the Expedition who is not Down, chosen by the players, rolls Survive. It needs 1 success. Up to 3 comrades on the Expedition who are not Down may Help, spending nothing. It can be Pushed and Covered, and is never tried again. If no soldier on the Expedition can make it, it fails, with no Help and no Push.
  2. Rations. Spend 1 ration. A Squad that holds none meets hunger.
  3. Camp Relief. If the camp roll succeeded and the camp did not meet hunger, every soldier on the Expedition loses 1 Stress.
  4. The night hazard. Roll D6 in the open, add the Distance Band of the day's last Leg as the Leg Hazard table does, and add 1 if the camp roll failed. Nothing else adds to it. Read the Night table. A Titan Engagement it begins takes its field rating from the kind of the Waypoint the camp is made at: the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached, or, after a retreat in a Waypoint scene that left no Leg slot, the Waypoint that Leg started from. The band added above is still the day's last ridden Leg's, whatever the retreat. A camp at the gate the Expedition left, after a retreat on the day's first Leg that left no slot, rolls its field rating on the setup table, as a gate gives none, and the Expedition goes on. If a Titan Engagement the row begins becomes a retreat before it ends, the Squad falls back overnight (see A retreat at night).
  5. A day passes, with a care window that holds every soldier on the Expedition in its scope, in which Field Repair is rolled; then the Death Rolls of Day limits; then Health lost to damage comes back; then a day of healing (see Healing and Field Repair).

A retreat at night. If a Titan Engagement the Night table begins becomes a retreat before it ends, the Squad falls back overnight to the Waypoint the day's last Leg started from, and the Night Camp goes on there at step 5, with no new camp roll, ration, or night hazard. It still counts as a Night Camp made on this Expedition. Falling back is not riding a Leg: it makes no Leg roll, spends nothing, and reads no Leg hazard. The next day's first Leg rides that Leg again, from its first step, with a new Leg roll and a new hazard, and counts toward that day's Legs. A Titan Engagement the Night table begins that ends any other way leaves the camp where it was. If the camp is already at the Waypoint the day's last Leg started from, the Squad falls back no further: it breaks off where it is, the camp goes on at step 5 with no new camp roll, ration, or night hazard, the night counts as the one Night Camp already counted, and the Leg queued for the morning is ridden as the next day's first, at Steady, unchanged.

Titans do not walk at night. Only an Abnormal does.

Night table (D6, plus the Distance Band of the day's last Leg, plus 1 if the camp roll failed)
TotalNightWhat happens
3 or lessQuiet nightNothing happens.
4RestlessEvery soldier on the Expedition gains 1 Stress.
5SpoiledSquad Supply loses 2 rations, or all it holds if fewer.
6Spooked horsesOne horse on the Expedition that is not lame, chosen by the players, goes lame. If none, nothing happens.
7ProwlersAt Near or Far, a Skirmish begins against three Bandits who have the Ambush and carry firebrands in place of clubs. At Deep, resolve Restless instead.
8 or moreAbnormal at nightA Titan Engagement begins against the Sprinting Abnormal, or the Abnormal the Mission Brief names, with every soldier dismounted at Distant. If it becomes a retreat before it ends, the Squad falls back overnight.

Rations and hunger

Squad Supply holds rations by Funding: at Funding 3 the stock is 8 rations (see Squad Supply). A Leg spends them by its Leg roll and its Pace, a Night Camp spends 1, and the Titans far off and Spoiled rows spend or lose them.

When a Leg or a Night Camp must spend rations and Squad Supply holds fewer than it must spend, the Squad meets hunger. It spends every ration it holds, and every soldier on the Expedition gains 1 Stress, with no roll. A Night Camp that meets hunger gives no Camp Relief.

Every roll on an Expedition

Rolls on an Expedition. None of them is ever tried again.

Circumstances apply to the Leg roll, the camp roll, the Straggler's Endure roll, and every called roll in a Waypoint scene. The Straggler's Endure roll, and any other roll a row's own text calls for, takes the step in force when the hazard is rolled: Standard, unless the GM named one before then for a condition of that Leg that lasts. None is named for it after, and none for the danger the row itself describes, which the row prices. A Titan Engagement or a Skirmish a row begins takes its Circumstances as any fight does, including a step the GM names for a condition that begins during it. The hazard and night hazard rolls are table rolls and take none. Every need stands.

Downtime

Downtime is the time the Squad spends inside the Walls between Expeditions.

When it runs

  • It begins when the Squad reaches the gate that ends an Expedition, and runs until the next Expedition begins.
  • It spans seven days Playtest rule, which pass at the infirmary. Nothing else in Downtime takes days.
  • No interim day and no interim issue happen while it runs. Its seven days and the next Expedition's issue take their place.
  • Every soldier in the Squad takes part. Stress never falls below a soldier's minimum, and Grief never below 0.

Downtime, in order

  1. The infirmary (below).
  2. Downtime Actions. Each player character takes one Downtime Action, in the order the players choose, using the roll-off if they disagree. Each Squadmate takes the Squadmate relief instead.
  3. The Squad Action. The Squad takes one, chosen by the players, using the roll-off if they disagree. While the playtest configuration holds, Recruit is not offered, so the Squad Action is Honoring the Fallen.
  4. After the actions, in order:
    1. items and supplies Command granted by Requisition arrive;
    2. Prosthetics are fitted (see Lost limbs);
    3. medical Retirement is offered again (see Retirement);
    4. every Retirement and promotion that has fallen due resolves (see Promotion).

The infirmary

  1. One care window, with every soldier in the Squad in its scope (see Treat Injury). Field Repair is not rolled in it; in Downtime, Maintain Gear restores gear.
  2. Seven days, each resolved in this order:
    1. Every lethal Critical Injury with a Day limit gets one infirmary roll: 4 base dice, needing 1 success. It takes no Push, no Help, no Stress Dice, and no Talent, Gear, or Bonus Dice. A success stabilizes the Critical Injury, as a successful treatment does, without treating it.
    2. Every lethal Critical Injury that still has a Day limit causes a Death Roll.
    3. Every living soldier gets back all Health lost to damage.
    4. Every held Critical Injury's remaining healing time drops by 1 day, and one that reaches 0 heals. An untreated Critical Injury whose rider stops it healing untreated, such as a Pierce that holds the ball, does not drop, and a lethal Critical Injury cannot heal while it is still lethal (see Injury Types).

The seven days hold no care window of their own. A death on an infirmary day causes no Fear Roll. Its Grief is the Grief of a day passing, and promotion is timed as for a day passing (see Grief).

Downtime Actions and the Squad Action

A Downtime Action is what one player character does with their Downtime: one per Downtime. The Squad Action is the one thing the Squad does together: one per Downtime.

Downtime Actions (each player character takes one per Downtime)
Downtime ActionEffect
RecoverThe soldier loses 2 Stress.
Visit HavenThe soldier goes home to their Haven and loses 2 Stress and 2 Grief.
RequisitionThe soldier makes one Requisition, or Helps a comrade's Requisition roll with it (see Requisition).
Maintain GearEvery rated item the soldier holds returns to its rating: ODM Gear is no longer Jammed, a lame horse is no longer lame, and a worn kit or firearm is whole again. A ruined Blade Set stays ruined.

The Squadmate relief. Squadmates take no Downtime Action. Each Squadmate loses 2 Stress and 1 Grief.

Squad Actions (the Squad takes one per Downtime)
Squad ActionEffect
Honoring the FallenEvery soldier in the Squad loses 1 Grief.
RecruitNot offered while the playtest configuration holds, and it brings no Squadmate then. Otherwise, new Squadmates join until the Squad Pool holds 6 minus the number of player characters. The players choose each one's template and name it, and it joins as a starting Squadmate does, with Standard Issue (see Squadmates).

Every roll in Downtime

Rolls in Downtime. The Requisition roll and Size Up on the quartermaster are under Requisition.
RollWho rollsDice and needsHelp and PushTried again
Infirmary rollRolled for the patient, Down or not; no soldier rolls it.4 base dice, with no Talent, Gear, Stress, or Bonus Dice. Needs 1.Never.Once per Critical Injury per infirmary day, and again on each later day while it is still lethal.

Requisition

Requisition is how a soldier gets gear beyond Standard Issue. There are no prices. Funding decides what Command will consider, and the item's Scarcity and your roll decide whether you get it.

When

Requisition is a Downtime Action. Each player character may make one Requisition per Downtime. It is never made before a campaign's first Expedition, and never outside Downtime, so the first playtest rides out on Standard Issue alone. Requisition starts at the first Downtime.

Scarcity and the Funding gate

Every row of the list has a Scarcity. Funding sets which Scarcity Command will consider, and the Scarcity sets the successes the roll needs before the Ledger. In this playtest, Funding is 3 (see Standard Issue).

Scarcity
ScarcitySuccesses needed, plus the Ledger
Standard1
Limited2
Rare3
The Funding gate
FundingScarcity Command will consider
1–2Standard
3–4Standard, Limited
5–6Standard, Limited, Rare

Making a Requisition

  1. Name one row the Funding gate allows, with any choice the row asks for, and the soldier the item is for.
  2. Roll Persuade or Recall, chosen before rolling: talking the quartermaster round, or citing the regulation and the officer who signed it.
    • Needs: the row's Scarcity, plus the Ledger.
    • Help: up to 3 comrades in the Squad who are not Down. A player character Helps by taking Requisition as their Downtime Action and spending it on the Help. A Squadmate Helps at most once per Downtime and spends nothing.
    • Circumstances: the GM may name them for leverage you bring, such as a favour owed, a Commendation, or a captured specimen. Scarcity and the Ledger are needs and never change.
    • Push and Cover as usual. Stress gained here rides out on the next Expedition.
    • Never tried again this Downtime: it was the soldier's one Requisition.
  3. On a success, Command grants the row, and the Ledger rises by 1. On a failure, Command says no, and nothing else happens. Successes beyond the needs do nothing.

The Ledger and Size Up

  • The Ledger is Command's patience within one Downtime. It starts at 0 and counts the Requisitions Command has granted the Squad this Downtime.
  • Size Up on the quartermaster. Once per Downtime, before any Requisition roll, one soldier in the Squad who is not Down, a Squadmate included, chosen by the players, may roll Size Up. It needs 1 success, cannot be Helped or Covered, can be Pushed, and is never tried again. It is not a Downtime Action. On a success, the Ledger counts 1 lower for the whole Squad for the rest of this Downtime, never below 0.

What a grant gives

  • Items belong to the soldier the Requisition named and are Kept Items (see Kept Items). A Kept Item counts as items, and can be passed, taken, and shared out like any item of its kind. Standard Issue never takes it away unless the soldier hands it in at an exchange.
  • A Blade Set rated above 1 is still ruined by any wear. ODM Gear or a horse replaces the soldier's own, which leaves play.
  • Squad Supply units are added above the stock. They are not Kept Items.
  • A grant arrives at the after-actions step of this Downtime, before Prosthetics are fitted and before the next Standard Issue.

The playtest Requisition list Playtest rule

The GM may add a row for their table: a canon item of the era, at a Scarcity the era allows. An added ODM Gear, Blade Set, horse, or firearm is at least Limited, and every added row is marked untested. An added row changes nothing else: the Funding gate, the Ledger, one Requisition per Downtime, and Command's answer once the roll is made stand.
