Skirmishes

A Skirmish is a scene in which soldiers fight people: Military Police, bandits, a Garrison picket. People roll their attacks, can be blocked, can be talked down, and hurt a soldier with damage, never with a Critical Injury directly. No Titan takes part, and Titan Engagements do not change.

Starting a Skirmish

  • What starts one. A rule that names a Skirmish, such as the Night table's Prowlers row or a Mission Brief, or scene framing that sets the Squad against people, as scene framing starts a Titan Engagement. A Waypoint scene, or the stakes of a failed called roll, may begin one by the fiction.
  • What the start names. One Foe kind from the Foe table, and, if it says so, how many Foes, which side has the Ambush, that it is night, and which soldiers take part. One Foe group takes part in a playtest Skirmish. Its number is the start's, or else the kind's group size. A Skirmish that begins at a Night Camp is at night.
  • Who takes part. The soldiers the start names. If it names none, every living soldier in the Squad on the same Expedition, or every living soldier in the Squad when no Expedition is under way.

Setting out

  1. Set out the Foe group: its Foes labelled from 1 upward, each at full Health, with its weapons, and its firearms loaded.
  2. Every soldier taking part is Apart from every Foe, unless the start says otherwise.
  3. Settle the Ambush.
  4. Round 1 begins.

Engaged and Apart

  • You are Engaged with a Foe while within arm's reach of it, and Apart from it otherwise, separately for each Foe. Engaged and Apart are not Positions. A Skirmish has no field, no zones, no Positions, no Anchor Rating, no ODM move, no Fly roll a rule calls for, and no airborne soldier. The one Fly roll in a Skirmish is a called roll the GM names for Fly (see A called roll, below), and it makes no soldier airborne.
  • Your move does one of these: you close in and become Engaged with one Foe you name; you break away and become Apart from every Foe you are Engaged with; or you stay as you are. A move may also include one mount or one dismount.
  • The move of a soldier who holds a Foe, and of a Down soldier, changes nothing.
  • A Foe becomes Engaged or Apart only through the foe rule.

The Ambush

  • From the start. A start may give the Ambush to the Foes, as the Prowlers row does.
  • Trying for it. Before round 1 of a Skirmish whose start does not give the Foes the Ambush, the GM rules whether the Squad can approach unseen at all. If it cannot, no side has the Ambush. If it can, one soldier taking part who is not Down, chosen by the players, rolls Sneak, with the Circumstances the GM names. It needs the Foe group's Watch, cannot be Helped or Covered, can be Pushed, and is never tried again. On a success the Squad has the Ambush; on a failure no side has it.
  • What it does. A soldier or a Foe has not yet acted until its first card of the Skirmish has come up.
    • In round 1, every card of the side with the Ambush comes up before every card of the other side. Cards within a side keep their order.
    • A soldier's Fight or Shoot roll against a Foe that has not yet acted gains 2 Bonus Dice. A Foe with the Ambush rolls 2 more Attack Dice against a soldier who has not yet acted.
    • An attack by the side with the Ambush on a target that has not yet acted cannot be answered. The soldier makes no Reaction and the Foe rolls no Guard, so every success of the attack counts.

Rounds and turns

  • Cards. At the start of each round, deal initiative cards as in a Titan Engagement (see The round): one to each soldier taking part, living, Down or not, and one to the Foe group while any of its Foes is in the Skirmish. Cards come up lowest first. There are no Wings and no swap step.
  • A soldier's card. The soldier takes one turn of one move and one action, in either order. A Squadmate takes its turn on its own card and never Pushes or Covers.
  • The Foe group's card. Each Foe still in the Skirmish takes one turn, in label order, by the foe rule.
  • End of the round. Every Foe of a group that broke leaves. Then check the ending.
  • Turns count. Your turn in a Skirmish counts for every rule that counts turns: a turn time limit, spending a turn, and a Drive's turn tests (see Turns).

What a soldier can do

Your action in a Skirmish is one of these:

  • Fight (Strength) against a Foe you are Engaged with, with the Blade Set in your handles for Gear Dice, or bare-handed or with an object that is not a gear item, which gives none. A Fight declared as a Grapple takes no Gear Dice.
  • Shoot (Agility) with a loaded firearm, which gives its Gear Dice, against a Foe the weapon can target. A musket is never fired at a Foe you are Engaged with. The firearm is empty after the roll, and a Pushed Gear Die showing 1 wears it. You may also Shoot a flare at a person, spending 1 flare of Squad Supply and no Shot, with no Gear Dice.
  • Reload one empty firearm, spending 1 Shot of Squad Supply and your action, and, for a musket, your move as well.
  • Parley or Size Up (below).
  • Help, Treat Injury, Rally, Lift Comrade, Pass Item, Take Item, or Change Canister. Treat Injury and Rally work as in a Titan Engagement, with no Position requirement: the patient or the Rallied comrade is any other soldier taking part, or yourself where those rules allow it, and a failed use can be tried again on a later turn (see Treat Injury and Rally).

Release ends a hold you have on a Foe, with no roll, spending nothing. Swap Blade Set, Shed Load, and Mount or Dismount work as the Gear chapter gives them. No entry used only in a Titan Engagement can be taken (see the Action Catalog).

An improvised act. You may attempt an act no entry covers. An act you describe that is not an entry taken as written is an improvised act, even when it would make a change an entry makes. Before you build the pool, the GM names as its model entry one entry marked as an action that this section allows, never Block or Dodge, never a roll or a fixed roll, and never an option, which rules out Reload, Release, Swap Blade Set, Shed Load, and Mount or Dismount, and that ruling stands. The act spends your action, is rolled as the model entry is, takes Circumstances as any roll, carries the model entry's restrictions (a Foe's Guard still rolls against it), and produces only the model entry's effect at its size (see When the steps do not resolve an action). An object that is not a gear item, thrown at a Foe Engaged or Apart, is modelled on Shoot, at damage 1 and Crush, with no Gear Dice.

A called roll. An act no entry marked as an action this section allows could model, and that would change nothing the Skirmish tracks (no Foe's Health, Guard, Grit, Parley value, Attack Dice, or Watch, no Engaged or Apart, no card), is a called roll (see When to roll). It spends your action, on your turn, and never stands in for a Reaction; Help on it is Help in a Skirmish (below), each helper spending their action, at no cost the GM names; a fall it stakes is low or high, never extreme; a cost of time or position changes only the situation outside what the Skirmish tracks; and success never gives an effect an entry has. You can try it again on a later turn.

The GM may name Fly for a called roll in a Skirmish, as for any called roll outside a Titan Engagement. It is the one Fly roll a Skirmish has: it takes the ODM Gear's Gear Dice, as a Dodge made with ODM Gear does here, and a Pushed Gear Die showing 1 wears the gear; it makes no soldier airborne, makes no Gas Roll, and ODM Gear that Jams on it drops no one (see Jam); and it gives no Engaged or Apart, which only a move changes. A soldier whose ODM Gear is Jammed cannot be named for Fly; the GM names Agility alone.

Help on a roll in a Skirmish comes from up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action, whether Engaged or Apart.

Attacks

Every attack in a Skirmish

  1. The attacker rolls. A soldier rolls Fight or Shoot with a full pool and may Push before any cancelling roll is made. A Foe rolls its Attack Dice in the open: base dice that succeed on 6, never Pushed, with no other dice. The attack's successes are its Severity, announced.
  2. The target's cancelling roll. A soldier may make a Reaction, a Block or a Dodge (below). A Foe rolls its Guard: its Guard dice, rolled in the open as base dice, never Pushed, spending nothing, against every attack on it.
  3. Cancel. Each success of the cancelling roll cancels one success of the attack. The successes left are the attack's Net Successes. The attack lands on 1 or more Net Successes and does nothing on 0. Against a target who makes no cancelling roll, every success of the attack is net.

Reactions against a Foe

  • Block (Strength, with a Blade Set if you have one; otherwise with no Gear Dice) answers a Fight attack. Dodge (Agility, with ODM Gear, or with your horse while mounted) answers a Fight attack or a shot.
  • A Reaction spends a turn, as it does against a Titan. You make at most one Reaction against each Foe per round. Its successes cancel against the attack it answered and, separately, against each later attack that Foe makes on you that round, and are never used up. A Block cancels only Fight attacks.
  • The reacting side Pushes last, after the Foe's roll is known.
  • Your Reaction takes the Circumstances step in force when the Foe group's card comes up, and no step is named or changed for it after.
  • No Reaction answers an attack the Ambush leaves unanswered, and a state or result that forbids Reactions forbids them here too.

Damage

  • An attack that lands deals its weapon's damage, plus 1 for each Net Success beyond the first, with the weapon's Injury Type. A Grapple deals none.
  • On a soldier, it is damage at a rolled Injury Location. It marks Health lost, and at 0 current Health the soldier is Down and gains a Critical Injury of the weapon's Injury Type (see Health and Critical Injuries). A person never inflicts a Critical Injury directly.
  • Cut and Pierce reach a soldier from a Foe's weapon in a Skirmish, or as damage a called roll's stakes named (see Harm a ruling names), and each carries its rider: a lethal Cut bleeds, so its Death Roll comes at the end of each of your turns until it is treated, and a Pierce from the 7 row up holds the ball, so it is harder to treat and does not heal until treated (see Injury Types).
  • On a Foe, damage lowers its Health, never below 0. A Foe has no Critical Injuries, no Stress, and no Down state. At 0 Health it is out of the Skirmish: dead if the damage was Cut, Pierce, or Burn, and out cold if it was Crush. A Foe out of the Skirmish takes no further part and is never a target again in it.

Weapons

Skirmish weapons
WeaponAttackDamageInjury TypeTargetNotes

Your own weapons are a Blade Set, bare hands, a firearm, and a flare. A sabre, a knife, a club, or a firebrand in your hands is an object that is not a gear item. Firearms have no use in a Titan Engagement.

The Grapple, Held, and Release

  • A Grapple is a Fight roll against a Foe you are Engaged with, declared as a Grapple before rolling, with no Gear Dice. Talents that name Fight apply. If it lands, the Foe is Held by you, and it deals no damage.
  • A Held Foe cannot move and attacks only its holder, bare-handed. It still rolls Guard.
  • The holder cannot move, Shoot, or Reload, and takes no action but a bare-handed Fight against the Held Foe, or Help. At any point of their turn they may take Release, which spends nothing and ends the hold.
  • A hold ends when the holder takes Release, becomes Down, or dies, or when the Foe is out of the Skirmish. A Foe never Breaks Free and never releases.
  • A soldier Held by a person cannot move or Dodge, attacks only the holder, bare-handed, and ends the hold with Break Free: a roll on their action that needs 2 successes, which up to 3 comrades taking part may Help. No playtest Foe Grapples, so this rule waits for later Foes.

Foes

Every Foe of a kind shares its row. Attack Dice and Guard are base dice that succeed on 6, and they take no Circumstances. Grit is how many of the group must be out of the Skirmish before it breaks, at the latest. Parley is the base a Parley against the group needs. Watch is what a Sneak roll for the Ambush needs. The group size is used when the start names no number. The three kinds below are this playtest's whole list: a Foe kind of the GM's own making waits for the full game, so the GM adds none.

Foe table

Garrison sentryBare hands, or an object that is not a gear itemMusketA Garrison soldier on picket duty.

The foe rule Playtest rule

The foe rule is the default: every Foe acts by it unless the GM directs one.

  • Directing a Foe. On a Foe's turn, when the fiction gives that Foe a reason, the GM may direct its target or its action in place of the rule's pick, and says the reason aloud before the Foe acts: the sergeant goes for the medic who shot his man. A reason the table cannot hear is not a reason.
  • What a directed Foe still does. It takes only a turn the steps below can give (it Fights, Shoots, reloads, closes in and Fights, or does nothing), with its kind's weapons, and never attacks a Down soldier. A Held Foe still acts only as step 1 gives. It never leaves the Skirmish alone: a Foe leaves only at 0 Health or when its group breaks or surrenders, so a GM who wants a Foe to run breaks its group sooner, with a reason said aloud.
  • What stays as written. A Foe's Attack Dice and Guard, rolled in the open and never adjusted; damage and Net Successes; whether a Foe at 0 Health is dead or out cold; and that a Foe never Pushes, Helps, or Covers.

On the Foe group's card, each Foe still in the Skirmish that has not broken takes one turn, in label order. Take the first step whose test the Foe meets.

  • Candidates: every soldier taking part who is alive and not Down.
  • Last attacker: of the candidates, the one who most recently made a Fight or Shoot roll against this Foe, landed or not.
  • Lowest card: of the candidates a step names, the one whose initiative card this round is lowest.
  1. The Foe is Held. It Fights its holder, bare-handed.
  2. The Foe is Engaged with a candidate. It Fights with its Fight weapon: its last attacker, if they are Engaged with it; otherwise the Engaged candidate with the lowest card.
  3. The Foe has a loaded firearm and at least one candidate. It Shoots with its Shoot weapon at its last attacker, if any; otherwise at the candidate with the lowest card.
  4. The Foe has an empty firearm. It reloads.
  5. The Foe has at least one candidate. It becomes Engaged with the candidate with the lowest card and Fights them with its Fight weapon.
  6. None of the above. Nothing.

A Foe never Pushes, Helps, Covers, Grapples, Breaks Free, Releases, Parleys, Sizes Up, or makes a Reaction. A Foe has no Stress, makes no Fear Roll, and has no Stress Response.

Guard. A Foe's Guard is its cancelling roll. When your Fight or Shoot roll against the Foe is final, the GM rolls the Foe's Guard dice in the open as base dice. Each success cancels one of your attack's successes, and the attack lands on 1 or more Net Successes. Guard is rolled against every attack on the Foe, however many come in a round, and a Held Foe still rolls it. It never takes a Push, Help, Bonus Dice, Stress Dice, or Gear Dice, and it spends nothing.

A Foe's firearm is loaded when the Skirmish begins and empty after it Shoots. A Foe's Reload spends its turn and no Shot; a Foe never runs out.

Grit and breaking

  • When the number of the group's Foes out of the Skirmish reaches its Grit, the group breaks, at the latest. At once, every Foe of it still in the Skirmish that is not Held becomes Apart from every soldier and takes no further turn, and each leaves at the end of the round. A Held Foe stays Held and leaves at the end of the round its hold ends.
  • Sooner. The GM may break the group sooner, or have it surrender, with a reason said aloud as it happens (the officer falls; the sergeant sees the flare), never later. A group broken sooner breaks as above. A group that surrenders yields as the Surrender ask gives, and the Skirmish ends.
  • A failed Parley made as a threat raises the group's Grit by 1 and its Parley value by 1 for the rest of the Skirmish, so a failed threat hardens later Parleys.

Parley

  • When. On your action. Each soldier Parleys at most once per Skirmish.
  • The roll. Persuade (Empathy), or, as a threat, Strength in place of Empathy. A threat is still a Persuade roll, so a Talent that names Persuade adds its dice.
  • Needs. The group's Parley value (the Foe table) plus 1, minus 1 for each of its Foes out of the Skirmish, never below 1; then add the ask's number below.
  • Help from up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action. It can be Pushed and Covered.
  • On a success, the group yields: it does what the ask says, and the Skirmish ends. On a failure, nothing happens, except that a failed threat raises the group's Grit and its Parley value by 1 each.
Parley asks
AskAdds to the needsOn a success
Stand down+0The group leaves.
Let us pass+0The group lets the Squad go its way.
Answer+0The group answers one question truthfully, from what its Foes know.
Surrender+1The group lays down its arms and gives itself up to the Squad.

Outside a Skirmish. When a Mission Brief or scene framing sets the Squad before a Foe group from the Foe table with no Skirmish under way, each soldier may make one Parley with that group. It needs the group's Parley value plus the ask's number, cannot be Helped or Covered, can be Pushed, and is never tried again by that soldier with that group. On a success the group does what the ask says; on a failure nothing happens. Talking with anyone who is not a Foe group from the Foe table is a called roll.

Size Up

When you Size Up a Foe group, on your action, roll Size Up (Instinct). The whole Squad may try it once per Foe group per Skirmish. It needs 1 success, cannot be Helped or Covered, can be Pushed, and is never tried again. On a success, the GM states the group's Grit, how many more of its Foes must be out before it breaks, and what each of its Foes will do on the group's next card under the foe rule as things stand. For a Foe the GM already means to direct, the GM states instead what it will do and the reason.

What the GM states holds. Each Foe does what the GM stated on its turn on that card, unless something happens after the Size Up that changes it. For a Foe stated under the foe rule, that is a change in what the rule tests, after which the rule picks again, or a new reason the fiction gives it, said aloud. For a Foe stated as directed, that is a new reason, said aloud, or an action it can no longer take, after which the foe rule picks unless the GM directs it again with a reason said aloud. The GM never directs a Foe on that card for a reason held back at the Size Up.

Ending a Skirmish

A Skirmish ends when one of these is true:

  • Every Foe of the group is out of the Skirmish, has left, or has yielded.
  • The Squad leaves. At the end of a round, if every soldier taking part who is not Down is Apart from every Foe and holds no Foe, and every Down soldier taking part is carried by a comrade, the players may declare that the Squad leaves.
  • No soldier is standing. Every soldier taking part is Down or dead. The Foe group leaves and takes nothing.

When a Skirmish ends

Resolve the steps that follow the end of a Titan Engagement, with "Skirmish" in place of "Titan Engagement", outside the Skirmish (see When a Titan Engagement ends):

  1. Turns spent in advance, and pending results, are cancelled, and every ban on Reactions ends.
  2. Every soldier who took part loses 1 Stress.
  3. Every lasting Stress Response gained during the Skirmish ends.
  4. Every Turn limit becomes an Engagement limit.
  5. Aftermath rolls, with every soldier who took part counting as holding the patient's Position.
  6. The Death Rolls of Engagement limits. A death at this step causes no Fear Roll.
  7. A care window, with every soldier who took part in its scope.
  8. Grief for the deaths during the Skirmish, for every soldier who took part.
  9. Retirement, then promotion. Left items and dropped Blade Sets are shared out among the soldiers who took part (see When a soldier dies or retires).

Fear Rolls, Drives, Talents, and Squadmates

  • A comrade dies: every other soldier taking part who is alive makes the Fear Roll. A Down soldier makes none.
  • A first kill. You kill a person when damage you deal brings a Foe to 0 Health with Cut, Pierce, or Burn, a flare included. If you have never killed a person before, you make the Fear Roll for a first human kill when the kill resolves, and your sheet records it (see Fear Rolls). A Drive may shrug it off.
  • The GM's trigger. A Skirmish is outside a Titan Engagement, so the GM may call a Fear Roll in one: rarely, for an event at least as horrifying as a listed trigger, for the witnesses the GM names (see Fear Rolls). It is never called for an outcome a called roll's stakes named.
  • Drives. A Drive's turn tests count Skirmish turns. A Drive's Position triggers need a Titan Engagement and are never met in a Skirmish.
  • Talents. A Talent limited to once per Titan Engagement can also be used once per Skirmish. A Skirmish that begins inside a Leg or a Night Camp has its own use, and neither use spends the other. A Talent whose effect reads a Position does nothing in a Skirmish unless its row says what it does there, as Carrying Voice's does: you may target any soldier taking part. Hand-to-Hand adds its dice to Fight, with a Blade Set, bare-handed, or as a Grapple, and to Block. Blade Discipline also names Fight.
  • Squadmates take part like player characters, on their own cards, directed as usual (see Squadmates).

Every roll in a Skirmish

Example: a Block against a trooper's sabre

Round 2 of a Skirmish against a Military Police patrol. Private Hanne Roth (Health 5, Strength 3, Stress 1, a Blade Set rated 1 in her handles) is Engaged with Trooper 1, and she struck at it last round. The Foe group's card comes up first.

Trooper 1 attacks. By the foe rule it is Engaged with a candidate, and Hanne is its last attacker, so it Fights her with its sabre. The GM rolls its 7 Attack Dice in the open: 6, 6, 6, 5, 3, 2, 1. Only a 6 counts for a Foe, so the attack scores Severity 3.

Hanne Blocks. She has made no Reaction against Trooper 1 this round, and neither her move nor her action is spent, so the Block spends this round's turn. Her pool is 3 base dice, 1 Gear Die, and 1 Stress Die. She rolls base 6, 4, 2; Gear 6; Stress 3. Two successes. She does not Push.

Net Successes. 3 minus 2 leaves 1 Net Success, so the sabre lands. It deals its damage of 2, plus nothing, since there is no Net Success beyond the first: 2 Cut damage at a rolled Injury Location. Hanne's current Health falls from 5 to 3. Without the Block, all 3 successes would have been net: 2 + 2 = 4 damage, leaving her at 1 current Health, one graze from Down. Her 2 Block successes also cancel against every later attack Trooper 1 makes on her this round.

Card 9: Private Dieter Lang. Dieter is Engaged with Trooper 1 and Fights it with his Blade Set: Strength 4, Blade Set 1, Stress 1. He rolls base 6, 6, 3, 1; Gear 6; Stress 2. Three successes, and he does not Push. The GM rolls Trooper 1's Guard of 4 dice: 6, 5, 2, 1. One success cancels one of Dieter's, leaving 2 Net Successes. The Blade Set deals 2, plus 1 for the second Net Success: 3 Cut damage. Trooper 1 falls from Health 4 to 1.

Quick reference

Rolling

  • Pool: attribute + one dice Talent + Bonus Dice (4 at most) + Gear Dice from one item + Stress Dice. Every 6 succeeds. A Stress Die showing 1: one Stress Response, and no Push.
  • Called roll: before you build the pool, the GM names the entry (or an attribute alone), the Circumstances, and the stakes. Needs 1. Once rolled, nothing about it changes. Stakes that name damage or a fall come with your current Health said aloud; a fall a ruling names is low or high, never extreme, in a fight as well as outside one. In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish a called roll is only for an act no entry marked as an action could model that changes nothing the fight tracks: it spends your action, is made on your turn and never in place of a Reaction, its Help is the fight's, success on it never finds Squad Supply, a fall it stakes lands as any fall does, and you can try it again on a later turn.
  • Circumstances: Effortless +3, Routine +2, Easy +1 (Bonus Dice, under the cap); Standard nothing; Hard −1, Harsh −2, Desperate −3 (penalties after the cap, never below 1 base die). One step per roll, weighing every condition at once; never changes a need. A Reaction takes the step in force when the attacker's card comes up.
  • Help: +1 die each, 3 helpers at most. In a Titan Engagement: in the roller's zone or an adjacent zone, spending the helper's action. In a Skirmish: a comrade taking part who is not Down, Engaged or Apart, spending their action. Outside both: as the calling rule says, or on a called roll as the GM rules, where Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when the Help is declared. Help on a called roll in a fight is that fight's own Help, at no cost the GM names.
  • Push: re-roll every die not showing 6 except a Gear Die showing 1; +1 Stress and a new Stress Die, unless a comrade Covers it. A Pushed roll whose Gear Dice show any 1 wears its item by 1 point.
  • Cover: a player character takes the Push's Stress, and no Stress Die is added. In a Titan Engagement, from the Pushing soldier's zone or an adjacent zone; outside one, only a player character who could Help that roll. Squadmates never Cover.
  • Injury Location (D6): 1 left arm, 2 right arm, 3 left leg, 4 right leg, 5 torso, 6 head. No side given: odd left, even right.

Health and Down

  • Health: 2 plus half of Strength plus Agility, rounded up. Halve, round up, then add 2. It runs 4 to 6 on a standard build and as high as 8 at the Lifepath's ceiling. It never changes.
  • Each untreated Critical Injury crosses off 1 box, up to your Health. Damage marks the boxes left. Current Health is Health, minus the boxes crossed off, minus Health lost.
  • Down at 0 current Health, or on a row that says so. Damage that brings you to 0 also inflicts a Critical Injury of its Injury Type at once.
  • A Titan attack is always a Critical Injury and never damage, whatever your Health. Steam, a fall, and Corpse Heat are not Titan attacks.

A Titan attacks

  • Attack Dice are Titan Dice, rolled by the GM in the open: 5 or 6 succeeds. Titan Dice never Push and take no Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, Stress Dice, or Circumstances. The successes are the Severity; 0 is a whiff on everyone.
  • Dodge (Agility + ODM Gear, or the horse if mounted): each success cancels one. The card lands on 1 or more Net Successes. No dodge: every success is net.
  • One Reaction per Titan per round, spending your earliest turn whose move and action are unspent. It cancels against each of that Titan's cards, separately.
  • Critical Injury: +1 to the roll per Net Success beyond the first. Never for the Grab's crush, a fall, damage, or a Stress gain.
  • Knock loose: a target airborne, On Body, or at Blind Spot falls. A mounted target is not affected.
  • Wreck: the Titan's own zone drops one rating step (Urban or Giant Forest to Wooded, Wooded to Sparse, Sparse to Open), whether the card landed or whiffed.
  • Stride (Small 1, Medium 2, Large 2, Sprinting Abnormal 3, grounded 0): after the ladder and before the behavior, the Titan moves toward its Attention holder's zone, one zone closer each step, the lower number on a tie, and stops in their zone. On Body and Grabbed soldiers ride along; Blind Spot soldiers stay and detach. It never wrecks, harms, or marks.
  • Retargeting: when the Attention holder is not in a Position the behavior needs, the Titan runs the Attention Ladder again over only the soldiers who are, its Attention moves to the one it picks, and the behavior lands on them. It falls back only when nobody qualifies.
  • Frenzy: each Focus Titan starts at 0, gains 1 at the end of rounds 3, 6, and 9, and caps at 3. Its behavior roll is D6 + Frenzy, and a total above its table's highest result reads as the highest. Finish the fight, or it gets worse.

The round

  1. Wings: assign or keep them.
  2. Deal cards 1 to 20: one per player character, one per Squadmate on no Wing, and Tempo cards per Focus Titan.
  3. Swap: both in the same zone or adjacent zones, or both have left; neither Down nor Grabbed; once each.
  4. Play lowest first. Your turn is one move and one action, in either order. A Wing Squadmate acts right after its player character. A Titan's card: ladder, Stride, behavior.
  5. End: Gas Rolls; Regeneration +1; Frenzy +1 at the end of rounds 3, 6, and 9 (cap 3, during a retreat too); Background clocks +1, then the retreat clock +1 (neither during a retreat); everyone who made no ODM move loses all their Momentum.

The field and Positions

  • Field: 13 zones by default (7 or 19 when named). Titan A starts in the centre, the Squad one zone below it. Every zone but the centre is an edge zone; a step off an edge zone leaves.
  • Attachments: G Ground, A Anchored (both free), B On Body, S Blind Spot, X Grabbed, P Pinned (each naming a body in your zone).
  • Position, read from zone and attachment: On Body or Grabbed naming it, On Body; Blind Spot naming it, Blind Spot; Pinned under it or in its zone, In Reach; another zone, Distant.
  • Two soldiers: the same Position is the same zone; one step is the same or an adjacent zone.
  • An action never changes a Position. Only your own move does, plus this closed list: letting go, any fall, a Fear Roll's forced step, a Grab landing or ending, a Titan's Stride, a zone becoming Open under a Titan, a Titan arriving or dying, a Titan's body coming down, being carried, Fall Back, a grounded Titan standing in an Open zone, and starting placement, leaving, returning, and a retreat's forced moves.
  • Blind Spot is not on the Titan: you are anchored to terrain in its zone, out of its sight with its Nape in reach. It has no facing, and it stays behind when the Titan strides.
  • Inside a zone (see three shapes): Open, In Reach to On Body and no Blind Spot while it stands; Sparse, a chain, In Reach to On Body to Blind Spot; Wooded, Urban, Giant Forest, a branch, In Reach to either, and those two joined.
  • Moves: on foot, 1 step; mounted, up to 2 zone steps, never into Urban, stopping on entering a standing Titan's zone unless it is Open; ODM, a Flight. Grounded: attachment steps on foot too; in an Open zone it adds On Body to Blind Spot.

Flight and Momentum

  • Flight: every ODM move is a Fly roll with your own ODM Gear. It needs nothing, and the first step is free. Each success is 1 Momentum, up to the anchors of the zone you start in.
  • Carry: each further step, at most 2: into Open 2, Sparse 1, Wooded, Urban, or Giant Forest 0; off field 1; an attachment step 1. It never ends free in an Open zone; ending free, you are Anchored.
  • Loud: crossing a Titan's zone marks you on it whatever you rolled; no successes marks you on the Titans in the zone you end in, or the nearest one. Quiet stops both.
  • Momentum: public, 0 to the anchors of your zone; excess is lost at once when your zone changes or its rating falls, and at the end of the move. Lost in full at the end of any round in which you made no ODM move.
  • Spend: Carry (its cost); 1 each for Bite (1 Bonus Die on a strike or Break Attention this turn against a Titan in the zone your Flight ended in), Brace (1 Bonus Die on your next dodge this round), Quiet (no mark this turn), Clean line (no Gas Roll this round).
  • Anchors: Open 0, Sparse 1, Wooded 2, Urban 3, Giant Forest 3, by zone.
  • Terrain Traits: Open, a mounted Break Attention gains 1 Bonus Die; Sparse, a zone's first wreck does nothing; Wooded, none; Urban, Blind Spot is a roof and not airborne, so a Jam does not drop you; Giant Forest, falls there are raised one band.
  • Mounted charge: a mounted move into or out of a Titan's zone may mark you loud on it, once per move.

Strikes

  • Working ODM Gear (not Jammed, Gas Rating above 0) is needed for a Nape strike, and for a Body Part strike from On Body or Blind Spot, unless the Titan is grounded.
  • Nape strike: Strength + the Blade Set in your handles, from Blind Spot; + Openings (never your own); +2 if grounded. Not while you hold its Attention or are Grabbed, and never in a retreat. Nape Depth kills; short, each success is an Opening.
  • After a Nape strike you are still at Blind Spot. The strike counts as hooking into the Titan for the Attention Ladder only, so a striker who pulls the Attention onto themselves cannot strike again until it moves: a comrade's Draw Attention, a Break Attention, or the Titan retargeting to someone it can reach gets it off them.
  • Body Part strike: eyes from On Body or Blind Spot; arms and legs from In Reach or On Body. Grounded: any part from In Reach, On Body, or Blind Spot, except a holding arm. Successes add to the count; at Toughness the part worsens one state; past Broken, Openings.
  • Broken leg: grounded, and its body falls. Broken arm: frees its soldier. Broken eyes: no flare or cloak decoy.

Killing a Titan

  • Steam (a kill: On Body and Blind Spot; a Regeneration fill that heals a part: On Body): D6 1 to 3, no harm; 4 or 5, 1 Burn damage; 6, 2 Burn damage.
  • Falling Titan (a kill unless already grounded, or a grounding): each soldier On Body or at Blind Spot who is not airborne rolls Leap Clear (Agility + ODM Gear or horse, needs 1, spends nothing) and lands on the Ground in its zone. A failure, or a Down soldier: Pinned. The body wrecks its own zone.
  • Pinned: a Crush Critical Injury that cannot be lethal; In Reach, no move or Reaction. Arm or leg: a limb pin, with a few actions left. Torso or head: a body pin, with no action. Under a Corpse: a Burn Critical Injury at the start of each turn. Losing the pinned arm or leg frees no one.
  • Heave (action, Strength, In Reach or On Body): +1 to the Heave count per success; everyone the body pins is freed at Small 2, Medium 3, Large 4. Heaving a Corpse: 1 Burn damage. Or Break the pinning Body Part.

The Grab

  • Lands on 1 or more Net Successes, with no rider: the hold (first unbroken arm, Toughness 1); a torso Crush Critical Injury that cannot be lethal; you hold its Attention; witnesses make Fear Rolls. On a Pinned target: the crush only.
  • It spends the action of your 1st counted turn. At that turn's end you are lifted; at the 2nd's end, devoured.
  • Out: Break Free, needs 2; Pry Loose, needs 2, from On Body (lifted: 2-die penalty on both); strike the arm, 2 while Intact, 1 while Wounded (lifted: only from On Body or Blind Spot); Break Attention; kill it.

Attention Ladder

  1. Hooked in: On Body, or struck its Nape (struck first).
  2. In Reach.
  3. Just hurt it (a Body Part strike with a success).
  4. Loudest: Draw Attention or Scream (in its zone only); a Flight that crosses its zone, or ends in or nearest it with no successes; a mounted charge into or out of its zone.
  5. Nearest: On Body, In Reach, Blind Spot, Distant; it counts no zones.
  • Marked as loud also meets every rung any other candidate meets, except hooked in.
  • Ties: narrow by each lower rung; then the current holder keeps it; then the lowest card this round (not before the deal or at an end step); otherwise nothing holds it until its next card.
  • Marks last until the end of its next card that resolves a behavior. Down or carried soldiers meet only the nearest rung.

Break Attention

  • Perception + ODM Gear or horse. Needs 1 as its Attention holder, otherwise 2 (2 for anyone if it holds a Grabbed soldier); +1 for a Feint; +1 per decoy in a row.
  • The decoy holds for Tempo cards; extra successes are Openings; it frees a Grabbed soldier.
  • Flare: a flare in Squad Supply, eyes not Broken. Every Background clock fills 1.
  • Riderless horse: your own horse, not lame, ridden or in your zone. On a success it bolts.
  • Thrown cloak: from On Body or Blind Spot, once per Titan Engagement, eyes not Broken.
  • Feint: from In Reach or On Body, with working ODM Gear, mounted on your horse that is not lame, or on foot against a grounded Titan.

The retreat

  • When the retreat clock fills (8 segments, unless the rule that began the fight names another length), or a Background clock fills with two Focus Titans alive. No Nape strikes, and no one returns.
  • Move first, one of: out (to free, then one ring further out, zones with no body first); leave from any edge zone, whatever stands in it; toward a Down, Grabbed, or Pinned comrade (fewer zones between you); stay in their zone after acting for them. On a turn you Lift Comrade, the move comes after the lift.
  • Stay limit: after as many rounds as the clock has segments, counted from the round after it began, no moves toward or beside a comrade Pinned under a Corpse (every retreat), or any comrade (no Focus Titan alive).
  • Past the limit: a soldier the both-legs grade keeps from moving is not standing.

Gear and falls

  • Gas Roll after ODM use: 2 dice, 3 after a Pushed roll made with ODM Gear; each 1 lowers the Gas Rating by 1. A full canister is 3.
  • Jam (ODM Gear at 0; you fall if airborne, but never from Blind Spot in an Urban zone) or dry (Gas Rating 0): no ODM move, Nape strike, or strike from On Body or Blind Spot, unless the Titan is grounded.
  • Blade Set: any wear from a Pushed roll ruins it. Swap in a carried one once in your turn: in a Titan Engagement the swap spends your move, so you refit and still strike, and lose that turn's Flight.
  • Carry: Strength + 4 items. More is Overloaded: every ODM move also spends your action.
  • Falls: D6 + band. Low +0: Distant, In Reach, and every fall from a horse, which is never raised. High +2: On Body, Blind Spot. You land on the Ground in your zone. A Giant Forest zone or a Large reference body raises the band one step, to extreme +4 at most. Damage: 2 or less, 0; 3 or 4, 1; 5 or 6, 2; 7 or 8, 3; 9 or more, 4. A fall a called roll's stakes name is low or high, never extreme, inside a Titan Engagement as outside one, and no height step raises it.

Expeditions Playtest rule

  • Leg: the Lead rolls the Post's entry (Hard Ride: Ride), needing 1. Success: 1 ration, and the hazard gets −1 per extra success. Failure: 2 rations, hazard +1. Hard Ride: double rations, hazard +1.
  • Help on a Leg roll or the camp roll: up to 3 comrades who are not Down, spending nothing, each once per Leg on a Leg roll. A roll no soldier can make fails.
  • Hazard: D6 + band (Near 0, Far 1, Deep 2) + Night Camps made + Post; −2 for the Signal Relay flare. A retreat: fall back and ride the Leg again.
  • Night Camp: Survive, needing 1; spend 1 ration. Success and no hunger: Camp Relief, −1 Stress. Night hazard: D6 + band, +1 after a failed camp roll, the band always the day's last ridden Leg's. Then a day passes. A retreat at night: fall back overnight, and no further if the camp is already at the fallback Waypoint.
  • Hunger (short of rations): spend them all; every soldier gains 1 Stress.

Skirmish Playtest rule

  • You Fight (Strength + Blade Set) or Shoot (Agility + firearm) against the Foe's Guard: base dice that succeed on 6.
  • A Foe's Attack Dice succeed on 6. Block a Fight (Strength, with a Blade Set if you have one, otherwise no Gear Dice) or Dodge a Fight or a shot. One Reaction per Foe per round.
  • 1 or more Net Successes: the weapon's damage, +1 per Net Success beyond the first. A Foe at 0: dead (Cut, Pierce, Burn) or out cold (Crush).
  • Grit: the group breaks when that many are out, or sooner with a reason said aloud. Parley needs the Parley value + 1, −1 per Foe out, never below 1, + the ask.
  • Foes act by the foe rule unless the GM directs one with a reason said aloud. A Foe never attacks a Down soldier.

The printable field

The Standard field at play size. Print this page on A4 landscape at actual size, with no scaling, slip it into a sheet protector, and write each zone's rating letter in its dashed rim band in dry-wipe; strike it through and write the new letter when a wreck lowers it. Tokens stand for Titans, soldiers, and horses. Write each soldier's attachment letter beside their token.

For the GM

This part is written for the GM. It holds how the GM rules, how the GM runs a Titan Engagement, the Titans' stat blocks and Behavior Tables, and the values an Abnormal keeps hidden. Only the Hidden values page is GM only: players do not read it. Everything else in this part is public.

Rulings

"Tell them the price before they jump. Once they jump, the dice are theirs."Section Commander, to a new Squad Leader

You rule where the rules are silent. You rule before the dice, never after them. Whether to roll, what the roll needs from the fiction, how the circumstances tilt the pool, and what failure costs are your calls. A rolled die, a table row, a Titan's card, and a Foe's open dice stand as they fell.

When to call a roll

  • Call a roll when a soldier attempts something no rule covers, its outcome is in doubt, and failure would cost something. Call rolls sparingly: a roll is for a moment with something at stake.
  • No roll when failure would change nothing, or the soldier has the time, the tools, and the training to do it unpressed. It happens.
  • Impossible: say so before anyone rolls. It does not happen.
  • A soldier who says they are looking rolls Spot in the open as a called roll. A soldier who does not know something is there may get a passive roll (see When to roll): you build their pool as it stands, name the Circumstances to yourself, and roll out of sight, outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish only. A passive roll is not a called roll and has no stakes: failing it means only that the soldier does not notice.

Stakes first

  1. Name the roll: the Catalog entry closest to the act, from Spot, Size Up, Survive, Persuade, Endure, Sneak, Recall, Ride, and Fly, or an attribute alone if none fits.
  2. Name the Circumstances: one step, or Standard (see Circumstances).
  3. Name the stakes: what failure costs, from the menu below, and, where it is not obvious, what success gives: the act itself, information, a better position the next roll reckons with, an object that is not a gear item, or found Squad Supply. Success never gives a gear item, Stress relief, a Health box or healing, a Commendation, a Research Point, or a Faction Standing change; each of those comes only from its own rule or table.
  4. Let the player answer. They may declare Help, change their approach, or back out. Then they roll, and nothing you named changes.

The menu

A failed called roll costs what you staked, and nothing else. Either it does not happen, which is the default and always available; or it happens at one cost; or it does not happen and the one cost falls anyway. One cost per failed roll:

  • Stress: the soldier gains 1 Stress.
  • A fall: by the fall procedure, low or high as you name from the height, or low if you name none. Never extreme, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one, and no height step raises the band you name. In a fight it is a fall in full: it lands as the fall procedure gives, and that change of Position is the fall rule's, not your ruling's.
  • Damage: 1 to 3, of a named Injury Type: Crush for a blow, Cut for an edge, Pierce for a point, Burn for fire. Never 4 or more.
  • A lost item or unit: one carried item lost, or 1 unit of one kind of Squad Supply spent. Never wear, which is the price of a Push.
  • Time or position: a change in the situation, described, that the next roll must reckon with: the window closes, the patrol arrives, the door is barred, the Squad is noticed. A Skirmish or a Titan Engagement can begin this way.

Say the Health. When the stakes name damage or a fall, say the soldier's current Health with them, so the table knows before the dice whether a failure can put that soldier Down with a Critical Injury.

Never a stake: a Critical Injury dealt directly, an Injury Location, a row, or a time limit; a Death Roll, a Scar, or Grief; a Fear Roll; Stress relief, a Health box, healing time, or a care window opened or closed; a Titan's roll, a Foe's roll, or a table result.

Move the story on. A failure should complicate, not halt. A soldier tries the same thing again only when something has changed: a new approach, a better tool, another soldier, or time spent, at a cost of time or position you name before the new roll. That cost falls when they try again, whatever the result, and it is not the new roll's stakes. A cost you name for Help on a called roll works the same way: time or position only, falling when the Help is declared, and never counted against the one cost of a failed roll.

Found supplies. Name the place with the stakes: a house, a wagon, a storehouse. On a successful called Spot or Survive to search it, you may add at most 1 unit per success, of one kind of Squad Supply that place could plausibly hold. A place yields Squad Supply to one called search only: once a called search of it has been rolled, whatever the result, no later roll finds Squad Supply there, and the other soldiers present search it by Helping that roll. A place is one building, wagon, cache, or the like the Squad can search in one go. A Waypoint scene yields Squad Supply to at most two called searches, whatever the number of places you name in it; no later search in that scene finds any, whatever its result. No called search is made in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, and success on a called roll in a fight never finds Squad Supply: a place at hand is searched once the fight has ended, under these caps. Nothing else adds units by ruling.

People with reasons, Titans without

  • Foes act by the foe rule unless you direct one. Direct a Foe only when the fiction gives it a reason, and say the reason aloud before it acts. You may break a group, or have it surrender, sooner than its Grit, with a reason said aloud; never later. You rule whether the Squad can approach unseen for the Ambush. A Foe's dice, damage, and the ban on attacking a Down soldier stay as written (see Skirmishes).
  • Titans have no reasons. You never steer a Titan: its cards, its Attention, its Attack Dice, and its tables stay as written. Your judgment reaches a Titan Engagement only through the soldiers' own pools (see Circumstances in a fight).
  • Framing. As Command, you may name a Titan Engagement's Focus Titan, Anchor Rating, and Background Titans before any setup roll, and you write an Expedition's Mission Brief, within the bounds the route rules give, and frame its Waypoint scenes (see Setting up a Titan Engagement, Expeditions, and Waypoint scenes). Framing a Large Titan with Background Titans every time makes a campaign deadlier than the setup table does.
  • Improvised acts. In a fight, rule whether what the soldier describes is an entry taken as written or an improvised act, and name the model entry before the pool is built. The model entry is an action that soldier could take in that fight, never a Reaction, a roll, a fixed roll, or an option. The act copies that entry's effect at its size, and only a Nape strike taken as its entry is written kills a Titan: an act modelled on the Nape strike is a strike that falls short, whatever its successes. When no entry marked as an action could model the act and it would change nothing the fight tracks, it is a called roll instead, which spends the action, is made on the soldier's turn and never in place of a Reaction, takes that fight's Help, and stakes nothing the fight tracks. Taking cover is a move, never a dodge, and makes no later dodge easier (see When the steps do not resolve an action).
  • Squadmates are the players' to direct. You may voice one.

Horror

Outside a Titan Engagement you may call a Fear Roll for an event at least as horrifying as a listed trigger, naming the witnesses who roll (see Fear Rolls). Call it rarely. As advice, not a rule: call at most one in a scene, and none for a horror the soldiers have already rolled for that day. Never call one inside a Titan Engagement, and never for an event a listed trigger already covers. Never call one for an outcome a called roll's stakes named, since a stake is never a Fear Roll; a listed trigger that outcome later meets still applies.

What stands

You apply all of these as written and rule on none of them:

  • the Push, which no ruling forbids or forces, Covering, and Help's effect and cap;
  • the sources of Bonus Dice (Circumstances is the one source you name), the cap of 4, the penalty rule, and what a roll needs;
  • the fixed rolls and their tables: the Fear Roll, the Stress Response roll, the Gas Roll, the Death Roll, the performance roll, the fall roll, and the infirmary roll;
  • the Critical Injury, Stress Response, Scar, and Grief tables, Down, Death Rolls, healing, and care windows; Stress relief, which comes only from a written trigger;
  • gear wear, Standard Issue, the Funding gate, the Ledger, and one Requisition per Downtime;
  • the Behavior Tables, the Attention Ladder, Attack Dice and Titan Dice, the Reaction procedure, the rule that only a Nape strike kills a Titan, the size of Openings, Steam, the Falling Titan, Corpse Heat, the Grab countdown, Regeneration, the retreat clock, and the end tests;
  • the standard Titans' numbers, and which hidden values a Read reveals;
  • the Leg roll, except its Circumstances, the hazard and Night tables, their rolls, and their modifiers' amounts, which the route you set as Command, the night camps made, the Leg roll, and the flare decide, the Squad always reaching the next Waypoint, rations, and the Straggler's random victim;
  • Squadmates, which the players direct;
  • results stand, a Titan's Next Behavior included once it is rolled.

The playtest log

Record each of these on the Rulings log: every step other than Standard you name in a Titan Engagement, and the roll it was named for; every framed start that departs from the setup table; every route you set as Command, with its Legs, each Leg's band and Post, the Hard Ride days, and its Depots; every Foe you direct; every Fear Roll you call; and every death, Critical Injury, Scar, and point of Stress a ruling caused. Count the called rolls you make each session.

Running a Titan Engagement

"It does not hate you. It does not want anything. That is why you can predict it."Lecture notes, Training Corps, third year

You roll each hidden Next Behavior, roll each Titan's Attack Dice in the open, keep the tracker, and reveal facts when a rule says so. Before the dice, you rule on three things: the Circumstances of the soldiers' rolls, the framing of the fight, and, for an act a soldier describes, whether it is an entry taken as written or an improvised act with a model entry (see Rulings). You never re-roll or change a roll, a table row, or a result. Who holds Attention, where a Titan strides, which behavior it resolves and against whom, whether a move is allowed, where a soldier lands, who lies in a falling body's path, when a Titan regenerates, when a Background Titan enters, and when the fight ends all come from the rules.

The Titans' stat blocks and Behavior Tables sit in this part to keep the player chapters short; show them to any player who asks. What stays hidden is each Titan's Next Behavior until it resolves or is revealed, and an Abnormal's hidden values until a Read reveals them.

Playtest configuration Playtest rule

The first playtest campaign runs 4 player characters and no Squadmates. Recruit is not offered, no one is promoted, and every Straggler victim is a player character (see Playtest configuration).

Also run from Part One: starting a Titan Engagement, the field, attachments and Positions, the Stride, wrecking, the round, dodging a Titan, the Attention Ladder and when Attention changes, decoys, a grounded Titan, Steam, the Falling Titan, Pinned, Heave and cutting free, the Corpse, the Grab, retreats and forced moves, and the end of the fight.

Setting up a Titan Engagement Playtest rule

The rule that begins a Titan Engagement names its field (its size, its field rating, and the rating of any zone), its Focus Titan, any Background Titans with their clock lengths, and the length of its retreat clock.

Framing. Before any roll on these tables, you may name what a Mission Brief could for anything the starting rule leaves unnamed: the Focus Titan (any Titan in this packet, standard or Abnormal), the field size (the Skirmish field, the Standard field, or the Set piece), the field rating, the rating of any zone, and the Background Titans, which are none, one, or two, each on a clock of 4, 6, or 8 segments, the counts and lengths these tables use. A Brief that says "the ruined chapel on the ridge" names one zone Urban. What you name is never rolled, so no Abnormal roll is made for a Focus Titan you name, and no terrain roll for a zone you rate. A value the starting rule names stands, and you never swap it. These stay closed: Titan A starts in the centre zone and the Squad in its start zone unless the starting rule names other Positions; you never name a soldier's zone or attachment, a Titan's zone, a Stride's length or route, a tie, an entry zone, a Carry cost, or which zone a wreck takes; the retreat clock is 8 segments unless a rule names its length; a row once rolled stands; and the Fear Rolls of the start are made as the list gives them.

Roll whatever the starting rule and your framing leave unnamed on these tables. When you name nothing, the tables are the whole setup.

  1. Field size. The Standard field. Nothing is rolled.
  2. Field rating. Roll D6.
  3. Zone terrain. The centre zone and the Squad's start zone take the field rating. Every other zone, in number order, rolls D6 on the zone terrain table. Write each zone's letter on the field and on the tracker. The rating each zone takes here is also its start rating.
  4. Focus Titan. Roll D6 for its Size Class. The Focus Titan is the standard Titan of that class. On Medium, roll D6 again: on a 6 the Focus Titan is the Sprinting Abnormal instead.
  5. Background Titans. Roll D6 for Background Titans and their clock lengths. Then, for each Background Titan, in the order the clocks are listed, roll D6 on the Size Class table. Each is the standard Titan of the class it rolls; never make the Abnormal roll for one.
  6. Retreat clock. 8 segments. Nothing is rolled.

Retreat clock: 8 segments, for every Titan Engagement these tables set up.

A Titan Engagement a hazard begins

On an Expedition, Titan Engagements begin from the rows of the Leg Hazard table and the Night table (see Expeditions and the Night Camp). The row names what it names; the tables above give the rest.

  • Field rating. A Leg's hazard takes it from the kind of the Waypoint the Leg leads to. A night hazard takes it from the kind of the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached. The gate sets none, so a Titan Engagement on the Leg to the gate rolls its field rating on the table above. The zones are then generated from it.
  • Who takes part: every living soldier on the Expedition, mounted or not as they rode, unless the row says otherwise.
  • Retreat clock: no row names one, so every hazard's Titan Engagement has 8 segments.
  • A retreat on a Leg. If a Leg's Titan Engagement became a retreat before it ended, the Squad falls back to the Waypoint the Leg started from and rides the Leg again from its first step, with a new Leg roll and a new hazard.
  • A retreat at night. If a Titan Engagement the Night table began became a retreat before it ended, the Squad falls back overnight to the Waypoint the day's last Leg started from, the Night Camp goes on there at its day-passes step, and the next day's first Leg rides that Leg again (see A retreat at night).

Starting the fight

  1. Note the field size and field rating, generate the zones, and write each zone's rating on the field and on the tracker; each is also that zone's start rating. Note the Focus Titan, each Background Titan with its clock length, and the retreat clock, which starts empty. No soldier holds Momentum yet.
  2. Label the Focus Titan A and stand it in the centre zone.
  3. Its Body Parts start Intact with a count of 0, it has no Openings and no previous behavior, its Regeneration clock is empty, and its Frenzy is 0. Roll its Next Behavior out of sight.
  4. Every soldier who takes part stands on the Ground in the Squad's start zone, unless the starting rule names other Positions: a named In Reach is the centre zone, and a named Distant the start zone. Mounted soldiers stay mounted and dismounted soldiers stay dismounted, and no one is airborne. Each dismounted soldier's horse stands in the start zone.
  5. Check its Attention Ladder with no cards dealt. If one soldier meets a higher rung than every other, as the Straggler's victim in the centre zone does, they hold its Attention. Otherwise nothing holds it, and its first card turns it: with everyone in the start zone, a Squad of two or more starts that way.
  6. Soldiers make the Fear Rolls for a first Titan Engagement and for an Abnormal.
  7. The Squad's Squad Tactics become unused.
  8. Round 1 begins.

The tracker

Keep one line for the Titan Engagement, one row for each Focus Titan, one row for each Corpse, and one row for each Background Titan. Everything on the tracker is public except an unrevealed Next Behavior and the values an Abnormal keeps hidden.

  • Engagement line: the field size and field rating; the field readout, each zone's rating by number, marked where it is below its start rating, whose anchors are the Momentum cap of every soldier in that zone; the round; the retreat clock's filled segments out of its length; whether a retreat is under way, and the round it began (the retreat's stay limit counts from it); the Squad Tactics and which are used; whose cloaks are thrown; and the round in which no soldier was left standing, while the ending waits.
  • Focus Titan row: its label, Size Class, and stat block; its zone, and its Stride (0 while grounded); its Frenzy, 0 to 3, raised by 1 at the end of every third round; its cards this round; the Attention holder, or the decoy holding it and how many cards its hold has left, or nothing; the Next Behavior (face down), the previous behavior, and any Call It on the Next Behavior; each Body Part's state and count; Openings and who created each; the Regeneration clock (only its filled segments for an Abnormal whose clock length no Read has revealed); decoys in a row; any Grabbed soldier, with countdown turns and whether lifted; the marks standing on soldiers; its Heave count out of its Heave rating; and each soldier its body pins, by limb with the pinning Body Part (or none), or by the body.
  • Corpse row: when a Focus Titan dies, its row stays for its Corpse, marked Corpse: its zone, which never changes; each Body Part's state and count, which never regenerate; its Heave count out of its Heave rating; and each soldier it pins.
  • Background Titan row: its Size Class, its stat block, and its clock.
