A Medium | zone 4, Stride 2 | Frenzy 0/3 | cards 4 | Att: Jonas | Next: [hidden] | Prev: Bite | Eyes I0 LA W1 RA I0 LL B0 RL I1 | Op 2 (Mila, Mila) | Regen 1/3 | Decoys 0 | Grab: - | Marks: Mila struck its Nape | Heave 0/3 | Pins: -

A Corpse | zone 4 | Eyes I0 LA I0 RA I0 LL B0 RL W1 | Heave 1/3 | Pins: Mila (right arm, RA), Jonas (body)

Background 1 Small | clock 4/6

Reading the rows. Zones lists each zone's number and rating letter; a letter in brackets is the start rating of a zone a wreck has lowered, as 4S(W). zone and Stride are where the Titan stands and how far it strides. Att is the Attention holder, or the decoy with the cards its hold has left (Att: flare, 1 left). Next and Prev are the Next Behavior and the previous behavior. Each Body Part (Eyes; LA and RA for the arms; LL and RL for the legs) shows I, W, or B for Intact, Wounded, or Broken, then its count. Frenzy is the Titan's Frenzy of its cap of 3, added to its behavior roll. Op is the Openings, with who created each. Regen is the Regeneration clock's filled segments of its length. Decoys is decoys in a row. Grab is a Grabbed soldier, with countdown turns and whether lifted. Marks are the marks standing on soldiers. Heave is the Heave count of the Heave rating. Pins lists each Pinned soldier with the pinned limb and the pinning Body Part, or "body" for a body pin.

Update it after the deal, after each Titan card, after each strike or Break Attention, after each counted turn of a Grabbed soldier, after each fall of a Titan's body and each Heave, and at the end steps. Update a Titan's zone after its Stride, and a zone's rating whenever a Wreck effect resolves or a Titan's body falls. Players track everything else on their sheets: their zone and attachment, Momentum, mounted, airborne, carried and carrying, Pinned, turns and actions spent in advance, Stress, Stress Responses, Critical Injuries, Health, gear, and Gas Rating. The zone and attachment columns of the Squad sheet are the record every Position the ladder reads comes from.

Roll together any rolls that do not depend on each other: the end-step Gas Rolls, and the Fear Rolls every witness makes for one event.

How to read a stat block and a Behavior Table

A stat block lists a Titan's Size Class; whether it is an Abnormal; its Tempo (cards a round); its Nape Depth; its Regeneration clock's segments; its Body Parts in order, each with its Toughness; its Heave rating; its Stride; and its Attention Ladder. The Body Part order breaks Regeneration ties and decides which arm Grabs. The Heave rating is the Heave count that lifts its body, living or a Corpse, off the soldiers it pins (see Heave). A standard Titan takes every number from its Size Class. An Abnormal lists its own.

A Behavior Table gives one row per entry, and every result from 1 to 6 belongs to exactly one entry. Thrash is never rolled.

  • D6, Behavior, and Tier. Results 1 and 2 terrorize, 3 and 4 control, 5 and 6 kill.
  • Targets: "holder" is the soldier holding the Titan's Attention; "holder and everyone in their zone at their Position" adds every other soldier in the holder's zone who holds the same Position relative to the Titan and is not Grabbed, never a soldier elsewhere on the field.
  • Holder's Position: the Positions the holder must hold for the entry to happen. "Any" means all four. Remember that Blind Spot is not on the Titan: the soldier is anchored to terrain behind it, out of its sight with its Nape in reach, while On Body means literally on it. An entry that needs On Body does not reach a soldier at Blind Spot. In Reach, On Body, and Blind Spot exist only in the Titan's own zone, whatever its size; an entry that lists Distant reaches a holder in any other zone. The requirement is checked after the Stride.
  • Body Parts used: the kinds the entry needs unbroken. "arm" needs one unbroken arm; "both legs" needs both legs unbroken.
  • Attack Dice: how many Titan Dice the entry rolls when a card resolves it. A telegraph-only entry rolls none.
  • Effects, applied in order to each target the card lands on. Every effect that harms names its Injury Type.
  • Fallback: the entry that happens instead when no soldier at all holds a Position this entry needs. While anyone does, the Titan retargets onto them rather than falling back. The fallback is then read the same way, and the Titan retargets for it in its turn (see Checking the Attention Ladder).
  • Grab: "yes" marks an entry that starts the Grab. The Closing Hand Scar reads this mark.

Titan Dice

  • A Titan's Attack Dice are Titan Dice. Each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success.
  • Roll them in the open when a card resolves the entry. Use a colour no soldier's pool uses.
  • Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice. The pool is the entry's Attack Dice and nothing else. A Titan never makes a Reaction.
  • The roll's successes are the card's Severity, the same number for every target.
  • A roll of 0 successes whiffs against every target, a target who cannot make a Reaction included.
Effects in a Behavior Table
EffectWhat it does to a target the card lands on
Stress +1The target gains 1 Stress, once, whatever the Net Successes.
Crush or Bite Critical Injury (location, lethal or not)A Titan attack of that Injury Type at that Injury Location (see Critical Injuries). "side rolled": a D6 gives the side, odd left and even right. "rolled location": roll the Injury Location. Its roll adds 1 for each Net Success beyond the first; a "cannot be lethal" entry still caps the row, and an instant-death row still kills.
Knock loose (fall: Crush)An airborne target, or one at On Body or Blind Spot, falls; the fall's damage is Crush. A mounted target is not affected. The same whatever the Net Successes.
Grab (crush: Crush)The Grab procedure (see Running a Grab). Its crush is a Crush Critical Injury. The same whatever the Net Successes.
TelegraphThe Next Behavior rolled after this card is revealed to every soldier, whether the card landed or whiffed.
WreckThe Titan's own zone takes one rating step toward Open, once per card, whether the card landed or whiffed and whatever the Net Successes. It touches no soldier and no horse. A Sparse zone's first wreck does nothing, and an Open zone takes no further step.

Tiers. Any tier may wreck the Titan's zone. A terrorize entry inflicts only Stress and may telegraph. A control entry adds falls and Critical Injuries that cannot be lethal. A kill entry adds lethal Critical Injuries and the Grab. Thrash works at any Position, uses no Body Parts, rolls its Size Class's control pool, and inflicts nothing lethal.

Rolling the Next Behavior

A Focus Titan always has a hidden Next Behavior. Roll it:

  • when the Titan becomes a Focus Titan;
  • after each of its cards that resolved a behavior, whether the card landed or whiffed;
  • after each of its cards that came up while a decoy held its Attention.

A card that resolves nothing because the Titan holds a Grabbed soldier, or because nothing holds its Attention, keeps the Next Behavior.

To roll it

  1. Roll D6 and add the Titan's Frenzy. A total above the table's highest result reads as that highest result. Find the entry.
  2. The entry cannot be rolled if it was the Titan's previous behavior, or if it needs Body Parts the Titan no longer has unbroken.
  3. If it cannot, take the entry holding the next higher result and test again. At Frenzy 0, after 6 comes 1. At Frenzy 1 or more the roll never wraps: if the entry at 6 cannot be rolled, turn back down the table to the next result below the total, then the next below that, to 1.
  4. If no entry passes, the Next Behavior is Thrash.

Frenzy. Each Focus Titan starts at Frenzy 0 and gains 1 at its own end step of every third round (the third, the sixth, the ninth), to a cap of 3, during a retreat as well. It is public: keep it on the tracker and say it aloud when it rises. It is added to the roll above, so a Titan at Frenzy 3 never rolls lower than its table's result 4. It adds no dice, changes no Attack Dice and no need, and moves no soldier.

The Frenzy a roll takes is the Frenzy standing when you roll, and nothing re-reads it afterwards. A Titan that has just become a Focus Titan is at 0, so its first Next Behavior is a plain D6. A Next Behavior rolled in one round and resolved in the next was rolled at the earlier round's Frenzy, so the escalation reaches the table one card late. The end step raises the counter and never touches a roll already made.

Rolling never checks Positions; those are checked when the card comes up. A Read that reveals the Next Behavior reveals its whole row, Attack Dice included. Its Severity exists only once its card rolls.

Resolving a Titan's card

A card of a Titan that has died does nothing.

When a Focus Titan's card comes up

  1. Holding. If it holds a living Grabbed soldier, the card resolves nothing. Do not check the ladder. The Next Behavior and the marks stay.
  2. Decoy. If a decoy holds its Attention, the card resolves nothing and the hold has one card fewer left. The Next Behavior counts as spent: it becomes the previous behavior, any Call It on it ends, and you roll a new one. If that was the hold's last card, the hold ends and you check the ladder; otherwise the decoy keeps its Attention. Either way the marks stay and decoys in a row does not change.
  3. Attention. Otherwise, check the Attention Ladder. If nothing holds its Attention, the card resolves nothing, the Next Behavior is kept, and the marks stay.
  4. Stride. If the holder is in another zone, the Titan strides toward them, up to its Stride, one zone closer each step, the lower-numbered zone on a tie, stopping as it enters their zone (see The Stride). A grounded Titan's Stride is 0. Soldiers On Body or Grabbed go with it; soldiers at its Blind Spot stay and detach. Nothing is chosen, rolled, harmed, or marked.
  5. Choose. Take the Next Behavior's entry, against the Positions the Stride left. If the Titan lacks the Body Parts it uses, the behavior is Thrash. If the holder does not meet its Position requirement, the Titan retargets: check the Attention Ladder again over only the candidates who do meet that requirement, move its Attention to the soldier the ladder picks, and resolve the entry against them. Only if no candidate meets the requirement does the entry become its fallback, and the fallback is tested the same way. If the fallback is Thrash, repeats the previous behavior, or needs Body Parts the Titan no longer has, the behavior is Thrash. Otherwise, if the holder does not meet the fallback's own Position requirement, the Titan retargets over the candidates who do. Only when nobody meets the fallback either is the behavior Thrash. Thrash can always happen, and it is the only entry that can follow itself.
  6. Roll and announce. Announce the behavior's name, tier, and targets. Roll its Attack Dice as Titan Dice in the open and announce the successes: the card's Severity, the same for every target.
  7. Reactions. On Severity 0 the card whiffs against every target, a target who cannot make a Reaction included, and no one dodges. Otherwise each target, knowing the Severity, chooses whether to dodge, or cancels with a dodge already made against this Titan this round. Each dodge success cancels one of the card's successes, separately for this card. The card lands on a target on 1 or more Net Successes and whiffs on 0. A target who makes no Reaction, or cannot make one, cancels nothing: every success is net against them. A Call It on this behavior adds its Bonus Dice to a dodge made against this card, never to an earlier dodge compared against it (see Dodging a Titan).
  8. Effects. For each target it lands on, apply the effects in order, targets in card order, a Wing Squadmate just after its player character. Only a Critical Injury reads the Net Successes. A telegraph and a Wreck apply whether the card landed or whiffed, and a Wreck takes one rating step off the Titan's zone once, even where the entry landed on no one. A target who dies receives none of the card's later effects; every other target still does.
  9. Next. A card that whiffed has still resolved a behavior. The behavior becomes the previous behavior, decoys in a row returns to 0, every mark for this Titan clears, and you roll the new Next Behavior. If the behavior had a telegraph effect, reveal the new Next Behavior. It stays revealed until a card resolves it or a decoy's card spends it.

A Pinned target cannot make a Reaction, so a card lands on them on 1 or more successes. A Grab that lands on a Pinned soldier lands only its crush (see Running a Grab).

Example: a card that lands, and one that whiffs

A standard Medium Titan's card comes up. It holds no Grabbed soldier and no decoy holds it. Its ladder gives its Attention to Private Lena Falk, who stands in its zone, In Reach, so it does not stride. Its Next Behavior is Bite: In Reach meets the entry, and Bite uses no Body Part.

Roll and announce. You announce Bite, a kill entry, against Lena, and roll its 9 Attack Dice in the open: 6, 2, 5, 3, 1, 4, 5, 2, 3. Three dice show 5 or 6: Severity 3.

Reactions. Lena's turn this round is unspent, so her dodge spends it. Agility 3, ODM Gear 3, and Stress 2 give 3 base dice, 3 Gear Dice, and 2 Stress Dice. She rolls base 6, 4, 1; Gear 6, 3, 2; Stress 5, 2. Two successes cancel two of the Bite's three, leaving 1 Net Success. The Bite lands.

Effects. Lena gains a Bite Critical Injury at the torso, which can be lethal. With 1 Net Success its roll adds nothing. Had she not dodged, all 3 successes would be net, and the roll would add 2.

Next. Bite becomes the previous behavior, decoys in a row returns to 0, the marks clear, and you roll a new Next Behavior out of sight. If this is the fight's fourth round the Titan sits at Frenzy 1, so that roll is D6 + 1, and a total of 7 reads as result 6.

Had Lena been in the next zone. The Titan would first have strided into her zone, and she would have held In Reach when it chose. Had she been at Blind Spot, Bite needs In Reach or On Body, so the Titan would retarget: you would check the ladder again over only the soldiers at In Reach or On Body, move its Attention to the one it picks, and bite them instead. Bite would fall back to Thrash only if nobody at all were standing there.

Had the dice whiffed. Had no Titan Die shown 5 or 6, the Bite would whiff against Lena, she would make no dodge and keep her turn, and the Next step would still happen in full: Bite the previous behavior, the marks cleared, a new Next Behavior rolled.

Checking the Attention Ladder

Candidates are the living soldiers holding a Position relative to the Titan, except soldiers who have left and soldiers held by another Titan. A horse is never a candidate. No ladder reads a Position relative to a Corpse.

To check the ladder

  1. Find the highest rung any candidate meets (the rungs are in The Attention Ladder). Those who meet it are tied. If that rung is nearest, only the candidates at the closest Position are tied.
  2. If that rung is hooked into its body and anyone tied there is marked as having struck its Nape, only they stay tied.
  3. Narrow the tie by each lower rung in turn. At the nearest rung, keep those at the closest Position any of them holds.
  4. If still tied, the soldier already holding its Attention keeps it.
  5. Otherwise the lowest card this round takes it, a Wing Squadmate counting just after its player character. Skip this step when no cards have been dealt this round, and at an end step. A Titan that enters mid-round because a flare filled its clock uses that round's cards.
  6. Otherwise nothing holds its Attention until its next card. The order of the Squad sheet never chooses.

Down and carried soldiers are candidates, but they meet only the rungs that allow it: on the standard ladder, only nearest. The Sprinting Abnormal's current holder rung also allows it, for a holder who is not at Distant.

Retargeting: the second check

When a card's entry needs a Position its Attention holder does not hold, check the ladder a second time with a narrowed field. The candidates are only the soldiers who hold a Position the entry needs; everything else about the check is unchanged, tie-breaks included. The soldier it picks takes the Titan's Attention, and the entry resolves against them.

  1. Narrow the candidates to those who hold a Position the entry names.
  2. If none do, the entry becomes its fallback, and the Titan's Attention does not move. Check the fallback the same way: narrow the candidates to those who hold a Position the fallback names, and run the ladder over them. Only when nobody holds one of those either is the behavior Thrash.
  3. Otherwise run the ladder over the narrowed field and move the Titan's Attention to the soldier it picks.
  4. Announce the behavior against that soldier, and resolve the card as usual. Its Attention stays with them until something moves it again.

It is what a predator does: the thing it wanted is out of reach, so it takes what it can catch. Say it aloud, so the table sees the Titan turn.

Marks (struck its Nape, hurt it, loud) are public. Each clears at the Next step of the Titan's next card that resolves a behavior, landed or whiffed. A card that resolves nothing leaves every mark standing: every card of a decoy's hold, the last included; a card while the Titan holds a Grabbed soldier; and a card with nothing holding its Attention.

Titan harm, clocks, and the end

Body Parts and Regeneration

  • Body Parts. Record each strike's successes one at a time on the part's count. At the part's Toughness, move it one state toward Broken and reset the count to 0. Past Broken, each success is an Opening with its creator's name. A Broken leg grounds the Titan (see A grounded Titan), and its body comes down (below).
  • Regeneration fills 1 segment at the end step, retreat or not.

When a Regeneration clock is full

  1. Erase every Opening on the Titan.
  2. Reset every Body Part's count to 0.
  3. Move the most damaged Body Part one state toward Intact: Broken before Wounded, then the one listed first.
  4. If a Body Part moved, every soldier who holds On Body relative to the Titan is scalded by Steam. A fill that moves nothing releases no Steam. If the part was the Titan's last Broken leg, the Titan stands: every soldier it pins is freed, and its Heave count returns to 0.
  5. Empty the clock.

The Falling Titan

A Focus Titan's body comes down when it becomes grounded, and when it dies unless it was already grounded. It rolls no Attack Dice, and no Reaction answers it.

  • The wreck. The body takes one rating step off its own zone, with the fall and before any Leap Clear.
  • The path: every soldier who holds On Body or Blind Spot relative to it. An airborne soldier swings clear with no roll and keeps their attachment. In Reach and Distant are outside the path, and so is a soldier Grabbed in another Titan's hand. A soldier this Titan holds when it becomes grounded stays Grabbed and is outside the path. A soldier its death frees from its hand is in the path, at On Body.
  • Leap Clear. Each soldier in the path who is not airborne, Down, or carried rolls Leap Clear. A success lands them on the Ground in its zone, In Reach. A failure pins them. A Down soldier in the path is Pinned with no roll. A carried soldier follows their carrier: both clear, or both are Pinned.
  • Record each pin on the Titan's row. Each Pinned soldier gains a Crush Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location, which cannot be lethal and takes no Net Success rider; that location names the pin (see Pinned).
The pinning Body Part
Injury Location rolledPinPinning Body PartIf that part is Broken
Left armlimb pinleft armright arm; none if both arms are Broken
Right armlimb pinright armleft arm; none if both arms are Broken
Left leglimb pinleft legright leg; none if both legs are Broken
Right leglimb pinright legleft leg; none if both legs are Broken
Torso or headbody pinnonenone
  • Corpse Heat. A soldier Pinned under a Corpse gains a Burn Critical Injury at the start of each of their turns, from the first turn that begins after the fall or the death: at the pinned limb and its side, or at the torso for a body pin. It can be lethal and takes worsening. Under a living grounded Titan there is no heat (see the Corpse). A pinned arm or leg lost to Corpse Heat leaves the soldier Pinned by the same Body Part, and the lost-limb grade applies at once.
  • Freeing. A Heave count that reaches the Heave rating frees everyone the body pins. The Heave count is cleared only when a living Titan stands, never at a fill or a death. A Heave against a Corpse costs the heaver 1 Burn damage. Breaking the pinning Body Part frees everyone it pins; on a Corpse that strike leaves no mark and creates no Opening, and a Corpse's Body Parts never regenerate. A living Titan that stands frees everyone it pins. The end of a Titan Engagement frees no one (see Heave and cutting free).

A Titan's death

When a Titan dies, in order

  1. Relief. Every soldier holding a Position loses 1 Stress.
  2. Freed. A soldier it holds is freed. If they were lifted, resolve their fall first. For steps 3 and 4 they hold On Body relative to the dying body and are not airborne; then they stand on the Ground in its zone.
  3. Steam. Every soldier at On Body or Blind Spot relative to it is scalded by Steam.
  4. The fall. The body comes down (above). If it was already grounded, it falls no second time: the soldiers it pins stay Pinned, now under its Corpse, and Corpse Heat begins.
  5. Removals. Remove its Openings, Next Behavior, Regeneration clock, marks, and remaining cards this round.
  6. Detach. Every attachment naming it ends, except Pinned, and the detach rule applies: Anchored if airborne, Ground if not, in the same zone.
  7. The Corpse. The body stays on the field as a Corpse in the zone it died in, and every Position relative to it is read from where everyone is. Its tracker row stays as its Corpse row, with its zone. Horses and left items stay in their zones.
  8. Ending. If no Focus Titan is alive and no soldier is Pinned, the Titan Engagement ends.

The Corpse lies in its zone and never moves. It counts as a grounded Titan for attachment steps. A Position relative to a Corpse is a Position in the Titan Engagement for every rule that reads one: the ending tests, witnesses, the care window, Stress relief, Grief, Heave, Corpse Heat, the retreat's forced moves, and returning; and its zone counts as a Focus Titan's zone for leaving and for the retreat. It holds no Attention, plays no cards, has no clock, and takes no mark. Heave and the strike on a pinning Body Part are the only acts made against it.

Example: a kill with a soldier On Body

A standard Medium Titan stands in zone 7, Wooded. Its right leg is Wounded with a count of 1. Private Mila Brandt holds Blind Spot, Private Jonas Keller holds On Body, and Private Kurt Adler holds In Reach. None is airborne. Mila strikes the Nape and reaches its Nape Depth of 4.

Relief and Steam. All three lose 1 Stress. Mila and Jonas are scalded: Mila's D6 on the steam table shows 5, and she takes 1 Burn damage. Jonas's shows 2, and he takes none. Kurt, at In Reach, rolls nothing.

The fall. The Titan was not grounded, so its body comes down, and zone 7 drops from Wooded to Sparse. Mila and Jonas each roll Leap Clear. Mila scores 1 success and lands on the Ground in zone 7. Jonas scores none: he is Pinned. His Injury Location roll gives his right leg, so he gains a Crush Critical Injury there that cannot be lethal, and the Titan's right leg pins him.

Removals, Positions, and the Corpse. All three are in zone 7, where the Corpse lies, and none but Jonas is attached to it, so all three hold In Reach relative to it. Its row reads A Corpse | zone 7 | Eyes I0 LA I0 RA I0 LL I0 RL W1 | Heave 0/3 | Pins: Jonas (right leg, RL). Jonas is Pinned, so the Titan Engagement does not end.

The next round. At the start of Jonas's turn he gains a Burn Critical Injury at his right leg. He holds In Reach and keeps his action, so he strikes the right leg that pins him, needing no working ODM Gear. His first success reaches its Toughness of 2: the leg is Broken, and he is freed. No Focus Titan is alive and no soldier is Pinned, so the Titan Engagement ends.

Frenzy at the table

Set each Focus Titan's Frenzy to 0 the moment it becomes one, a Background Titan that walks in included, and raise it by 1 at the Frenzy end step of every third round (the third, the sixth, the ninth), just after the Regeneration clocks, to a cap of 3. At the end of any other round, leave it alone. It is public. Say it when it rises, because it is the clock the players can do something about: it is the packet's promise that a fight they let run turns into one they cannot win.

Two Focus Titans hold their own Frenzy each. A Titan that enters in round 7 starts at 0 while the first is at 2. A Titan that walks in from a Background clock arrives at the step after Frenzy, so any rise that round has already happened and it stays at 0 through its whole first round, first rising at the end of the next third round; one a flare brings in mid-round rises with the rest at that round's end, when it is a third round.

Background Titans and the retreat

  • Background Titan clocks fill 1 segment at the end of each round, and 1 segment for each flare spent on Break Attention, once that Break Attention and any Hook and Cut it allows are resolved.
  • Entering. When a Background Titan's clock is full and fewer than two Focus Titans are alive, it becomes a Focus Titan: it takes the next unused label and starts clean. It enters at its entry zone: the edge zone that holds no soldier and is farthest from the nearest soldier, a tie going to the lowest-numbered; if every edge zone holds a soldier, the edge zone holding the fewest, then the lowest-numbered. Every soldier in another zone holds Distant relative to it, and soldiers who have left stay out. It checks its Attention (at the end step no cards choose; after a flare, that round's cards do). If it is now one of two Focus Titans, every soldier who holds a Position makes the Fear Roll for a second Focus Titan; if it is an Abnormal, they make the Fear Roll for an Abnormal; either way each soldier makes one Fear Roll at most for the entry. It is dealt cards from the next round, and it strides from its first card. When several clocks are full at once, resolve them in the order the tracker lists them.
  • The retreat begins when the retreat clock is full, or when a Background Titan's clock fills while two Focus Titans are alive; that Titan does not enter. Record the round it began on the Engagement line. From then on no Background Titan's clock fills, the retreat clock does not fill, and no Titan enters; each Focus Titan's Regeneration clock still fills. Each soldier's forced move comes before their action, except that staying with a comrade comes after the action taken for them, and a move on a turn whose action is Lift Comrade comes after the lift (see Retreats).
  • Forced moves and the dead. A Corpse's zone counts as a Focus Titan's zone for the retreat, and while no Focus Titan is alive a step out goes farthest from the nearest Corpse. A soldier may step toward, or stay with, a Pinned comrade as with a Down or Grabbed one; a Pinned comrade is in the pinning body's zone, and the staying actions are Heave against that body or a strike on the pinning Body Part.

The run-on while anyone is Pinned

  1. While no Focus Titan is alive and a soldier lies Pinned under a Corpse, the rounds go on. The Background Titans' clocks and the retreat clock fill as usual, and a Background Titan may enter.
  2. The Titan Engagement ends when the last Pinned soldier is freed or dies, unless no soldier standing ends it first.
  3. The stay limit. A retreat's moves toward and beside a comrade the limit covers are open only on its first rounds, as many as its retreat clock has segments, counted from the round after the round it began. With an 8-segment clock, a retreat that began in round 10 leaves them open through round 18. The limit covers a comrade Pinned under a Corpse in every retreat, even after a Background Titan has entered, and every comrade, Down, Grabbed, or Pinned, while no Focus Titan is alive.
  4. From the next round on, no soldier steps toward or stays with a comrade the limit covers. Each such move is the retreat's first or second option, or, for a soldier who can make neither, letting go or changing nothing.
  5. While a Focus Titan is alive, moves toward a Down or Grabbed comrade, or one Pinned under a living Titan, have no such limit.

The ending

Check both tests as soon as the event that could make one true is fully resolved, Fear Rolls and Drive decisions included (see Leaving and the end of the fight).

  • No Focus Titan is alive, and no soldier is Pinned: it ends at once. Background Titans that have not entered never do.
  • No soldier standing: no soldier who holds a Position, relative to a Focus Titan or a Corpse, is alive and not Down. This includes every soldier having left. A body-pinned soldier is not standing; a limb-pinned soldier who is not Down is.
  • Past the stay limit: in a retreat, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive, from the first round past the stay limit, a soldier whom a lost-limb grade forbids every kind of move (today, the both-legs grade), and who is not Down, Pinned, or carried, is not standing. The test reads the grade, not the turn. Check it as that round begins, before its wings step, and after every event from then on; no returner exists in a retreat, so once it is met the Titan Engagement ends at once. The soldier keeps their zone, their turns, and every action their Position allows, and a comrade in their zone may still lift them and carry them out.

When no soldier is standing, look for a returner: a soldier who has left, is alive, and is neither Down nor carried, while no retreat is under way. If no soldier holds a Position, or there is no returner, the fight ends at once. Otherwise record the round on the Engagement line; the fight ends at the end of the next round if still no one stands, or at once when the last returner is gone. Erase the record if a soldier stands again.

When it ends with no soldier standing:

  • While a Titan lives, every soldier still holding a Position, each one Down or, past the stay limit, unable to move, dies, left to the Titans.
  • Whether or not a Titan lives, every soldier still Pinned dies, left under the body.
  • These deaths have no witnesses and cause no Fear Roll, but they count for Grief, and their left items leave play.
  • With no Focus Titan alive, every other soldier, a soldier past the stay limit who cannot move included, goes through the end steps as after any kill. The end steps read the Positions soldiers hold relative to each Corpse as they stand at the end.

Running a Grab

A Grab lands on 1 or more Net Successes. Nothing in the Grab reads the Net Successes: its crush takes no rider.

  • Record the Grabbed soldier, the arm (the first unbroken arm in the stat block; its Toughness is 1 and its count 0 while it holds them), and the countdown. Then resolve the steps in When a Grab lands: the hold, the torso crush (a Crush Critical Injury that cannot be lethal), Attention, and the witnesses' Fear Rolls.
  • Count the soldier's own turns that come up after the Grab lands, including a turn spent in advance and a turn taken while Down. The Grab spends the action of the first counted turn, whether or not a dodge or anything else already spent it. At the end of the first, if they are still Grabbed, mark them lifted. At the end of the second, if they are still held, the Titan devours them, and every witness makes a Fear Roll.
  • While the Titan holds a living soldier, each of its cards resolves nothing, its Next Behavior waits, and the marks stay. Its Regeneration clock still fills.
  • If the Grabbed soldier dies by any rule, a Death Roll included, the Grab ends at once: the arm returns to normal, the dead soldier holds no Position and does not fall, nothing holds the Titan's Attention until its next card, which checks its ladder and can act again, and every witness makes one Fear Roll for the death.
  • A Grab on a Pinned soldier. A Grab cannot take hold of a Pinned soldier. When a Grab lands on one, from the Titan whose body pins them or any other, only the crush lands, as the Grab's Titan attack, with no rider. There is no hold, no change of Attention, no Fear Roll, and no countdown. The soldier stays Pinned.
  • A holding Titan's body. A soldier the Titan holds when it becomes grounded stays Grabbed and is outside the Falling Titan's path. A soldier its death frees holds On Body relative to the dying body for its Steam and its fall.

Round length Playtest rule

With the playtest configuration's 4 player characters (see Playtest configuration), a round against one Focus Titan should take 20 minutes at most, and a round against two Focus Titans 30 minutes at most. A round with a Grab may run longer by the witnesses' Fear Rolls.

Time your rounds and record them on the feedback page.

The Titans

"Small ones crawl, medium ones reach, large ones fall on you. All of them bite."Garrison sergeant, Wall Rose

Every page of this chapter is public: show it to any player who asks. A standard Titan takes every number from its Size Class and uses the standard Attention Ladder. Every one of them starts at Frenzy 0, gains 1 at the end of every third round, and caps at 3, and every behavior roll is D6 plus that Frenzy. Each strides toward its Attention holder before its behavior resolves: a standard Titan by its Size Class, an Abnormal by its own stat block. How to read a stat block and a Behavior Table explains every column, every effect, and the Titan Dice. Below each table, a line for each entry describes the behavior for narration; the row is the rule.

A Behavior Table kills only through a lethal Critical Injury, by its Death Roll or an instant-death row that the Net Success rider can reach; through the Grab's devour step; or through a fall one of its effects causes. A Titan's body also harms with Steam, its fall, and Corpse Heat, which no entry deals (see Titan harm, clocks, and the end). No entry affects a horse.

Attack Dice are Titan Dice, rolled by the GM in the open when a card resolves the entry: each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success, and Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice.

  • Gape. Its jaw drops open far wider than any face should and hangs there, as if the soldier were already in it.
  • Lurch Closer. It lurches at the soldier head first, however it can, and the way it leans shows what it will try next.
  • Clutch at the Legs. It drops low and snatches at the soldier's legs and wrenches at whatever it gets hold of.
  • Scrabble. It throws itself down and rolls in the dirt to scrape the soldier off its back.
  • Bite. It ducks its head low and sinks its teeth into the soldier's nearest leg.
  • Grab. It clutches the soldier in one small hand and drags them toward its mouth.
  • Thrash. It writhes and rolls in every direction at once.

Attack Dice are Titan Dice, rolled by the GM in the open when a card resolves the entry: each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success, and Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice.

  • Fixed Grin. It stops dead, its eyes wide and empty above a fixed grin, and the way its weight shifts shows what it will do next.
  • Snap Short. Its head swings down at the soldier, and its jaws snap shut on the air a moment too late.
  • Swat. It swings the back of its hand at the soldier, as a person swats at a fly.
  • Shake Off. It hunches and rolls its shoulders to shake off whatever clings there.
  • Bite. It lunges with its jaws wide and closes them on the soldier's body.
  • Grab. Its fingers close around the soldier and do not let go.
  • Thrash. It jerks and twists at everything near it, with no aim at all.

Attack Dice are Titan Dice, rolled by the GM in the open when a card resolves the entry: each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success, and Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice.

  • Loom. It rears up to its full height, and the way its bulk leans shows what it will do next.
  • Heavy Tread. It walks straight at the soldier, and every step shakes the ground under everyone beside them.
  • Crush. It slams its bulk down beside the soldier, standing or fallen, and pins the soldier's legs under it.
  • Shrug Off. It heaves its shoulders and scrapes its back and neck along whatever stands near it.
  • Bite. It stoops, turns its head to the soldier it has fixed on, and bites. It reaches a soldier clinging to its body without moving and folds almost double to reach one on the ground.
  • Grab. A hand bigger than the soldier closes around them and starts to lift.
  • Thrash. It heaves its whole body about, lashing at nothing in particular.

For the GM · public

The Sprinting Abnormal

A Medium Abnormal that runs. It appears when a rule names it, such as the Abnormal hazard rows, or on a 6 on the setup table's roll for a Medium Focus Titan. When it starts a Titan Engagement as a Focus Titan, or becomes one, every soldier holding a Position makes the Fear Roll for an Abnormal.

At the table, everyone sees what any Focus Titan shows: it is Medium, it is an Abnormal, it draws two cards a round and strides up to 3 zones on each of them, and its Body Parts' counts and states and its clock's filled segments are on the tracker. Its Behavior Table is public. Its Toughness, Nape Depth, Regeneration clock length, and Attention Ladder are hidden until a Read reveals each one (see Hidden values).

2VeerTerrorizeholderAnynone3Stress +1; TelegraphThrashno

4Pitch HeadlongControlholderOn Body, Blind Spotnone3Knock loose (fall: Crush)Thrashno

never rolledThrashThrashholderAnynone6Knock loose (fall: Crush)noneno

Attack Dice are Titan Dice, rolled by the GM in the open when a card resolves the entry: each die that shows 5 or 6 is a success, and Titan Dice never Push and never take Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, or Stress Dice.

  • Run Past. It runs past on long, pounding legs without slowing, and everyone beside the soldier feels the ground jump.
  • Veer. Its head lolls and its whole body tips one way, and the way it tips shows where it will go next.
  • Trample. It runs straight through the soldier, and its feet come down wherever they come down.
  • Pitch Headlong. It flings its whole body forward and rolls over, a slow, heaving roll that scrapes off whatever clings to it.
  • Grab. A hand shoots out at the soldier and closes around them.
  • Headlong Lunge. It closes the last of the gap in one long stride and snaps at the soldier with its whole head.
  • Thrash. It spins and rolls on the spot, twisting every way at once.
  • It runs through people. Run Past and Trample work against a holder at Distant or In Reach, so a rider in another zone is not out of its path. With a Stride of 3 it crosses most of the Standard field on one card before the entry resolves, and Run Past strikes only the holder and the soldiers at their Position in their zone. Trample's leg Critical Injury cannot be lethal; a rider it puts Down falls.
  • Its hands and jaws need you close. Its Grab and Headlong Lunge need In Reach or On Body, so it retargets onto anyone standing there when its Attention holder is not. The Lunge falls back to Pitch Headlong only when nobody at all is. Both roll 6 Attack Dice.
  • Cut one leg. Run Past, Trample, and Headlong Lunge need both legs. With one leg Broken it is grounded: it only veers, pitches over, and grabs, and Nape strikes against it gain the grounded dice.
  • Clinging to it is risky. Pitch Headlong knocks loose the holder at On Body or Blind Spot, though its slow roll rolls only 3 Attack Dice.
  • Only Run Past wrecks. Its pounding legs drop the rating of the zone it stands in. Pitch Headlong, a roll against the soldiers on its body, wrecks nothing.

GM only

Hidden values

Players do not read this page. The values listed under Hidden until Read are revealed only by a Read, and a Read that picks one reveals it to every soldier. The Sprinting Abnormal's Size Class, that it is an Abnormal, its Tempo, its Heave rating, and its Behavior Table with its Attack Dice are public.

Until a Read reveals its Regeneration clock length, the tracker shows only the clock's filled segments. Its public counts and state changes show its Toughness over time.

Sprinting Abnormal: Attention Ladder, highest first
RungA soldier meets it whenDown or carried soldiers
1. Hooked into its bodythey hold On Body relative to it, or are marked as having struck its Napecannot meet it
2. Loudest or brightestthey are marked as loudcannot meet it
3. Current holderthey hold its Attention and a Position other than Distantcan meet it
4. Nearestas the highest rung met, they hold the closest Position any candidate holds, in the order On Body, In Reach, Blind Spot, Distant; when it narrows a tie, they are the tied soldiers at the closest Position any of them holdscan meet it

As on every ladder, a soldier marked as having struck its Nape comes before a soldier who is only On Body, and the tie-breaks are the same: the current holder, then the lowest card (not before the deal or at an end step), then nothing.

  • A blade at its Nape or a soldier On Body turns it first, whoever else is making noise. A Nape striker who falls short draws its Attention, and its next behavior only when that behavior reaches Blind Spot.
  • Then noise. While no one is hooked into it, a soldier marked as loud outranks everyone below. Draw Attention, or a Fear Roll result that works like it, sets that mark only in its zone; a Flight or a mounted charge can set it from farther off (see What a Titan remembers).
  • Then the one it is already running down. Its holder keeps its Attention while at On Body, In Reach, or Blind Spot, until a Nape strike, a soldier On Body, or noise turns it. A comrade standing closer does not pull it away. A holder who gets away to another zone, mounted or on foot, keeps it only while they are the nearest, and it strides after whoever holds it.
  • Only then whoever is nearest. When nothing holds its Attention, or its holder is at Distant, it takes the soldiers at On Body, then In Reach, then Blind Spot, then Distant. A Squad that starts in one zone starts with nothing holding its Attention, so its first card turns to whoever has come closest, or to the lowest card when no one has moved.
  • It ignores reach and pain. It has no rung for soldiers in its reach or for soldiers who just hurt it, so the cutters at its legs draw it only when it is running no one down and they are the nearest.
  • Down soldiers meet current holder and nearest. A holder who goes Down anywhere but Distant stays its quarry until something above turns it.

Glossary

Every game term this packet uses, and every term those definitions use. Terms for rules Phase 1 does not include are gathered at the end.

Military

Survey Corps
The military branch that ventures beyond the Walls, and the branch the Squad serves in.
Garrison
The military branch that defends and maintains the Walls.
Military Police
The elite interior branch, open only to top graduates, and the commonest human Foe.
Training Corps
The three-year program every soldier completes before joining a branch.
Cadet
A member of the Training Corps who has not yet graduated.
Rank
A soldier's place in the chain of command: Private, Squad Leader, or Section Commander.
Funding
The Survey Corps' political and financial backing, rated 1 to 6, which sets what Requisitions and resupply can deliver.
Squad
The Survey Corps unit made up of the player characters and their Squadmates.
Squadmate
A named NPC soldier who rides into the field with the player characters, can be directed by any player, can be targeted by Titans like anyone else, never Pushes or Covers, and can be promoted to a player character.
Squad Pool
The Squadmates currently serving in the Squad.
Playtest configuration
The Squad the first playtest campaign runs: 4 player characters and no Squadmates. While it holds, Recruit brings no Squadmate and no one is promoted; Squadmates stay the rule for every other campaign. Playtest rule
Wing
A Squadmate's attachment to one player character during a Titan Engagement. The Squadmate acts right after that character.
Squad Tactic
A tactic owned by the Squad rather than any one soldier, usable once per Titan Engagement when its condition is met.

Character

Lifepath
The rolled character creation procedure that walks a Cadet through their origin and three years of Training Corps events. It is the only way to a key attribute of 5 or 6 or a Top 10 Class Rank.
Template Build
The character creation procedure that gives a soldier the attributes of their Specialty's Squadmate template (18 points, key attribute 4) and lets the player choose an Origin, a Drive, and the soldier's Talents. Used when the GM allows it for the campaign.
Free Build
The character creation procedure in which the player places one of two arrays of 18 attribute points (every attribute 2 to 4, the key attribute at 4, at most two attributes at 4) and chooses an Origin, a Drive, and the soldier's Talents. Used when the GM allows it for the campaign.
Graduation Exam
The optional played prologue of three Trials whose Merit replaces the third Training Year's performance roll and so helps decide each Cadet's Class Rank.
Trial
One of the three tests of the Graduation Exam: the ODM balance test, the Titan dummy course, and the squad field exercise.
Class Rank
A graduate's final standing in their class, or none for a built or promoted soldier. The Top 10 are offered a place in the Military Police.
Specialty
A character's trained specialization, chosen at Graduation or at a build's Specialty step, which grants bonuses but never exclusive permission to attempt anything: Slayer, Flier, Hunter, Tactician, Leader, Medic, Engineer, Rider, or Brawler.
Talent
A narrow trained ability that adds dice or bends a rule, but only for the actions it names from the Action Catalog.
Action Catalog
The complete list of named actions and rolls a soldier can take, which Talents reference by name and which a called roll is made for (the closest entry marked for called rolls, or an attribute alone). An improvised act in a fight uses one entry marked as an action as its model, never a Reaction, a roll, a fixed roll, or an option.
Wits
The attribute for planning, mechanics, and medicine.
Instinct
The attribute for gut calls, survival, reading people, and Reading Titans.
Haven
What a soldier has to return to, which lets them recover Stress and Grief during Downtime.
Origin
Where a Cadet was born and raised, the first stage of the Lifepath.
Training Year
One of the three Lifepath stages spent in the Training Corps, each with a training event and a performance roll. The Graduation Exam, if played, replaces the third year's performance roll.
Merit
What a Cadet earns from Training Year performance, which decides their Class Rank. A built or promoted soldier records none.
Retirement
A soldier leaving play to become an NPC instructor or Squad contact: at five Scars, or by choice after losing both arms or both legs. That choice is offered when the procedure in which the loss happened ends, and again at the end of every Downtime while the loss stands. A Prosthetic is the alternative.
Drive
What keeps a soldier fighting, written as a "When I ..." trigger, which lets them shrug off one Fear Roll result per session while that trigger is met by their own act, a Position they moved into, or their own ODM flight. Position triggers require a Titan Engagement; turn triggers work outside one only when a rule extends them, while Freedom's flight trigger can work anywhere.

Rolls and harm

Push
Re-rolling every die that did not show a 6, except a Gear Die showing 1, at the cost of 1 Stress, allowed only if no Stress Die showed a 1. A 1 on a Gear Die stays and wears that gear by 1 point.
Stress
A soldier's mounting fear and adrenaline, which makes them both more capable and more likely to break.
Stress Dice
Dice added to attribute rolls equal to current Stress, unless the roll explicitly excludes them, which can succeed like any die but can also set off a Stress Response.
Stress Response
The harmful reaction set off when a Stress Die shows a 1.
Resolve
A soldier's steadiness, half of Instinct plus Empathy, rounded up, plus 1 per Scar and minus 1 per point of Grief, which counts against Stress Response and Fear Roll results.
Gear Dice
Dice contributed by equipment. A Push rolls a Gear Die showing 2 to 5 again, keeps a 6, and locks a 1. When a Pushed roll is final, any 1 showing wears that equipment down by 1 point, one point per roll.
Help
Adding 1 die to a comrade's roll, up to 3 helpers. In a Titan Engagement it spends the helper's action and needs the same zone or an adjacent zone; in a Skirmish it spends the helper's action and needs a comrade taking part who is not Down; outside both, the rule that calls for the roll states who can Help and what it spends, and on a called roll the GM rules who is present and able, with Help spending nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when the Help is declared. Help on a called roll in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish is that fight's own Help, at no cost the GM names.
Bonus Dice
Dice from Help, Openings, and the other sources a rule names, such as Call It, a grounded Titan, the Ambush, or a plus step of Circumstances, which together can add at most 4 to a roll.
Called roll
A roll the GM calls for an act no rule covers, when its outcome is in doubt and failure would cost something. Before the pool is built the GM names its Catalog entry or attribute, its Circumstances, and its Stakes; it needs 1 success, can be Pushed, Covered, and Helped as the GM rules, and its result stands. In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish it spends the action and is made only for an act no entry marked as an action in that fight could model and that changes nothing the fight tracks; Help on it is then that fight's Help. A passive roll is not a called roll: it is a Spot or Size Up the GM makes out of sight for something the soldier does not know is there, outside a fight, with no stakes, no Push, no Help, and no Stress Response, and failing it means only that the soldier does not notice.
Circumstances
The one step of the seven-step ladder the GM names for a roll's situation: Effortless (+3), Routine (+2), Easy (+1), Standard (nothing), Hard, Harsh, or Desperate (a 1, 2, or 3 die penalty). Standard when the GM names none. Plus steps are Bonus Dice under the cap of 4; minus steps are penalties after the cap, base dice only, never below 1 base die, never Gear or Stress Dice. They change dice, never what a roll needs, and never touch Titan Dice or a Foe's dice. In a Titan Engagement the assumption is Standard, and the GM names a step only for something no rule already prices. Hard alone always names this step; the Pace is always written in full as Hard Ride, and the Talents Hard to Kill and Hard Rider keep their names. A step named for a lasting condition is the step of every roll it touches while it lasts, and a further condition is weighed into one step, never added. A Reaction takes the step in force when the attacker's card comes up.
Stakes
What the GM names before a called roll is made: what success gives (the act, information, a position, an object that is not a gear item, or found Squad Supply; never a gear item), and what failure costs from the fixed menu (it does not happen; 1 Stress; a fall, low or high and never extreme; damage of 1 to 3; a lost item or unit of Squad Supply; lost time or a worse position). A Critical Injury is never handed out as a cost, though damage that brings current Health to 0 inflicts one as all damage does, so the GM says the soldier's current Health with any stakes that name damage or a fall. Named before the dice, fixed once they roll.
Ruling
A call the GM makes where the rules are silent: whether to roll, which entry a called roll uses, its Circumstances and Stakes, whether a retry is possible, how a Foe with a reason acts, and how a scene is framed. A ruling acts before the dice and never changes a die, a row, a card, or a need. The rules the GM applies as written are under What stands.
Rally
Clearing a comrade's lasting Stress Response or pending Fear Roll result with an Empathy roll, but never their Stress. In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish it is an action; outside them it is not. No soldier can Rally themselves.
Covering
Taking on a comrade's Stress from their Push, so that the Push adds no new Stress Die.
Reaction
A dodge or block made when a Titan or a Foe acts against the soldier, outside the soldier's own turn, rolled knowing the attack's Severity. Each of its successes cancels one of the attack's, and the attack lands on 1 or more Net Successes. It spends the soldier's earliest turn, starting with this round's, whose move and action are both unspent. Against a Titan, one dodge covers all of that Titan's cards for the round, cancelling against each separately; a Titan cannot be blocked, and no Reaction is made against a card that whiffed. Against a Foe in a Skirmish, one Reaction covers that Foe's attacks for the round, and a Block answers a Fight attack.
Net Successes
The attack's successes left after the Reaction's successes cancel them one for one. The attack lands on 1 or more. A Critical Injury from a landed Titan attack adds 1 to its roll per Net Success beyond the first, and a person's damage adds 1 per Net Success beyond the first. Against a soldier who cannot react, every success of the attack is net.
Fear Roll
A roll forced by a horrifying event on the closed list of triggers, such as a Titan seizing a comrade, rather than by the dice. Outside a Titan Engagement the GM may call one for an event as horrifying as a listed trigger; inside one, only the list does.
Scar
A lasting named trauma with a mechanical effect, which also raises the soldier's minimum Stress and Resolve by 1.
Grief
The weight a soldier carries after a comrade dies, gained by the fight's participants when it ends, or by the Squad after other deaths and any Fear Rolls they cause. Each point lowers Resolve by 1, up to 3 held, until dealt with during Downtime.
Health
How much punishment a character can absorb before they can no longer act: 2 plus half of Strength plus Agility, rounded up, kept as a row of boxes, from 4 to 8. Each untreated Critical Injury crosses off one box, up to Health; damage marks the boxes left; at 0 the soldier is Down.
Critical Injury
A lasting injury with an Injury Location and an Injury Type, labeled with whether it puts the soldier Down, whether it is lethal and how fast, its effect, and its healing time. A Titan's attack inflicts one directly, without reducing Health first, adding 1 to the roll per Net Success beyond the first. Each untreated one crosses off a Health box, and a later injury to the same location and side is rolled with a worsening bonus.
Down
The state of a human at 0 current Health, whether from damage, from untreated Critical Injuries crossing off every Health box, or both, or holding an untreated Critical Injury whose row says so, who can do little more than crawl. Reaching 0 current Health through damage also inflicts an immediate Critical Injury.
Death Roll
The Strength roll, which cannot be Pushed, made each time a lethal Critical Injury's time limit runs out, to see whether the soldier dies.
Injury Location
Where a human's Critical Injury lands: left arm, right arm, left leg, right leg, torso, or head.
Injury Type
What kind of harm a Critical Injury or damage is: Crush, Bite, Burn, Cut, or Pierce. The rule that inflicts the harm names it, and it picks the Critical Injury's name and any rider the type carries. Burn comes from Steam, Corpse Heat, a firebrand, and a fired flare; it heals slowly, kills by the day, and needs a medical kit or medical supplies to treat. Cut comes from a blade and bleeds: its lethal rows kill by the turn until treated. Pierce comes from a shot and lodges: from the 7 row up, Treat Injury on it takes a 1-die penalty, and it does not heal until treated.

Titans

Titan
A mindless, regenerating humanoid, many times a person's height, that dies only when its Nape is destroyed.
Abnormal
A Titan whose behavior breaks the standard pattern and that uses its own Behavior Table.
Size Class
A Titan's height category: Small (3 to 5 m), Medium (6 to 10 m), or Large (11 to 15 m).
Nape
The only location where a strike can kill a Titan.
Nape Depth
The successes a single Nape strike needs to kill a Titan, set by Size Class, or by an Abnormal's own stat block. A strike that falls short turns its successes into Openings.
Body Part
A breakable location on a Focus Titan other than the Nape: its eyes, arms, and legs.
Body Part State
Intact, Wounded, or Broken. A Broken Body Part rules out the behaviors that use it until it regenerates.
Toughness
The successes, accumulated across strikes, needed to move a Body Part one state toward Broken.
Regeneration
A Titan's single regrowth clock, which ticks at the end of each round, its length set by Size Class, or by an Abnormal's own stat block. When it fills, the most damaged Body Part moves one state toward Intact and all Openings are erased.
Behavior Table
The table that decides what a Titan does. Titans act from it instead of taking ordinary turns, and each entry that harms or terrorizes lists the Attack Dice it rolls.
Attack Dice
The pool a Behavior Table entry or a Foe's weapon rolls when it resolves. A Titan's entry rolls 3, 6, 9, or 12 Titan Dice, set by its tier and Size Class; a Foe rolls its Attack Dice as base dice. An entry that only telegraphs rolls none.
Titan Dice
The dice a Titan's Attack Dice are rolled with, in the open when its card comes up. Each succeeds on 5 or 6. They never Push and take no Bonus Dice, Help, Gear Dice, Stress Dice, or Circumstances. Use a colour no soldier's pool uses.
Thrash
The default behavior every Behavior Table falls back to when no other entry can happen.
Frenzy
A number each Focus Titan holds, starting at 0, rising by 1 at the end of every third round (the third, the sixth, the ninth), and capped at 3. It is added to the Titan's behavior roll, and a total above the table's highest result reads as that highest result. Every Behavior Table is ordered from terrorize to kill, so a Titan grows more dangerous the longer the fight lasts.
Retargeting
What a Focus Titan does when its Attention holder is not in a Position the rolled behavior needs: the Attention Ladder is read again over only the soldiers who are, the Titan's Attention moves to the one it picks, and the behavior resolves against them. The entry falls back only when no soldier qualifies.
Next Behavior
A Focus Titan's upcoming Behavior Table result, rolled in advance and hidden until it happens or is Read. Reading it reveals its Attack Dice.
Telegraph
A behavior that announces what the Titan's next card will do.
Wreck
A Behavior Table effect that takes one rating step off the Anchor Rating of the Titan's own zone, whether the card landed or whiffed. A Titan's body falling wrecks its zone as well. A Stride never wrecks.
Stride
The zones a Focus Titan moves toward its Attention holder before each of its cards resolves a behavior: Small 1, Medium 2, Large 2, and as an Abnormal lists. A grounded Titan's is 0.
Grabbed
Gripped in a Titan's hand, unable to Help, Cover, or make a Reaction. The Titan lifts the soldier after their next turn and devours them after the turn after that, unless they get free.
Attention
The one soldier or decoy a Titan is fixed on at any moment, which its Behavior Table acts against; nothing holds it when no rung, its holder, or the cards pick one out, as at the start of a Titan Engagement. A soldier holding Attention cannot strike the Nape.
Attention Ladder
The ranked stimuli a Focus Titan turns its Attention toward each time it acts. The standard order is: hooked into its body, nearest person in reach, just hurt it (until the end of its next card that resolves a behavior), loudest or brightest, nearest (the closest Position). Abnormals have their own ladder.
Draw Attention
An action, or a Fear Roll result, that makes the soldier the loudest or brightest stimulus on the Attention Ladder until the end of the Titan's next card that resolves a behavior. Never from Distant.
Break Attention
An action that shifts a Focus Titan's Attention onto a decoy, such as a flare, a riderless horse, a thrown cloak, or the soldier's own Feint, for as many of its next cards as its Tempo. It is easiest for the soldier holding the Titan's Attention, and harder for each decoy the Titan has fallen for since one of its cards last resolved a behavior.
Feint
A decoy for Break Attention: the soldier's own pass across a Focus Titan's face from In Reach or On Body, made with working ODM Gear, mounted on their own horse that is not lame, or on foot against a grounded Titan. It spends nothing and needs 1 more success.
Severity
The successes an attack roll scored: a Titan's, a Foe's, or a soldier's against a Foe, announced to every target. A soldier's Reaction, or a Foe's Guard, must cancel every one of them to avoid the attack. A roll that scores 0 is a whiff against everyone.
Position
Where a soldier is relative to a Focus Titan or Corpse, read from their zone and attachment: Distant (another zone), In Reach (its zone), On Body, or Blind Spot. An action never changes one. Only the soldier's own move, and the closed list of rules that say so, do.
Blind Spot
The Position out of a Focus Titan's sight with its Nape within reach. It is not on the Titan: the soldier is anchored to terrain behind it, a tree or a roof, and is not touching it. It is the only Position a Nape strike can be made from, and a soldier who makes one stays there. Making a Nape strike counts as hooking into the Titan for the Attention Ladder only. It has no facing.
Opening
A token on a Titan created by successes beyond Breaking a Body Part, extra successes on Break Attention, every success of a Nape strike that falls short, or a rule that names it, such as Relentless. Any comrade of the soldier who created it can spend it as a Bonus Die on a Nape strike until Regeneration erases it; its creator never can.
Tempo
How many initiative cards a Titan draws each round.
Titan Engagement
A scene in which the Squad confronts one or more Titans.
Focus Titan
The Titan in a Titan Engagement that is tracked in full, with Body Parts, Attention, and a Behavior Table.
Background Titan
A Titan in a Titan Engagement that is run as a closing clock and enters as a second Focus Titan when the clock fills.
Steam
The scalding vapour a Titan's body gives off. At a Nape kill it burns every soldier On Body or at Blind Spot, and when a Regeneration fill recovers a Body Part it burns every soldier On Body. It is Burn damage, rolled on the steam table, and no Reaction answers it.
Falling Titan
A Focus Titan's body coming down when it dies or becomes grounded. Every soldier On Body or at Blind Spot who is not airborne must Leap Clear or be Pinned.
Leap Clear
The Agility roll, with ODM Gear or the horse, that a soldier in a Falling Titan's path makes to get out from under it. It needs 1 success, is not a Reaction, spends nothing, and can be Helped and Pushed.
Pinned
Pressed under a Titan's body, by one arm or leg or by the whole body. A Pinned soldier cannot move, Fly, Ride, or make a Reaction, is never lifted or carried, and holds In Reach relative to the body. It ends when the Heave count reaches the Heave rating of the Size Class, or of an Abnormal's own stat block; when the pinning Body Part (the Titan's arm or leg of the same kind and side as the soldier's pinned one) is Broken; or when a living Titan stands. Under a Corpse, the soldier takes Corpse Heat each turn. A soldier still Pinned when the Titan Engagement ends dies under the body.
Heave
The Strength action that raises a Titan's body off a Pinned comrade. Each success counts toward the Heave rating of the Size Class, or of an Abnormal's own stat block, and the count clears only when a living Titan stands. Heaving a Corpse costs the heaver 1 Burn damage.
Corpse
A dead Focus Titan's body, lying for the rest of the Titan Engagement in the zone its Titan died in as a grounded body that soldiers hold Positions relative to. A soldier on the field is in the Titan Engagement. Heave and the strike on a pinning Body Part are the only acts against it. It holds no Attention and plays no cards. The Titan Engagement does not end while a soldier lies Pinned under one.
Corpse Heat
The heat of an evaporating Corpse: a Burn Critical Injury at the pinned Injury Location at the start of each turn a soldier lies Pinned under it, and 1 Burn damage to each soldier who Heaves it.
Retreat clock
The clock every Titan Engagement carries, filled one segment at the end of each round after the Background Titans' clocks and never by a flare; when it fills, the Titan Engagement becomes a retreat. The rule that begins the Titan Engagement names its length, or the setup table gives 8.
Read
Observing a Titan during a Titan Engagement to learn facts about it, such as its Next Behavior, for the whole Squad.
Call It
Spending 2 of a Read's successes, one of them the success that revealed the Next Behavior, so that each soldier that behavior targets, other than the reader, gains 2 Bonus Dice on a dodge made against the card that resolves it. A dodge made earlier that only cancels against that card gains nothing.

Field

ODM Gear
The gas-powered grappling harness soldiers use to fly between anchor points and reach a Titan's Nape.
Field
The hexagonal zones a Titan Engagement is fought over: 7, 13, or 19, with 13 the default.
Zone
One hex of the field, about a Titan's reach across, with its own Anchor Rating, occupants, and effects.
Edge zone
A zone on the rim of the field, from which a soldier steps off field to leave.
Off field
Beyond the edge zones. A soldier there has left the Titan Engagement.
Attachment
Where a soldier is within their zone: on the Ground, Anchored, On Body or in the Blind Spot of a named body, Grabbed, or Pinned. With the zone it gives every Position.
Anchor Rating
How well a zone holds ODM Gear anchors: Open, Sparse, Wooded, Urban, or Giant Forest. It decides which attachment steps the zone allows, its anchors, what a Flight pays to enter it, and its Terrain Trait.
Field rating
The Anchor Rating a field is generated from, which the centre zone and the Squad's start zone take.
Anchors
What a zone's Anchor Rating gives it to hook into: Open 0, Sparse 1, Wooded 2, Urban 3, Giant Forest 3. A zone's anchors are the Momentum cap of every soldier in it.
Terrain Trait
The one printed line each Anchor Rating adds beyond its steps, anchors, and Carry cost, read in the zone it concerns, such as the Urban roof that keeps a soldier at Blind Spot from being dropped by a Jam.
Flight
Every ODM move made in a Titan Engagement: a Fly roll with the soldier's own ODM Gear that needs nothing, since its first step happens whatever the roll gives. Each success is 1 Momentum, each further step is a Carry paid in Momentum, at most 2, and a Flight that crosses a Titan's zone, or scores no successes, marks the soldier as loud.
Momentum
The public points a soldier gains from a Flight's successes, from 0 to the anchors of their zone, lost in full at the end of any round in which they made no ODM move. Spent 1 at a time on Carry, Bite, Brace, Quiet, or a clean line.
Jam
ODM Gear worn down to 0, which stops working and drops an airborne soldier.
Blade Set
A matched pair of ODM swords, the unit in which they are carried, counted, and lost.
Gas Rating
The gas left in a soldier's current canister, which drops as Gas Rolls turn up 1s.
Gas Roll
Two dice rolled each round a soldier uses ODM Gear, three after a Pushed roll that used ODM Gear. Each 1 lowers the Gas Rating by 1.
Standard Issue
The gear every soldier receives when they join the Squad and whenever a rule issues it, such as before each Expedition, scaled by Funding: ODM Gear, gas canisters, Blade Sets, a horse, and a Medic's medical kit or an Engineer's tool kit.
Requisition
Asking Command for gear beyond Standard Issue during Downtime, rolled against the item's Scarcity. Funding sets which Scarcity Command will consider; there are no prices.
Ledger
Command's patience within one Downtime: every Requisition Command grants raises the successes the Squad's next Requisition needs by 1.
Kept Item
An item received by Requisition, which Standard Issue never takes away.
Scarcity
How hard an item is to Requisition: Standard, Limited, or Rare.
Overloaded
Carrying more than Strength + 4 items, which makes every ODM move cost the soldier's action as well.
Prosthetic
A workshop-made arm or leg fitted at a Downtime to a lost side whose loss has healed, obtained by Requisition at Limited Scarcity. Each one lowers the lost-limb grade one step, never past the one-limb grade for two, and never removes the lost-limb rows' own penalties. It has no rating and no Gear Dice, counts as no item, and is never a weapon.
Squad Supply
The shared pool of rations, flares, medical supplies, and Shot the Squad draws on in the field.
Formation Post
The place the whole Squad rides in an Expedition formation, assigned by Command: Vanguard, Flank Scout, Signal Relay, Center, Supply Wagon, or Rear Guard. It changes only at a Waypoint.
Expedition
A Survey Corps operation beyond the Walls, travelled as a chain of Legs between Waypoints.
Leg
One stretch of an Expedition between two Waypoints. The Squad always reaches the next Waypoint, but failure makes the arrival costly.
Pace
How hard Command drives a Leg: Steady, or a Hard Ride, a third Leg squeezed in before dark at a higher cost.
Distance Band
How far an Expedition has pushed from the Walls: Near, Far, or Deep. Deeper bands make Leg hazards worse.
Waypoint
A named stopping point on an Expedition route, such as a forest, an abandoned town, or a supply depot. Its kind sets the field rating of a Titan Engagement on the Leg that leads to it.
Depot
A Waypoint that gives a full Standard Issue and a Squad Supply restock on arrival.
Lead
The soldier the players choose to make a Leg roll or the camp roll.
Night Camp
The stop after a day's Legs, at which the camp roll is made, the Night table is read, and a day passes.
Camp Relief
The 1 Stress every soldier on the Expedition loses when the camp roll succeeds and the camp did not meet hunger.

Skirmish

Skirmish
A scene in which soldiers fight people, such as Military Police, bandits, or a Garrison picket. People roll their attacks, can be blocked, can be talked down, and hurt with damage that grows by 1 per Net Success beyond the first.
Foe
A person or group of people the Squad fights in a Skirmish, run by the foe rule unless the GM directs one with a reason said aloud. Its Attack Dice and Guard are rolled in the open and never adjusted. At 0 Health a Foe is out of the Skirmish: dead by a blade, a shot, or fire, and out cold by a blow.
Engaged
Within arm's reach of a Foe in a Skirmish.
Apart
Not within arm's reach of a Foe in a Skirmish.
Held
Grappled by a person in a Skirmish: unable to move or break away, and able to attack only the holder, bare-handed. No playtest Foe holds a soldier; in the playtest only a soldier holds a Foe.
Grit
How many of a Foe group's members must be out of the Skirmish before the group breaks and leaves: the latest point at which it breaks, since the GM may break it sooner with a reason. Each Foe kind's Parley value, not its Grit, sets what a Parley needs.
Watch
The successes a Sneak roll needs to give the Squad the Ambush against a Foe group.
Ambush
The side that acts first in round 1 of a Skirmish. Its attacks on a target that has not yet acted gain 2 dice (2 Bonus Dice for a soldier, 2 more Attack Dice for a Foe) and cannot be answered by a Reaction or by Guard.
Guard
A Foe's cancelling roll in a Skirmish: its Guard dice, rolled in the open as base dice against every attack on it, each success cancelling one of the attack's successes. It is never Pushed or Helped, spends nothing, and the Ambush denies it.
Parley
A soldier's once-per-Skirmish Persuade roll (or Strength roll, to threaten) that asks a Foe group to stand down, let the Squad pass, answer a question, or surrender. It needs the group's Parley value (plus 1 in a Skirmish, minus 1 per Foe out, never below 1) plus the ask. Talking to anyone who is not a Foe group is a called roll.
Shot
The Squad Supply kind a firearm's Reload spends, one unit per Reload.

Campaign

Downtime
The time the Squad spends inside the Walls between Expeditions. It lasts seven days. Playtest rule
Downtime Action
What each player character does during Downtime, one per Downtime: Recover, Visit Haven, Requisition, or Maintain Gear. Squadmates take none.
Squad Action
The one shared action the Squad takes each Downtime: Honoring the Fallen, which eases everyone's Grief, or Recruit, which brings new Squadmates into the Squad Pool and is not offered while the playtest configuration holds.
Mission Brief
The GM's record of an assigned mission: objective, route, Distance Band, expected Titans, one complication, and rewards. Its route keeps within the bounds the Expedition rules give: 4 to 6 Legs, no Leg deeper than the interim route gives its place, and at most one Depot, never the gate. The hazard roll reads it as written.
Campaign Year
The year the campaign has reached, from 845 to 850, recorded before the Lifepath and advanced only by the Canon Clock rules. Origin conditions are tested against it.
Canon Tie
A character's optional personal link to one canon character, drawn from their Lifepath.

Terms for later rules

Shifter
A human who holds one of the Nine Titans and can transform at will. Always GM-controlled: either a threat, or an allied asset inside an Operation Frame.
Operation Frame
A reusable template for a set piece, made of an objective clock, a threat clock, special actions, and outcomes, such as Wall Defense or a Capture Operation.
Chase
A pursuit between riders and a Titan, resolved across Chase Bands.
Chase Band
The gap in a Chase: Grabbing Distance, Close, Far, or Escaped.
Discovery
A permanent advance in what humanity knows about Titans, with a cost, possible prerequisites, and a lasting effect. Some stay locked until the Canon Clock reaches them.
XP
A soldier's personal advancement points, earned from end-of-session questions and spent on Talents.
Commendations
The track of a soldier's standing with command, raised by objectives, following orders, and bringing soldiers home, lowered by disobedience, and used to trigger promotion.
Faction
One of the powers inside the Walls the Squad deals with: Survey Corps Command, Garrison, Military Police, Church of the Walls, Nobility, or Underground.
Faction Standing
The Squad's relationship with one Faction, from −3 to +3, added to the dice for dealings with that Faction.
HQ Upgrade
An improvement to the Squad's headquarters, such as stables, a workshop, an infirmary, a training yard, or a research lab, that improves a Downtime Action.
Canon Clock
The dated canon events that happen on schedule unless the player characters interfere.

Sheets

Copy these pages for play: a character sheet for each player character, one Squad sheet, and one tracker for the GM. Write in pencil. How to fill each field is in Your character sheet, The gear sheet and the Squad sheet row, and, for the GM, The tracker. The Expedition, Downtime, and Skirmish records follow Expeditions, Downtime, Requisition, and Skirmishes.

When a Focus Titan dies, mark its column Corpse. Its zone never changes again. Its Body Part states and counts, its Heave count, and its pins stay for the rest of the Titan Engagement, and its Body Parts never regenerate.

Playtest feedback

Fill in one page per session, and the Expedition tally once per Expedition. Write down what happened at the table, not what you think the rule should be. If a rule was unclear, write the question you asked and how the table read it.

Timed rounds

With players new to the rules and the playtest configuration's 4 player characters, time three rounds: one against one Medium Titan, one with a Grab, and one against two Focus Titans. Write how many player characters took part in each.
